> Conventions: "UNVERIFIED" = not checked against a primary source or not measured on real data. "[rule: tbv]" = an exchange / regulator rule that must be verified against the official text (audit items RV1–RV13, §2.4). "PLACEHOLDER" = a value proposed here that the user must confirm before any outcome is looked at (collected in §17). "ESTIMATE" = computed from formulas and assumed sample sizes, not from data. "Pending user confirmation at Gate 2" = a design proposal of the lead / researcher that the user may veto. **This plan makes no statement about what any result will be.**
> **Standing qualifier (T1).** The backtest has daily bars only. All fills in it are **retrospective synthetic fills under rule SF** (§3.1–3.2; I-§2.4) with the artificial filtration F_t^syn. Every monitoring quantity and every statement citing Theorem 1 or its corollaries on market data refers to **the synthetic fill stream, not to an executable strategy**, and must be worded so.

## 1. Scope and non-goals

**Research questions (quoted from `research_question.md` v4, which governs; all in a retrospective synthetic-fill backtest on A-share daily data, long-only, T+1, with costs).**
- **RQ-1 (pre-specified test).** Does a trade / abstain gate placed on a multi-factor base signal add economically non-negligible stock-selection value **within the same nominated batch**, relative to a stratified random choice at matched order opportunities?
- **RQ-2 (pre-specified test).** Does adding a feature group to the gate change that within-batch selection value? Two groups, tested separately and never merged in a confirmatory contrast:
   - **Z_H — holder-state variables**: capital-gains overhang, chip-distribution statistics, abnormal turnover (§5.1–5.3).
   - **Z_O — observable actions of other identifiable participants** (name fixed by v4; free data only — user decision of 2026-11-15, `STATUS.md`): Dragon–Tiger list disclosures, block trades, executed insider / large-shareholder share changes (§5.6). **What the features are:** stock-day aggregates of public disclosure events (indicator, days since the last event, scaled amounts); no participant identity, seat or name enters any feature, and the group is always used together with the listing-trigger control set R_O (§5.4).
- **RQ-3 (descriptive; no confirmatory claim).** How does the additive P&L difference between a gated strategy and the ungated base portfolio split into exposure-schedule, composition and cost terms; what are terminal wealth and drawdown at deployment level?

**What each part of the question is answered by (table of v4, with section references added).**

| Clause of the question | Estimand | Evidential status |
|---|---|---|
| Within-batch selection value (gate versus stratified random choice from the same nominated list) | θ_sel: net return per order in excess of the exact expectation of a stratified random choice from the same list (§8.3) | **Pre-specified** (cards C1, C2: equivalence tests **or** pre-declared estimation targets — which of the two is fixed by the rule of §10.1 before any development outcome is seen) |
| Increment of Z_H in the gate | ψ_H: difference of θ_sel between the gate with and without Z_H (§8.4) | **Confirmatory** (card C3: difference test) |
| Increment of Z_O in the gate | ψ_O: the same for Z_O | **Confirmatory** (card C4: difference test; leaves the family if no Z_O class passes the data audit, §2.4) |
| Contribution of the exposure schedule / timing | exposure-schedule term of Lemma 3, two-interval additive attribution (§8.2) | Descriptive |
| Contribution of costs | cost-difference term of Lemma 3 | Descriptive |
| Total value after deployment | AR, terminal wealth, CAGR, drawdown (§8.1, §9.1) | Descriptive |
| Monitoring interval for the loss probability of executed entries | time-uniform interval for r_T^entry (Theorem 1; synthetic fill stream) | Descriptive: shows how much evidence the trade stream can carry; never used to compare methods |

Multiplicity: the pre-specified tests form one Holm family (size m between 1 and 4, §8.4). Everything else is exploratory or descriptive and may not be worded as confirmed.

**Hypotheses (v4; one per card).**
- **H-A1 (C1):** θ_sel of the calibrated-confidence gate CONF, **as implemented and tuned in this plan** (§6.1, §6.3), lies within the pre-set equivalence bound.
- **H-A2 (C2):** the same for the generic loss-prediction gate without Z, LOSS(−Z), as implemented here.
- **H-ZH (C3):** adding Z_H to the gate changes θ_sel (two-sided).
- **H-ZO (C4):** adding Z_O to the gate changes θ_sel (two-sided).
- **Not hypotheses.** (i) The v3 sentence "most of the apparent improvement is reproduced by exposure- and cost-aware comparators" is **withdrawn as a hypothesis** (v4): no test in this plan concerns the exposure or cost terms, which are reported descriptively. A verdict on C1 / C2 supports a statement about within-batch selection of that gate and nothing about "where the improvement comes from". (ii) H-A1 / H-A2 are statements about two in-house implementations in one pipeline, not about "existing abstention methods" as a class; the matured-performance gate PERF and the trend filter have no within-batch statistic and are assessed descriptively only (§9.3); published selective-prediction methods are an optional second round (§6.2). (iii) **Monitoring is not a hypothesis** (v4: "H-T 不是待检验的假设"): Theorem 1 / Proposition 2 (statuses: header) supply an interval and a statement of the limits of evidence. (iv) **H-M (mechanism side-track): on hold**; nothing is scheduled.

**Positioning (M-S7).** This file contains no positioning against prior work and none was written for this revision: it would have to cite papers that `literature/` records as read at abstract level only. Positioning is in `literature/positioning.md` and `literature/novelty_report.md` (not re-read for this revision) and must be written into the paper by the writer, with verified citations, before the framing is frozen.

**Monitoring (descriptive).** Behaviour of the delayed-feedback interval of Theorem 1 on simulated streams with known truth (§12.1) and, descriptively, on the **synthetic fill stream** of the daily-bar backtest (rule SF, filtration F_t^syn; §3.1–3.2). On market data the interval's target is r_T^entry of that synthetic stream; it is **not** a statement about an executable strategy (I-§2.4), and every sentence about it carries the words "under the synthetic execution model". **The interval is not used to compare methods** (§9.4: estimated half-width about 0.17–0.22 at the end of the hold-out).

**Contributions and how they depend on the outcome:** §15.3.

**Non-goals.**
- Not a search for a profitable strategy or a strong factor set.
- **No claim that Z_H or Z_O measures anybody's private information.** Z_O records publicly disclosed actions; the plan does not model a game, does not solve for an equilibrium and **claims no game-theoretic contribution**. Poker is motivation only.
- No claim of "first abstention approach to stock trading"; theory statements are about monitoring, never about "not losing".
- Maximum drawdown and portfolio CVaR are empirical metrics only.
- Reinforcement learning is an optional round-2 baseline.
- No intraday or order-book data, no shorting, no leverage, no hedging, no market-impact model beyond proportional slippage, no live trading. **Default: no timestamped opening-auction data are obtained (they are not free); the user may decide otherwise (§17 U35).** Consequently no claim is made that any backtested order would have filled in the market.
- **Out of scope for this paper** (possible future extension if licensed data become available): restricted-share unlock schedules and pre-disclosed selling / buying plans (§5.6.4).

## 2. Data plan and audit checklist

Nothing in this section has been measured except what `experiments/data_feasibility_opponent_actions.md` reports (40 probe calls, 32 succeeded; values not cross-checked against any second source). **No modelling starts before the audit report is written and the lead has reviewed it.**

### 2.1 Sources (rewritten v0.6, A-1; **bar source BaoStock — confirmed by the user on 2026-11-15, §17.1 A, U53**)

**The user's earlier instruction (2026-11-15):** no Tushare; AKShare, because it needs no token; BaoStock, also registration-free, kept as a cross-check "unless the user says otherwise".

**What the smoke test found** (AUDIT_REPORT §0, §3.1, §5, §6). AKShare's Eastmoney bar interface (`stock_zh_a_hist_em`) succeeded on 1 of 9 attempts; the others were rate-limit disconnects, still occurring about three hours later (whether this is specific to this machine's IP is unverified). AKShare offers no `preclose`, no rows on suspended days, no ST history and no historical index constituents. BaoStock returned a complete daily history for 33 of 33 sampled delisted names (0 missing trading days); AKShare's Sina interface covered the same 33 names through their last traded day.

**Division of sources.** It changes how the instruction "AKShare" is carried out for bars; **the user agreed to it on 2026-11-15** (relayed by the lead). Both sources are free and need no token or registration; Tushare is still not used.

| Data | Primary | Cross-check | Notes from the smoke test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unadjusted daily bars (open, high, low, close, volume, amount), `preclose`, trading-status flag, ST flag, turnover | **BaoStock** | AKShare–Sina unadjusted bars (prices, volume, amount only) for every name that is ever in the universe; Tencent bars on a sample | BaoStock versus Sina on 113,449 stock-days: open / high / low within 1e-4 on 99.9991%, close on 99.981%, volume and amount on 100%. **This agreement is not independent validation:** BaoStock's upstream is undocumented (A17: undetermined). The 23 close-price differences are all SSE names between 2015 and 2018-08-17 (see RC-8; the explanation is a guess of the audit) |
| Corporate events (cash dividends, bonus shares, capitalisations, rights issues) | Merged event table of §3.11 (AKShare: Eastmoney and Sina dividend tables; BaoStock dividend table; backstop from `preclose`) | Announcements on the statutory disclosure platform, by hand, for unconfirmed events | The Eastmoney table alone is not usable: empty for 12 of 12 delisted names, and it misses special dividends |
| Adjustment factors | **None is used in labels or accounting.** Features use a factor built by us from `preclose` (§3.11) | Vendor factors are reported in the audit only | BaoStock and Sina factors include cash dividends; BaoStock's lack 11 events of a code-change name and have 5 anomalous rows (A5, A23: failed) |
| Index constituents | BaoStock snapshots, **for descriptive strata only** (§2.3) | None: AKShare returns current constituents only | Counts exact on 41 of 41 queries; irregular snapshots; two suspected late updates; no official list to compare with |
| Z_O (LHB, BLK, INS; §5.6) | AKShare (Eastmoney data-centre interfaces; 63 calls, 0 failures) | Exchange websites and the statutory disclosure platform (OA10); Sina's Dragon–Tiger table; 10jqka for executed share changes | v0.5 said there was no second free source for Dragon–Tiger data; there is one (Sina), and the exchanges' own daily disclosures are retrievable by date (AUDIT_REPORT §3.4, §6 item 10) |
| Not used | AKShare's Eastmoney bars (push2 / push2his); the Eastmoney suspension table `stock_tfp_em` (incomplete: 147 A-shares suspended on 2015-07-08 against 1,317 in BaoStock; no records before 2012-02-22) | — | — |

**Fallback that was prepared in case the user declined (U53-b: AKShare–Sina unadjusted bars as the primary source) — not in force; kept as the record of what was considered and of the known limits of the Sina feed, which remains the cross-check.** Known limitations, all from the smoke test: (i) no `preclose`: reference prices would have to be recomputed from event records, which `rule_verification.md` (A5) advises against because issuers may apply for a different formula, and which the smoke test found unreliable (the Eastmoney cash amount and the exchange's reference price disagree by more than half a cent on 29 of 467 events); limit prices and condition (S2) would rest on reconstructed values; (ii) no row and no status flag on suspended days: suspension would be inferred from a missing row; (iii) no ST history: SZSE's dated name-change table could supply ST dates for Shenzhen names, no dated source was found for Shanghai names, so the ST filter could not be applied point-in-time to SSE names; (iv) no share-capital series for delisted names, hence no turnover and no Z_H features for them — a survivorship pattern in the features; (v) one duplicated row was seen (one delisted name, a row dated 2021-06-27); (vi) AKShare's source code warns that bulk Sina pulls can get the IP blocked: 42 calls were not blocked, a pull of thousands of names is untested; (vii) no historical index membership either. Under U53-b the conventions of §3.4, §3.5 and §3.11 that read `preclose`, the status flag or the ST flag cannot be implemented as written; the plan would need another revision, and probably fallback F2 or F3, before anything can be frozen. Taking Sina prices but `preclose`, status and ST from BaoStock is the same reliance on BaoStock and is not offered as a separate option.

**Other sources.**
- **`hf-dataset:ellendan/a-share-prices`** (approved; MIT; **not downloaded**; its card states neither source nor adjustment nor coverage of delisted names): the only candidate for an external reference of limit prices (A7). Whether to download it is a lead decision (§16.2 item 12). Without it A7 is an internal-consistency check and the paper says so.
- **Tushare-derived datasets are not dependencies** (unchanged): `hf-dataset:joshuaxql/qlib_data` and the release asset of `github:chenditc/investment_data` remain "optional cross-check, only if the user separately confirms" (U32).
- Installed in `experiments/.venv` by the smoke test: `baostock` 0.9.4, `akshare` 1.20.63 (the latter earlier, by the feasibility probe).
- All raw pulls are stored as local Parquet / raw JSON with pull timestamp and library version; raw files are never modified.

### 2.2 Period (proposal, to be confirmed after the audit)
- Raw data from 2008-01-01 (warm-up for 250-day windows and chip distributions).
- Evaluation period 2010-01-01 to **2026-06-30**. The end date is chosen so that the sample ends before the main-board ST price-limit change of 2026-07-06 (§3.4) and is fixed before any result is seen. PLACEHOLDER: the user may prefer a different start (e.g. 2012) if the audit shows early-year data are poor.
- Development segment: 2010-01-01 to 2021-12-31. Final frozen hold-out: 2022-01-01 to 2026-06-30 (§4.4).
- **Notes added in v0.6.** (i) No trade is simulated before 2010-01-01, so the rows of the rule calendar (§3.10) that end before that date matter only for warm-up features. (ii) The last data day, 2026-06-30, precedes 2026-07-06, the day on which the 2026 revisions of the SSE / SZSE Trading Rules take effect (among them the main-board ST limit of 10%); the rule versions in force at the end of the sample are the 2023 revisions. Decision days of the cards end H + 10 trading days before the last data day (U50), so no simulated order is placed after 2026-06-30. (iii) The upstream tables of all three Z_O classes have records from 2005 (yearly counts in AUDIT_REPORT §3.3); the start criterion of OA3 is met on that evidence, the "no run of 20 trading days without records" check is still to be done.

### 2.3 Universe (rewritten v0.6, A-6; **confirmatory population pending user decision after the pilot, v0.8 U-PILOT; pilot screen §13A**)

**(v0.8, U-PILOT — user decision 2026-11-15, relayed by the lead.)** The population of the confirmatory study is **not decided** ("pending user decision after the pilot"). Two candidates are kept, neither is adopted: **(A) U-LIQ** as written below (whole market; each month the 800 most traded eligible A-shares); **(B) the stratified random sample of 1,000 A-shares** in `experiments/data/universe_sample_v1.csv` (drawn with `experiments/src/data_fetch/draw_sample.py`, seed 20261115, 30 strata board × listing-year bucket × delisted; kept on disk, not used in the pilot round), with a point-in-time liquidity screen inside it whose size would be fixed when (B) is chosen (one option, not decided: 800 × 1,000 / 5,478 ≈ 146 names, i.e. U-LIQ scaled by the sampling fraction). U54 (below) is therefore no longer a pending confirmation of U-LIQ alone; it is replaced by the choice between (A) and (B), which the user makes after the pilot. Everything that has to be frozen with the population (Freeze-1, the event table, the OA10 sampling frame, `design_power`) waits for that choice (§18). **The pilot round runs on a different list: §13A.**

*The text below is v0.6; it describes candidate (A).*

**Why v0.5's definition is withdrawn.** v0.5 used the point-in-time union of CSI 300 and CSI 500 constituents. The smoke test found that the free data cannot deliver membership at that precision: AKShare returns current constituents only; BaoStock returns irregular snapshots — the June 2021 rebalance does not appear in queries of 06-15, 06-21 and 06-28, and the snapshot date stays at 2022-08-01 in four queries from 2022-08-08 to 2023-01-03. Only late updates were seen, none early, but no official list of changes was available, so the signed criterion SD-A2 is **undetermined** and cannot be evaluated without work outside the free data sources (the effective dates quoted here are the audit author's background knowledge, unverified). A candidate set that depends on unvalidated membership dates would put an unaudited input under all four cards.

**Primary universe U-LIQ (proposal): a point-in-time liquidity screen of the whole market. Index membership is not an input of any decision.**
- *Eligible on a screen date s:* A-shares of the SSE and SZSE main boards, ChiNext and the STAR Market (no B-shares, no Beijing Stock Exchange); at least 120 market trading days since listing; traded on at least 80 of the 120 market trading days ending s; ST flag 0 on s. (120 and 80 are PLACEHOLDERS, U54.)
- *Ranking:* median daily traded amount (unadjusted `amount`, in yuan; a day without trading counts as 0) over the 120 market trading days ending s. The **N_U = 800** highest-ranked eligible names are members (PLACEHOLDER; 800 is the nominal size of CSI 300 plus CSI 500; sensitivity 500 / 1,000).
- *Screen dates and validity:* s = the last market trading day of each calendar month; the membership is used for all decision days of the following calendar month. Every input is dated ≤ s ≤ t−1, so membership is a function of G_t (A11 applies).
- *Candidate set C_t* = members ∩ {listed at least 60 trading days (U11)} ∩ {not suspended on t−1 (§3.5)} ∩ {ST flag 0 on every one of the 60 market trading days ending t−1 (§3.5, U55; v0.5 used the flag of t−1 only)}. Sensitivity as before: ST names included.
- *Edge cases (v0.8, H-10; the implementer's reading, confirmed; code `experiments/src/engine/universe.py`).* A member without a row on t−1 is not a candidate on t, and is counted. A market trading day on which a name has no row does not count as an ST day in the 60-day look-back. Ties in the ranking are broken by code in ascending order. Days since listing are counted from the listing date on the full market trading calendar (from 2005), not from the first row of the pull.
- ST / *ST names stay in the data and may be held when they become ST. A name that leaves the universe while held is held to its exit under LP.
- Delisted names are in the screen for every date on which they qualified; this requires bars for every A-share that was listed at any time since 2008, delisted ones included (smoke test: 33 of 33 sampled delisted names complete in BaoStock; the full exchange delisting lists are the reference of the formal A1).

**What this changes for the conclusions (to be stated in the paper).**
1. The universe is "the 800 most actively traded eligible A-shares, re-drawn monthly", not an index universe. Results may not be described as results for CSI 300 or CSI 500 stocks, and are not comparable with published index-universe backtests without that caveat. The screen tilts toward names with high recent trading activity, which can include small speculative names; the composition by board, size and vendor-reported index membership is reported by year.
2. Selection on trailing traded amount is selection on public past information. It creates no look-ahead; it does make the universe depend on past volume, the quantity from which abnormal turnover (Z_H) and the Dragon–Tiger listing triggers (R_O) are computed. This is the same for every strategy and comparator.
3. **Index membership appears only in descriptive tables**, as "vendor-reported membership (BaoStock snapshot) as of 20 market trading days before the decision day" — the lag that v0.5 prescribed for a failed SD-A2. Such tables carry the note "snapshot dates irregular; not validated against the index provider's announcements". No card, gate, filter or stratum of §8.3 uses membership.
4. The scope clause of every verdict sentence (§9.3) names this universe.
5. Cost: bars are needed for the whole market, not only for index ever-members (§2.5).

**Fallback U-IDX, if the user prefers an index universe:** BaoStock-reported membership of CSI 300 ∪ CSI 500, used with the 20-trading-day lag from the start, described in the paper as "vendor-reported, not validated". Late snapshots are not look-ahead, but SD-A2 stays undetermined unless the index provider's rebalance announcements are collected by hand (the audit report estimates about 33 rebalances for each of the two indices) — an option the user or lead may add; it does not fit the free, scripted data sources. The fetch budget of U-IDX is the one the audit report extrapolated (§2.5).

**Warm-up of feature windows (P12; unchanged in substance).** Primary windows (250 days) need raw data from 2008 for features starting about 2009. The 500-day CGO variant needs about two years and the 260-week variant about five years of history; with raw data from 2008 they are complete only from about 2010 and 2013. Rule: a feature is NaN until its full window is available; no partial-window values. Whether BaoStock history before 2008 is usable was not tested; the raw pull starts at 2008-01-01 unless that is established. **(v0.7, F-17; measured on the trial names that have data from 2008-01-02, feature report §3.3.)** First decision day with a value: 250-day features 2009-01-09 to 2009-01-12 (the primary CGO of v0.7 needs one row more); chip features 2008-11-12; the 500-day CGO variant 2010-01-18, i.e. after the first evaluation day 2010-01-04; the weekly variant not before 2013-02 (later with the one-week lag of v0.7). The evaluation period is not moved for a variant. A robustness run that replaces a primary feature by a variant starts on the variant's first complete date and is compared with the primary run on the same span.

Every item is repeated on the full panel (order: §18). The tables below this block are the v0.5 checklist, kept as the specification; the amendments of v0.6 follow the status table.

**Amendments of v0.6 to the checklist.**
- **A1:** "primary" now means the bar source of §2.1 (BaoStock; confirmed, U53).
- **A2 and the fallbacks.** The v0.5 fallback "use constituent intervals from the cross-check source" and fallback F1 for constituents do not exist: AKShare has no historical constituents. See §2.3.
- **A5 / A23:** closed as failed; share-and-cash tracking is the only method (§3.11). The A5 fallback "rebuild factors from dividend records" is replaced by the `preclose`-implied factor of §3.11, used for features only.
- **A8:** the reference "AKShare suspension records" is dropped; the check is the status flag against volume.
- **A16:** every rights issue of a name that is ever in the universe is hand-checked (the Sina rights table is a single source); cash amounts follow the acceptance rule of §3.11.
- **A21:** the object checked is the merged event table of §3.11; the reference is the `preclose` backstop plus hand resolution against announcements. The two unexplained `preclose` rows and the unexplained +27.9% day of 2009-06-10 found by the smoke test are resolved by hand under this item.
- **A25 (new; `rule_verification.md` C2).** Block trades are added to a stock's total volume after the block-trade session (RC-19). Whether the daily `volume` and `amount` of the bar source include them is unknown. Check: on days with block trades (BLK table), compare the bar's volume with a second source and with volume minus block volume. Descriptive; the capacity cap of (S3) is then described as including or excluding block trades, and if they are included the 20-day average used by the cap is recomputed net of the BLK amounts.
- **File names.** The smoke-test report is `experiments/results/data_audit/AUDIT_REPORT.md`. The formal audit report, written after the full pull, is `experiments/results/data_audit/AUDIT.md`.

| # | Audit item | How it is checked | Pass criterion (PLACEHOLDER thresholds) | If it fails |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Delisted stocks are retrievable (first task)** | Take a hand-compiled list of >= 30 A-share codes known to have been delisted between 2010 and 2025 (from exchange announcements; list stored with sources). Query each in BaoStock and AKShare. | >= 95% of the list return a full daily history up to the delisting date in AKShare (primary); the BaoStock result is reported alongside | F1, then F2, then F3 (below) |
| A2 | Historical index constituents are point-in-time | Query CSI 300 and CSI 500 membership on >= 40 dates spread over 2010–2026; compare counts (300 / 500), turnover between rebalances, and agreement with the cross-check source and with a sample of official rebalance announcements. **Signed check (v0.5, M-R8): see "Signed date audits" below** | Counts exact on >= 95% of dates; membership on sampled dates matches the cross-check source for >= 98% of names; a stock known to have left the index is absent after its removal date; **signed criterion SD-A2** | Use constituent intervals from the cross-check source (needs approval); else F2 / F3. **If only SD-A2 fails: the lagged-membership rule below** |
| A3 | Survivorship in the assembled panel | Fraction of ever-members that have no price history; fraction that end before 2026 | No ever-member lacks price history for its membership dates (after A1 fallback); report the number of delisted / removed names | Report as a limitation with a bound on affected stock-days; if > 1% of member stock-days are missing, trigger F2 / F3 |
| A4 | Price fields agree across the two sources | Field-by-field comparison of unadjusted O/H/L/C, volume, amount on the full panel | Relative difference < 1e-4 on >= 99.5% of stock-days per field; every remaining discrepancy cluster explained (e.g. unit conventions: shares vs lots, yuan vs thousand yuan) | Resolve by third source; unexplained disagreements > 0.5% => F2 / F3 |
| A5 | Adjustment factors and corporate actions | Reconstruct the adjustment factor from unadjusted vs adjusted series; compare with AKShare dividend / split records and the cross-check source on >= 200 ex-dates; check that adjusted returns on ex-dates equal the total return implied by cash dividend and share changes | Ex-date total return matches within 1e-3 on >= 99% of sampled events; no unexplained jump > limit-implied bound on non-ex-dates | Rebuild factors from dividend records ourselves; otherwise F2 / F3 |
| A6 | `preclose` is the exchange reference price | Check `preclose` against prior close and ex-date reference prices; check that realised daily range respects limits computed from `preclose` | Violations of the computed limit band on < 0.1% of stock-days, each explained (IPO days, resumptions, rule changes) | Use the cross-check source's limit table; document the rule calendar gaps |
| A7 | Limit-up / limit-down prices can be computed | Compute limit prices from `preclose`, board, ST flag and the rule calendar (§3.4) with exchange rounding; compare with a source that carries limit prices | Match to the cent on >= 99.5% of compared stock-days | Use the external limit table directly (needs approval) |
| A8 | Suspension flag is reliable | `tradestatus == 0` vs zero volume vs AKShare suspension records | Agreement on >= 99.5% of stock-days; a suspended day never has positive volume | Define suspension as zero volume (document) |
| A9 | ST flag is point-in-time | `isST` history against a sample of >= 50 ST designation / removal announcements. **Signed check (v0.5, M-R8): see "Signed date audits" below** | Dates match within 1 trading day on >= 95% of events; **signed criterion SD-A9** | Exclude ST by an alternative list (cross-check source `joshuaxql/qlib_data`, needs approval), or run the "ST included" variant as primary and document |
| A10 | Turnover definition | Compare `turn` with volume / float shares where float shares are available; compare BaoStock `turn` with AKShare turnover | The definition (free-float vs total shares; percent vs fraction) is identified unambiguously; cross-source relative difference < 1% on >= 99% of stock-days | Z is recomputed under both definitions; the one used is fixed before modelling and reported |
| A11 | Timestamps and look-ahead | Confirm every field used at decision time for day t is from day t-1 or earlier; confirm constituent and ST information is as known before the open of day t, not back-filled | Code-level check plus a shuffled-future test: features recomputed after truncating the data at t-1 are bit-identical | Fix; no exception allowed |
| A12 | Opening-price field supports the synthetic fill rule SF (§3.2) | Share of stock-days where `open` equals the computed limit-up / limit-down price; share with missing `open`; share of traded days with `open` outside [low, high]; how each source fills `open` on a day without trades. **Daily bars cannot show whether the opening auction matched (I-§2.4 items 1–3); this item does not test that** | Descriptive (no threshold); must be reported before the execution convention is frozen | If `open` is unreliable there is **no entry rule covered by the instantiation to fall back on** (VWAP-type entries are "not covered", I-§7): stop and report to the lead / user before any result is seen (§3.2) |
| A13 | Reproducibility of the pull | Re-pull a 5% random sample one week later | Identical values except documented upstream revisions | Snapshot is frozen with hashes; revisions logged |

**Fallbacks (in order of preference; F2 and F3 need a user decision):**
- **F1.** Combine sources: take delisted histories, constituent intervals or a missing field from whichever approved free source has them (BaoStock, or cross-check source 2). Each such substitution is logged per field and reported to the lead (see §2.1).
- **F2.** Restrict the claim: narrower universe or shorter period where the audit passes (e.g. CSI 300 only, or 2015 onward), with the restriction stated as a limitation. Results on a universe with known survivorship bias would be labelled as such and not used for the primary claims.
- **F3.** **Obtain better data through the user's university** (e.g. a CSMAR / Wind / RESSET-type licensed database with delisted stocks, point-in-time constituents, and official limit prices). This contradicts the original "free data only" constraint and is the user's decision (open question in §16). Tushare is **not** an option: the user decided on 2026-11-15 not to use it (AKShare instead, no token).

The formal audit report (`experiments/results/data_audit/AUDIT.md`; to be written after the full pull — the smoke-test report is `AUDIT_REPORT.md`) lists every item with measured numbers, pass / fail, and the fallback taken.

**Changes to the table above in v0.3.** A9's fallback no longer uses `joshuaxql/qlib_data` unless U32 is confirmed; without it the fallback is the hand-compiled ST announcement sample plus the "ST included" variant as primary. A2 / A5 / A7 use cross-checks (i)–(iv) of §2.1. Additional items:

**v0.6 (V-1): RV1–RV13 have been checked** in `experiments/rule_verification.md` (26 rule items, with the documents opened and the ones that could not be opened). The results, the conventions changed because of them, and the gaps that remain are in §3.10. The paragraph and table below are the v0.5 specification of what was to be checked and of the consequence agreed in advance for each mismatch; those consequences were applied in v0.6. The sentence "None has been checked" describes v0.5.

**Exchange / regulator rules to be verified against the official texts before modelling (added v0.4, T9; the 13 items of I-§9).** None has been checked; no rule original was opened for this or any earlier revision. For each item the audit must archive the official text (URL, document title, effective dates, retrieval date) **for every sub-period of 2010–2026 in which the rule differed**, and record in `AUDIT.md` whether the convention of §3 matches it. "Source" names the official site to consult; **the document titles are written from general knowledge and are UNVERIFIED — locating the right document is part of the item.** News reports and the summaries in the Appendix do not count. General rule on failure: a convention that contradicts the official text is changed **before any outcome is seen** and the change is logged; where the text cannot be found for a sub-period, the item is reported "UNVERIFIED for that period" and the consequence in the last column applies.

Segments or periods whose rule cannot be established are excluded from the candidate set (logged) |
| RV6 | (6) T+1 | H >= 1; §3.3 | SSE / SZSE Trading Rules | If any instrument in the universe is exempt, it is listed; H >= 1 is kept for all |
| RV7 | (7) Kinds of suspension (whole-day, intraday, auction-covering) and when they are announced | C_t rule (§3.5); (S1); O1 branch P1; A22 | SSE / SZSE Trading Rules and Listing Rules (suspension and resumption chapters); exchange suspension notices | If announcement timing is not available in free data, C_t uses the day-(t−1) status only (the primary rule of §3.5 already does) |
| RV8 | (8) Delisting stages and the pre-delisting trading period | Listing-status check of the O1 tree; ledger terminal value (§3.9); A14 | SSE / SZSE Listing Rules (delisting chapters), all versions; CSRC delisting-reform documents | Cases not classifiable as S-d / S-e are outcome U (label 1) as already specified; the ledger convention (U33) is unchanged and the count reported |
| RV9 | (9) Record date versus ex-date | Entitlement rule for distributions (§3.8 (iii), §3.9) | CSDC (chinaclear.cn) rules on distribution of entitlements; SSE / SZSE Trading Rules | The entitlement window "record dates t … t+H−1" is restated in terms of the official relation; if free data lack record dates, they are derived from ex-dates by the verified relation, else outcome M |
| RV10 | (10) Dividend withholding rules | Withholding rate (U41) | Ministry of Finance / State Taxation Administration / CSRC notices on differentiated dividend tax (mof.gov.cn, chinatax.gov.cn) | U41 is reset to the rate the official text implies for the holding periods in the plan; holding-period dependence remains unmodelled (stated limitation) |
| RV11 | (11) Treatment and value of unexercised rights | S-c convention "not subscribed, valued at zero" | SSE / SZSE rights-issue business rules; CSDC | The convention is kept as a conservative choice and labelled so; the number of affected entries is reported |
| RV12 | (12) Board-lot and odd-lot rules | Lot-rounding robustness run (U39) | SSE / SZSE Trading Rules; STAR Market provisions (minimum order size) | The lot-rounding run uses the official lot sizes per board; the primary run (fractional shares) is unaffected and stays labelled a simplification |
| RV13 | (13) Fee schedule: stamp duty, transfer fee, minimum commission | §3.6 | MoF / STA announcements (stamp duty); CSDC and exchange fee schedules (transfer fee, handling fees); commission is broker-specific (no official rate) | Cost calendar corrected by date; components whose history cannot be established are set to the highest rate found in the period (conservative, logged) |

**Audit items for Z_O (merged from `data_feasibility_opponent_actions.md` §6; thresholds are that file's placeholders).**

| # | Item | How | Pass criterion (PLACEHOLDER) | If it fails |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OA1 | Disclosure timing | Variables with an announcement date (executed share changes): share with announcement date >= event date and the gap distribution. Variables without one (Dragon–Tiger, block trades): **find and archive the exchange rule text** that fixes the publication time, for every sub-period of the sample; the descriptions in `opponent_action_variables.md` §3 are marked there as not verified against originals | Announcement date missing <= 1%; announcement before event <= 0.5%, each explained. Rule original archived for the whole sample period | The class is used only with the extra lag of §5.6.2, or dropped from Z_O |
| OA2 | Removal of ex-post fields | Every column of every table labelled "known at disclosure / computed ex post / current snapshot"; features read through a whitelist | 100% of feature inputs are "known at disclosure". Not on the whitelist: "上榜后 N 日", "解读" (contains success rates), "最新价", "涨跌幅" of the share-change table, names | The table is not used |
| OA3 | History depth and yearly coverage | Per class and year: share of trading days with any record; share of the universe with a record | No run of 20 trading days with zero market-wide records; the class's analysis start is fixed at its measured start (probe: 2012-01 retrieved; 2010–2011 untested) | Start later than 2013-01 ⇒ the class is not in the confirmatory feature group |
| OA4 | Delisted stocks retained | 30 names from the A1 list; record counts versus matched surviving names | Share with records >= 90% of that of matched survivors | Class labelled "survivorship-affected", exploratory only |
| OA5 | Completeness / value check | Executed share changes: 50 stocks compared across Eastmoney, SSE, 10jqka. Dragon–Tiger and block trades (single upstream): 30 records each against exchange disclosures by hand | Match rate >= 95% (a check of **values**; it is unsigned and says nothing about early dating — dates are audited by OA10) | Class labelled "not cross-checked"; not used for the confirmatory test |
| OA6 | Revision / back-fill detection | Full pull at audit start; second pull of the same history >= 30 days later | Changed rows (older than 60 days at first pull) <= 0.5% | First snapshot frozen; change rate reported. **This detects revisions after our first pull only; back-filling before it (the share-change table carries entry timestamps of 2019 for records of the 1990s) cannot be detected by a second pull. The signed audit OA10 bounds its effect on dates for a sample; outside the sample it remains a stated limitation** |

| OA9 | Point-in-time codes | Codes in each table mapped to the daily-bar codes (the probe saw current 920-codes and current short names on old records) | One-to-one mapping for SSE / SZSE main board, ChiNext, STAR; names never used as features or filters | Mapping table; unmapped rows dropped and counted |
| **OA10** | **Signed audit of disclosure dates (added v0.5, M-R8)** | "Signed date audits" below, per class | Criterion SD-O, per class | **The class leaves the confirmatory feature group Z_O** (it stays available for exploratory analyses, labelled "failed the signed date audit") |

#### Signed date audits (added v0.5, M-R8; all thresholds and sample sizes PLACEHOLDER, §17 U47)

**Why.** A match rate (OA5; A2; A9) does not separate a date recorded too late (harmless for look-ahead) from a date recorded **earlier than the information was public** (look-ahead). Early dating of Z_O records would push card C4 toward "a difference was detected"; early dating of index membership or ST status affects the candidate set of all four cards. Agreement between vendors that may share an upstream is not validation. OA6 cannot see back-filling that happened before our first pull. The audits below compare the table with **the original document**, not with another vendor.

**SD-O — Z_O classes (LHB, BLK, INS), one audit per class.**
- *Sampling frame.* Rows of the frozen first snapshot (OA6) for ever-members of the universe, dated from the class's analysis start (OA3) to 2026-06-30.
- *Sampling scheme.* Stratified random sample: strata = calendar year (and, for INS, direction of the change); allocation proportional to the number of rows with at least 5 rows per stratum; sampling seed and the list of sampled rows written to disk **before any original document is opened**; no replacement of rows after the draw. **(v0.8, J-2, J-3; the implementer's reading, confirmed; scripts `experiments/src/features/audit/oa10_sample.py`, `oa10_judge.py`.)** Explicit stratification: LHB and BLK by year (19 strata); INS by year × direction (38 strata). Allocation of the 300 rows: proportional by largest remainder, a stratum whose share is below 5 is raised to 5. **Back-fill is an implicit stratification inside each INS stratum** (rows allocated between "entered more than 30 days after the announcement date" and the rest in proportion, no minimum), not a third explicit dimension: year × direction × back-fill would give 76 strata and need at least 380 rows (feature report §7.7 item 2), which n = 300 cannot satisfy. No reserve rows are drawn. Criterion (i) is evaluated on the rows actually verified, with the Clopper–Pearson limit at that number; since (ii) caps unverifiable rows at 10%, at least 270 rows are verified, and over 270 – 300 verified rows the rule is "pass iff at most 1 leak" (1.74% at 270 rows with 1 leak; feature report §7.7 item 3). **The sample in `experiments/results/feature_tests/oa10_sample.csv` is provisional** (frame = all A-share rows, not the frozen first snapshot restricted to ever-members): it is redrawn with the same script and seed on the frozen snapshot and the ever-member list, before any original is opened; no original may be retrieved for the provisional list.
- *Reference (rewritten v0.6, A-7).* The original disclosure, retrieved from the official site and archived (raw response or file, URL, retrieval time). A third-party news page or another data vendor does not count. Where originals are, according to the smoke test (AUDIT_REPORT §3.4; tiny samples that show feasibility only): **LHB, SZSE main board** — static daily files, retrieved for three dates back to 2010-01-04, with a server `Last-Modified` time of 16:21–17:05 on the trading day (a file-server time, not a publication time); the paths for the SME board and ChiNext were not tried. **LHB, SSE** — query interface by date, two dates retrieved, no time stamp. **BLK, SSE** — query interface by date, two dates retrieved, no time stamp. **BLK, SZSE — the official interface was not reached (UNVERIFIED).** **INS** — the announcement index of the statutory disclosure platform returns title, PDF address and an announcement time; of the few stamps seen, some carry a clock time (07:38:29; 06:31; 06:33) and some are 00:00:01, i.e. a date only. Establishing, per class, exchange and year, that originals can be retrieved remains the first step of the audit; rows whose original cannot be found are "unverifiable" as before.
- *What a script may do and what it may not (v0.6; instruction of the lead).* A script may retrieve and archive the official documents, propose matches, and — as a **supplementary check reported separately** — compare whole days of the LHB and BLK tables with the exchange disclosures in both directions. **Criterion SD-O is still evaluated on the pre-drawn sample of 300 rows per class, and each sampled row is compared with the archived original itself**, not with another vendor and not with a script's summary. LHB and BLK: the sampled record must be found in the exchange's disclosure of the day the table assigns; the match may be made by script on the archived full document, and **30 of the 300 rows per class (PLACEHOLDER, U57; drawn at random before the comparison) are re-checked by hand** against the archived document — the smoke test's parser had read a truncated copy of one document and skipped the STAR Market section, so a parser cannot be trusted unchecked; any disagreement between hand and script voids the scripted matches of that class until the parser is corrected and all 300 rows are re-run. INS: the link between a table row and an announcement, and the announcement's date, are **confirmed by hand for every sampled row** (the script proposes candidates; the title or the PDF is read).
- *What the audit can and cannot show for LHB and BLK (v0.6).* The exchange originals are filed by trading day and carry no publication time. For these two classes d_orig is the trading day itself, and the audit tests **whether the record is in the official disclosure of the day the table assigns to it**; a sampled row not found there counts as a **leak** (conservative). The audit cannot test that the disclosure was public before the next open. That rests on the rule text — block trades: "after the end of each trading day" (RC-19); Dragon–Tiger list: no clock time in the rule (RC-18) — and the lags of §5.6.2 are set accordingly.
- *Additional stratum for INS (v0.6).* If the upstream entry-time field is available in the full pull (the smoke test read it from the upstream directly), INS rows are also stratified by "entered more than 30 days after the announcement date" versus the rest, because 59% of the 562 rows inspected were back-filled in that sense. **(v0.8, J-2, J-10.)** The field is available (feature report §7.4: 41.2% of the retained rows are back-filled in that sense; 100% for announcement years up to 2011, 89–98% for 2012–2018, 24% for 2019, 0.4–4.9% from 2020). It enters as the implicit stratification described above. *Back-filled rows in the audit:* leak, early, late and unverifiable counts, and the Clopper–Pearson limit of the leak share, are reported separately for back-filled and other INS rows. This is descriptive and **carries no separate pass rule**: about 136 back-filled rows are in a sample of 300, and with 136 rows the one-sided 95% limit is 2.2% even with no leak (my arithmetic: 1 − 0.05^(1/136)), above p_max. The pass rule stays the pooled criterion of the class. *Back-filled rows in the features:* used like every other row, dated by the announcement date rolled forward to a trading day plus ℓ (§5.6.2); the entry time defines the back-fill flag for this audit and for descriptive tables only and never enters a feature. *If INS fails SD-O:* every `zo_ins_*` column leaves the feature set of LOSS(+Z_O) used for card C4; the other classes stay if they passed; the INS regime indicators in R_O stay, because R_O is in every feature set (§5.4) and is not changed by any audit outcome; if no class passes, C4 is dropped (family rule of §8.4). An exploratory analysis with INS and a longer lag remains allowed and labelled.
- *Signed quantities,* in trading days: d_tab = the date the table assigns (INS: announcement date; LHB / BLK: the trade / listing date after which the pipeline treats the record as published); d_orig = publication date of the original (a document with no time stamp is treated as published after the close of d_orig). With ℓ the class's availability lag of §5.6.2 (1 or 2), the record becomes usable for the decision of day d_tab + ℓ and was public for the decision of day d_orig + 1 at the earliest. **(v0.7, F-9, G-4.)** For INS, d_tab is the announcement date **rolled forward to the next market trading day when it is not one** — the date the feature code actually uses (18.9% of the rows of the four trial year-files are dated on non-trading days); an audit on the unrolled date would test a time the pipeline does not use. The sampling unit is a row of the table. For LHB, where one stock-day can have several rows, a row matches if the stock appears in the official disclosure of that day.
  - **early** (reported): d_tab < d_orig;
  - **leak** (the criterion): d_tab + ℓ < d_orig + 1, i.e. the pipeline would use the record on a decision day before it was public;
  - **late**: d_tab > d_orig (reported; loses information, creates no look-ahead);
  - **unverifiable**: no original found. Counted separately; never counted as a match.
- *Criterion SD-O (pass).* (i) the exact one-sided 95% (Clopper–Pearson) upper confidence limit of the leak share among verified rows is ≤ p_max = **2%** (PLACEHOLDER); (ii) unverifiable rows ≤ 10% of the sample overall and ≤ 25% in every calendar year (PLACEHOLDER); if (ii) fails only for the earliest years, the class's analysis start is moved to the first year from which (ii) holds and the audit is evaluated on the rows from that year on (the OA3 rule then applies: a start later than 2013-01 removes the class from the confirmatory group). The shares of early and late rows, the distribution of d_tab − d_orig, and the leak share by year are reported whatever the outcome.
- *Sample size and how it was set.* n = **300 verified rows per class** (PLACEHOLDER). Two requirements, by exact binomial arithmetic (throwaway script, no data): (a) with zero leaks observed the upper limit must be below p_max — this needs n ≥ 149 at p_max = 2% (n ≥ 59 would suffice only for a 5% limit); (b) a class whose true leak rate is small should not fail by chance too often. With n = 300 the class passes if and only if at most 1 leak is observed (upper limits: 0.99% for 0 leaks, 1.57% for 1, 2.08% for 2); the probability of passing is 0.96 / 0.83 / 0.56 / 0.20 / 0.02 at a true leak rate of 0.1% / 0.25% / 0.5% / 1% / 2%. The criterion is therefore strict: a class with a true leak rate of 0.5% fails almost half the time. If the user prefers a different p_max or n, both are changed **before the sample is drawn**; the same arithmetic gives the pass rule.
- *What is not done after a failure.* The lag ℓ is not lengthened and the audit is not repeated on a new sample in order to pass. A failed class leaves the confirmatory group; an exploratory analysis with a longer lag is allowed and labelled.
- *Consequence for the family.* The set of classes in Z_O is fixed by OA1–OA5 and OA10 before any modelling. If no class remains, card C4 is dropped and the Holm family shrinks by one (§8.4).
- *Pre-specified sensitivity reported beside C4 (exploratory, unadjusted):* ψ_O recomputed with the INS class removed from the gate's feature set (the class with a back-filled table and a date-only stamp). Pre-fixed reading: if the verdict category of C4 (§9.3) differs between the primary feature set and the set without INS, the C4 result is reported with the sentence "sensitive to the inclusion of the share-change class".
- *Cost (v0.6).* LHB and BLK: scripted against the exchange interfaces, plus 30 hand re-checks per class; INS: 300 rows by hand. Wall time unmeasured. v0.5's estimate of up to 900 documents opened by hand no longer applies.

**SD-A2 — index membership.** For 150 sampled constituent changes (stratified by index and year; PLACEHOLDER) taken from the official rebalance announcements of the index provider (archived with URL): the table's first membership date minus the official effective date. *Early inclusion or early removal* = the table shows the change before the official effective date. Pass: exact one-sided 95% upper limit of the early share ≤ 2% (PLACEHOLDER). By the same arithmetic as above, with n = 150 this means **no early case in the sample** (upper limit 1.98% for 0 of 150, 3.12% for 1 of 150); 100 changes would not be enough (2.95% for 0 of 100). **If SD-A2 fails:** membership used for the day-t candidate set is the membership recorded 20 trading days earlier (PLACEHOLDER lag; applied to the whole universe and all strategies), the change is logged before any outcome is seen, and the limitation is stated. A2's other criteria and fallbacks are unchanged.

**SD-A9 — ST status.** On 150 sampled designation / removal announcements (PLACEHOLDER; A9's sample of >= 50 is enlarged for the same arithmetic reason): the flag's change date minus the announced effective date. Early = the flag changes before the effective date. Same pass rule as SD-A2. **If SD-A9 fails:** the ST flag is not used to filter the candidate set (the "ST included" variant becomes primary, as in A9's existing fallback), logged before any outcome is seen.

**Limit of all three audits, printed with their results.** They bound the share of early-dated records in a sample; they do not show that any individual record outside the sample is correctly dated, and they validate dates, not values (values: OA5).

**v0.6 amendments to SD-A2 and SD-A9 (A-6).** *SD-A2:* no official list of rebalance changes was obtained, and under the primary universe U-LIQ index membership is not an input of the candidate set (§2.3). SD-A2 therefore gates nothing under U-LIQ; if it is carried out, its result is printed with the descriptive membership tables. Under the fallback universe U-IDX the 20-day lagged-membership rule applies from the start, without waiting for SD-A2. *SD-A9:* the smoke test compared 27 Shenzhen events with SZSE's dated name-change table — a table of effective dates, not the announcements — and found no dated reference for SSE names. The formal SD-A9 (150 events of both exchanges, against announcements on the statutory disclosure platform) is unchanged. Whether enough SSE announcements can be located is UNVERIFIED; unlocated events are "unverifiable" and are never counted as matches. If SD-A9 cannot be evaluated for SSE names, the lead decides **before the freeze** between applying the fail consequence to the whole universe ("ST included" primary) and keeping the filter with the sentence "ST status of SSE names is vendor-reported and unaudited" in every scope clause; the decision is logged.

### 2.5 Fetch budget and rate limits (rewritten v0.6, A-10; ESTIMATES extrapolated from the smoke test, AUDIT_REPORT §5; **pending user confirmation together with U54, §17.1 U24**)

**(v0.8, U-PILOT.) State of the pull.** The whole-market pull and the pull of the random sample (B) were stopped by the user. For the pilot round (§13A) the data are: the bars of the 1,000 names of the pilot list (889 already fetched plus 111 added), the index series, the three Z_O tables (pulled by date for the whole market, complete; filtered to the pilot list when used), and dividend / rights events from **two sources only, EV1 (Eastmoney) and EV2 (Sina, with its rights table)**; the BaoStock dividend table (EV3) is not fetched. The budget below is the one of the full plan; the fetch needed for the confirmatory population is decided with it (§2.3).

Measured in the smoke test: mean time of a successful call and first-attempt failures. Extrapolations are arithmetic on those means; nothing at full scale has been run.

| Data | Interface | Measured | Extrapolation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unadjusted daily bars 2008–2026 with `preclose`, status, ST, turnover (14 fields) | BaoStock, one query per name | 25.5 s per name (median 23.1, maximum 74.1); 0 failures in 67 | **7.1 hours per 1,000 names.** U-IDX (2,000–2,500 names; the union over the sampled dates was 1,845, a lower bound): 14–18 hours. **U-LIQ needs every A-share listed at any time since 2008; that count N_all is in BaoStock's basic-information table but is not stated in the audit report (UNVERIFIED here): time = 7.1 h × N_all / 1,000.** The time is server response time, not our rate limit |
| Dividend table | BaoStock, one query per (name, year) | 1.0 s; 0 failures in 6 | 2,500 names × 19 years ≈ 13 hours. Reduced by querying only names that are ever in the universe and, for each, the years with a `preclose` step and the adjacent years |
| Index snapshots (descriptive strata) | BaoStock | 2.1 s per query; 0 failures in 41 | Weekly, two indices: about 1.1 hours |
| Cross-check bars | AKShare–Sina, 2 HTTP requests per name | 3.5 s per name; 0 failures in 42 | About 2.5 hours for 2,500 names; only names ever in the universe. **Risk of an IP block at this scale is untested** |
| Sample bars, third source | AKShare–Tencent | 3.4 s per two years | Sample only (a full pull would take about 10 hours) |
| Dividend tables | AKShare: Eastmoney `stock_fhps_detail_em` | 1.9 s per name; empty for delisted names | About 1.5 hours |
| Dividend and rights tables | AKShare: Sina `stock_history_dividend_detail` | Not reported separately | UNVERIFIED |
| Factor table (audit reporting only) | BaoStock | 1.1 s per name | Names of the A16 sample only |
| Dragon–Tiger daily summary | `stock_lhb_detail_em` | 4.8 s per 3–5-day window | 198 monthly calls; 20–40 minutes |
| Block trades | `stock_dzjy_mrmx` | 2.0 s per window | 198 monthly calls; about 10 minutes; the row limit of one window is untested |
| Executed share changes | whole table | 2.0 s per page of 500 rows | About 295 pages; about 10 minutes |
| OA6 second pull; OA4 / OA8 samples | as above | — | Under one hour; OA8: 2 × 200 calls |
| OA10 originals | exchange interfaces; statutory disclosure platform | 3 of 3 (SSE), 3 of 3 after one retry (SZSE) | At most a few thousand requests in total; SZSE block-trade interface untested |
| **Not used** | AKShare Eastmoney bars (push2 / push2his) | 8 of 9 attempts failed | — |
| **Not budgeted (excluded)** | seat-level Dragon–Tiger detail; margin detail; northbound per stock; shareholder counts | — | §5.6.4 |

**Total (ESTIMATE).** With BaoStock as the bar source and 2,000–2,500 names (U-IDX), the audit report extrapolates **about 1.5–2.5 days of unattended wall time** for the base pull, serial and rate-limited, and under one hour for Z_O. v0.5's "2–5 days base, under a day for Z_O" was of the right order but for the wrong reason: the time goes into BaoStock's slow responses, not into the number of requests. **Under U-LIQ the bar pull grows in proportion to N_all** (7.1 hours per additional 1,000 names); the other rows do not grow, because they are needed only for names that are ever in the universe. If N_all turns out to be about twice the U-IDX name count — it is not known here — the bar pull alone would be about twice 14–18 hours.

**Rules (adjusted to what was measured).** One process per upstream host, serial within the host; different hosts may run in parallel. BaoStock: one session, queries in sequence, at least 1 s between queries. HTTP upstreams: at least 1.5 s between requests to the same host (the value adopted at Gate 2; the smoke test ran at 0.6–1 s and, outside Eastmoney's push2, had 2 network failures in 402 first requests, both recovered by a retry). Failure: retry after 5 / 20 / 60 s, at most 3 times, then the request goes to a resume list. A rate-limit disconnect (the push2 pattern): wait 330 s, at most three such waits, then the host is dropped for the day — in the smoke test it had not recovered after three hours, so no further retries are spent on it. Sina: at most 1,000 names per day (PLACEHOLDER) and stop at the first sign of a block. Daily cap 3,000 requests per HTTP host (unchanged). Checkpoint after every request; every raw response stored with its timestamp; an empty upstream answer is recorded as "no record" (AKShare raises an exception in that case and must be wrapped); an empty volume field is parsed as 0. The first complete pull is hashed and dated; it is the "first snapshot" of A13 and OA6.

---

## 3. Execution and accounting conventions (to be frozen before any outcome is seen)

Proposals implementing the checklist of `theory/instantiation_ashare.md` §8 (items 1–13; cited "I-§8.n"). After Gate 2, the rule audit RV1–RV13 and audit item A12, they are written to `experiments/src/engine/config.py` and `experiments/src/engine/rule_calendar.py` (v0.7, E-20; v0.6 named `experiments/src/common/conventions.py` — "the conventions file" below means these two files), which the feature library reads as well (F-20), and the hash of both is recorded in every result file. **(v0.6)** The status of every A-share trading, tax and disclosure rule used below is given in the rule calendar (§3.10; source `experiments/rule_verification.md`); "[rule: tbv]" tags left in the text are superseded by it. The conditions for the freeze are in §18. A change after the freeze alters the estimand or voids the guarantee and is logged (I-§8 preamble).

### 3.1 Two information times; the declared filtration (T1, T2; setup §1, A1, A1-G; I-§2.1, I-§2.4; I-§8.1–8.2)

- **G_t — pre-submission information.** Data dated ≤ t−1 (through the close of t−1) plus the pre-open public information of day t that the pipeline actually holds: trading calendar, the day's price limits computed from the reference price, corporate events effective on day t. **Everything a strategy chooses for day t is a function of G_t:** the candidate set C_t, the base ranking, the nominated list, every gate / filter / scaling decision, every random draw (RND; seeds fixed in advance), and the complete specification of every order — name, quantity, order type, limit price — and the capacity cap of (S3). At most one buy order per name per day and at most M = K buy orders per day; the cap is enforced on submitted orders.
- **Fills are determined afterwards**, by rule SF of §3.2. No strategy can condition on them on day t.
- **Declared post-fill filtration (case (b) of I-§2; daily bars only):**
  F_t^syn := all data dated ≤ t−1, the pre-open public information of day t, and the day-t **open, traded flag and suspension flag** of all names (and hence the synthetic fills of day t).
  It is increasing in t. It is an **artificial** information set: on a day on which a name had no opening-auction match it contains facts that became known only later that day (I-§2.4). The phrase "information at the opening-auction match" is **not** used anywhere in this project's results; case (a) of I-§2.3 would need timestamped auction data, which are not free (default: not obtained; §17 U35).
- **Which abstract assumption this supports.** A1-G is an implementation requirement on the order-generation code, enforced by the truncation test (A11; §13 criterion 3): recomputing every order from data truncated at G_t reproduces it bit for bit. A1 is claimed only with respect to (F_t^syn). The monitoring target is therefore r_T^entry **of the synthetic fill stream, defined with F_t^syn** (setup §5 item 5).
- **Confirmation for T2 (every selection uses G_t only).** Checked item by item in this revision: C_t (§2.3, §3.5: status through t−1 only); base scores and nominations (§4: features through t−1); thresholds and calibration maps (§4.3: previous year); gates CONF, LOSS, LOSS-LR, NET-K, NET-U, SCORE, VOL, LIQ, TREND (§6.1: features through t−1); PERF and all online states (labels frozen by day t−1 at the latest, i.e. entries of day s ≤ t − d_label; returns through the close of t−1); VT (G0's returns through t−1); E-const-dev (previous year); order quantities (sleeve cash at the close of t−1, limit-up price from the reference price, §3.7); strata of §8.3 (data through t−1); RND draws; the placement and permuted-Z controls of §7.3. **Not strategies and labelled as such:** E-const-expost and E-sched (ex-post attribution references), the hindsight diagnostic of §11. One change was needed: v0.3 let C_t exclude names "suspended on day t", which is not in G_t unless announcement timestamps exist (§3.5).

### 3.2 Synthetic fill rule SF, entry price, and the fill-determination sensitivity (T1; I-§2.4; I-§8.3–8.4)

**All fills in the backtest are retrospective synthetic fills.** They are called that in every table, figure and sentence.

> **SF (primary; condition (S2) restated in v0.6, V-14).** A buy order for stock i on day t, generated from G_t — a limit order at the day's upper price limit P^up (§3.7, U38; P^up computed from the reference price by RC-5 and RC-6 of §3.10) — is recorded as a **synthetic fill in full at the daily open of day t** if
> (S1) the daily bar of day t exists, is flagged as trading and has positive volume (the flag means whole-day suspension only, A22; an empty volume field counts as zero);
> (S2) the daily open is **strictly below the order's limit price P^up**;
> (S3) the order size does not exceed the capacity cap, a function of G_t (§3.7, U40).
> Otherwise it is recorded as **not filled** and cancelled (never carried to a later day). Sell orders (limit orders at the lower price limit P^down): the same with "strictly above P^down" in (S2). **Of the day-t bar SF reads only the traded flag, the suspension flag and the open** — never the high, low, close or the size of the volume.

- No partial fills: one entry is one whole position (a "lot") with a single entry price. The same rule is applied to every strategy, comparator, reference path and label.
- **Benchmark entry price = the published daily open.** Slippage and fees are booked as costs (§3.6) and never applied to the price.
- **Consequence (i) — what the monitoring results are about.** Theorem 1 and Corollaries 1.1–1.2 (statuses: header) are applied to the synthetic fill stream with filtration F_t^syn. A real order may not have filled where SF records a fill (no auction match, limited auction volume, queue position) or may have filled at another price; μ_t is conditional on F_t^syn, not on what a trader knew at the match. **Binding wording for RESULTS.md and the paper:** "for the synthetic fill stream under SF (filtration F_t^syn)" / "under the synthetic execution model"; never "for the strategy", "executable", "tradable", or "at the opening auction match". The same qualifier applies to fill rates, to "blocked" buys and exits (blocked *as defined by SF*, not in the market; I-§6.2) and to every trade-level table.
- **Consequence (ii) — limit-up and no-match days are approximated, and the size of the approximation error is not estimable from daily bars** (I-§2.4: it needs at least a sample of auction data). What can be done with daily bars is a sensitivity to the fill determination, pre-specified here:

| Fill determination | Buy recorded as filled iff (sell side mirrored at the lower limit) | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **SF** | (S1) ∧ (S2) ∧ (S3) | primary |
| **SF-strict** (conservative perturbation; the band threshold has no basis in the rule text) | (S1) ∧ (S2) ∧ open ≤ reference price × (1 + 0.8 × limit band) ∧ order size ≤ one tenth of the primary cap. Sell side: open ≥ reference price × (1 − 0.8 × limit band), with the sell forms of (S1)–(S3). **(v0.7, E-6)** The threshold is compared in exact decimal arithmetic and is **not** rounded to the tick; (S2) is kept as a conjunct, so that every SF-strict fill is also an SF fill. 0.8 and 1/10 are placeholders adopted at Gate 2 (U36) | sensitivity |
| **SF-loose** (optimistic bracket; changed in v0.6) | (S1) ∧ open ≤ P^up, no cap. Fills at the limit price are credited although the rule text does not guarantee them; fills above the order's own limit price are not credited | sensitivity |

  Both variants read the same three fields of the day-t bar and G_t only. They are applied to the **frozen** models, thresholds and orders of the primary run (only the fill layer, the ledger and everything computed from them are re-run; no refit). Reported for each: fill rate by method and reason; θ_sel, ψ_H, ψ_O with unadjusted intervals; ΔAR and its attribution terms; the Theorem 1 interval at the end of the window. **Pre-fixed reading:** if the verdict category of any card C1–C4 (§9.3 wording) differs between SF-strict and SF-loose, the primary result is reported together with the sentence "sensitive to the synthetic fill determination". The variants are exploratory and never replace the primary specification. **Limit of this analysis, to be printed with it:** all three rules are retrospective; none can identify days without an auction match, so the sensitivity bounds the dependence on (S2) / (S3), not the gap between synthetic and real fills.
- **Rule basis of (S2), and why it was restated (v0.6, V-14; RC-3; `rule_verification.md` A3).** Under the call-auction principle of the Trading Rules, buy orders priced above the match price and sell orders priced below it are executed in full; time priority matters only among orders priced exactly at the match price. A buy order entered at the limit-up price is therefore filled in full **by rule** whenever the auction produces a match price strictly below the limit-up price, whatever its place in the queue. Consequences: (a) the order price of U38 is part of the fill rule — it is what gives the full fill a basis in the rule text — and may not be replaced by another limit price without changing SF; (b) v0.5 wrote (S2) as "the open is not the upper price limit". On an ordinary day the two forms are the same, because the open cannot exceed the limit. They differ on days on which a name trades without a price limit (RC-6 (g): first day of a delisting-arrangement period, first day after resumption of listing, an SSE follow-on-offering listing day, and others) and on days with a wrong `preclose`: there the open can lie above the computed P^up, and v0.5's wording would have recorded a fill at a price above the order's own limit, which no reading of the rule allows and which the pre-set quantity could not pay for. The restated (S2) records "not filled". (c) With this form SF needs no calendar of no-limit days. The pipeline cannot know those days point-in-time (their dates are not in the free data in dated form), and the restated (S2) gives the outcome of an order priced at the computed P^up either way. Whether such an order would have been accepted on a no-limit day was not checked (the valid order-price range on those days is not in `rule_verification.md`); such days are counted, not modelled.
- **What remains an assumption (unchanged in substance).** (i) Our own order does not change the match price; the cap (S3) stands in for this and the rules guarantee nothing. (ii) Daily bars do not show whether the opening auction matched. The published open is the first trade of the day (RC-4); it can come from the continuous session, or from a resumption auction later in the day. A buy order cancelled at the end of the opening auction (U39) would not have been filled on such a day although SF records a fill. (iii) The exchanges' maximum order sizes (RC-11) are not modelled; the number of simulated orders above them at the notional capital is reported.
- **Review of the two sensitivity variants (v0.6, V-14).** *SF-strict:* definition unchanged (U36 was adopted as a default at Gate 2). Its stated purpose is corrected. The condition "open at most 0.8 of the band above the reference price" has **no basis in the rule text**: below the limit price, queue position is irrelevant. It is kept as a conservative perturbation against assumption (i) — an own order moving a near-limit match price to the limit — and against errors in `preclose`; the one-tenth cap addresses (i) directly. *SF-loose:* changed from "(S1) only" to "(S1) and open ≤ P^up, no cap". Fills at the limit price are still credited; the rule text does **not** guarantee them (time priority decides), which is why SF-loose is an optimistic bracket and is labelled so. Fills above the order's limit price are no longer credited. On ordinary days neither change alters any fill of any of the three rules. The limit printed with the sensitivity stands: none of the three rules can identify days without an auction match.
- **Suspended-day rows and other row-level cases (v0.6, A-4, A-5; AUDIT_REPORT A8, A12, A22).** The `open` field was never missing and never outside [low, high] on the 196,686 traded stock-days of the smoke test; A12 is reported and U44 is not triggered. This is consistent with the definition of SF: SF never claimed to see whether the auction matched, and the audit confirms that daily bars cannot show it. BaoStock keeps a row on whole-day suspension days, with open = high = low = close = `preclose` and volume 0 or an empty string (1,273 rows with an empty string); the Sina, Eastmoney and Tencent feeds have no row. Implementation of (S1): status flag = trading **and** parsed volume > 0. The three inconsistent rows found (two flagged suspended but with volume, both on 2024-06-13; one flagged trading with empty volume) are "not filled" under this rule and are counted. **The `open` of a suspended-day row is never used as a fill price or as a valuation**; suspended names are valued by branch P1, computed by us (§3.8 (iii), §3.11). Intraday suspensions are flagged by BaoStock as ordinary trading days (4 of 4 checked); on such a day the published open may come from the resumption auction and SF records a fill at it. That lies within the declared meaning of "retrospective synthetic fill" (F_t^syn contains the day's open) and outside anything executable at 09:25. Days of the early-year routine one-hour suspension cannot be identified from the free data (RC-4). 2016-01-04 and 2016-01-07 (index circuit breaker; the opening auction ran normally, RC-7) are flagged in the trade ledger.
- **Wording.** The fill price is called "the published daily open", never "the auction price" (RC-4).
- **Delay robustness:** SF applied one day later (signal one day stale).
- **VWAP entry — outside the theory.** I-§7 lists entry rules with fills spread over the day as "not covered". v0.3's VWAP timeline is kept only as an empirical robustness run for Q-total and Q-select: entry at the day-t VWAP proxy (`amount / volume`; units UNVERIFIED until A4), blocked if the name did not trade or traded at one price equal to limit-up all day; exit by the primary rule. **No Theorem 1 quantity, no O1 label of §3.8 and no monitoring interval is computed on it;** results go in a separate table labelled "not covered by any theory result" and are never pooled.
- **If audit item A12 fails** (unreliable `open`): v0.3 made the VWAP timeline primary in that case. That is no longer possible, because no covered entry rule would remain. The pipeline stops and the lead / user decide before any outcome is seen (§17 U44).

### 3.3 T+1, holding period, exit, and the two lags (T3; setup A2(b), A3, §4; I-§4; I-§8.6–8.7)
- Shares bought on day t cannot be sold on day t (verified: RC-1). **H = 5 trading days (PLACEHOLDER)**; robustness H in {1, 10, 20}. H, d_label and d count **market trading days** of the exchange calendar (not a stock's own trading days).
- **Scheduled exit: the opening auction of day t+H**, by the liquidation policy LP of §3.8 (iv). No stop-loss, take-profit or discretionary exit.
- **Label-finalisation lag d_label = H + 1, fixed now.** Every O1 label of an entry of day t is computed **once**, from the data snapshot of day t + d_label (data available before the decision point of day t+H+1), stored, and **never recomputed or revised** — not when later data arrive, not when an upstream value is corrected, not for any other monitoring lag (setup A2(b); I-§3 "Snapshot"). The label needs facts known only at the close of day t+H (branches P3 / P4), which is why the lag this label supports is H + 1.
- **Monitoring lag d = H + 1, fixed now, deterministic (lead's decision; the user may veto, §17 U25).** d ≥ d_label, so A3 holds with d if every label is in the declared information set of day t + d_label; that is the availability assumption OA-next of I-§4, **an audit item (A18), not an established fact**, and with one downloaded file it can hold only "as a function of the downloaded file". Lanes are residue classes mod d of the market trading-day index. H = 1, 5, 10, 20 ⇒ d_label = d = 2, 6, 11, 21.
- **d is never chosen or changed after data are seen** (setup §4: the radius is not monotone in d). No analysis in this plan uses a second monitoring lag. If one were ever wanted, it would be a separately declared analysis **on the same frozen labels** (setup §4), never a recomputation of labels with a later data deadline.
- **No statement in this plan says that O1 has lag H.** A lag of H would need a different label that uses auction-time information only ("O1-auction", I-§4), which requires timestamped auction data; it is not specified and not used. v0.3's "d = H under OA-open" row and remarks are deleted.

### 3.4 Price limits (rewritten v0.6, V-2; the rule calendar is §3.10)
- **Reference price.** The data source's unadjusted `preclose` field. It is **never recomputed by us** from event records: the exchange's formula is known (RC-5), but an issuer may apply for a different one, and the smoke test found the Eastmoney cash amounts inconsistent with the exchange's reference price on 29 of 467 events.
- **Limit prices.** P^up = `preclose` × (1 + r), P^down = `preclose` × (1 − r), rounded half-up to 0.01 yuan in decimal arithmetic (`Decimal`, `ROUND_HALF_UP`; Python's built-in `round` rounds half to even and is not used); if a rounded limit differs from `preclose` by less than 0.01 yuan it is set to `preclose` ± 0.01 yuan (RC-5; verified). The band r is read from RC-6 by board, date and the ST flag of day t. The status in force on day t is announced before day t; in our file it is the flag on the day-t row, and it is treated, like `preclose`, as pre-open public information of day t (G_t). The candidate filter uses flags through t−1 only (§2.3).
- **Smoke test (A6, A7).** 13 of 196,622 traded stock-days lie outside the computed band: 9 first days of delisted names resuming after a long suspension (three of them identified as first days of a delisting-arrangement period by a same-day name change, six by the same pattern), one first day after resumption of listing, two resumptions after long suspensions, and one unexplained (+27.9% on 2009-06-10). Closes equal to the computed limit: 3,611 stock-days at limit-up and 3,142 at limit-down, i.e. the rounding rule reproduces actual limit closes. No source of limit prices was available for an external comparison.
- **Days without a price limit (RC-6 (g)).** The pipeline computes P^up and P^down on every day as if the day's band applied. It does not need to know the no-limit days: by the restated (S2) a buy is not filled when the open is at or above the computed P^up, and a sell is not filled when the open is at or below the computed P^down and is re-submitted on the next day. On a no-limit day with a fall beyond the nominal band the model therefore delays the exit by one day, which is what an order priced at P^down would experience. Audit item A7 classifies the out-of-band days after the fact, for the audit report only; **that classification uses the day's high and low and is never an input of a decision or of SF.** (`rule_verification.md` proposed dropping a name from the candidate set on a day whose move cannot be attributed to a class; not adopted, because it would condition a day-t decision on day-t prices — Revision log V-2.)
- **Buy not filled (synthetic):** (S1), (S2) or (S3) of §3.2 fails. A non-filled buy is **cancelled, not retried**; the money stays in cash for that slot. It is an unfilled order: not an entry (A_{ti} = 0), no label, recorded only in the order ledger (§3.8 (i)).
- **Sell not filled (synthetic):** the sell-side form of (S1)–(S3) fails on the exit day. The sale is re-submitted at each following opening auction (LP, §3.8 (iv)).
- Queue priority among orders priced at the limit is not modelled; below the limit price it does not matter, by rule (RC-3). (S2) never credits a fill at the limit price. Sensitivity: §3.2.

### 3.5 Suspension
- **Candidate set (changed in v0.4 for G_t-measurability).** A name is removed from C_t if it was suspended on day t−1 (a fact dated ≤ t−1). A name that traded on day t−1 and is suspended on day t receives an order if nominated; the order is recorded as not filled by (S1).
