Consequence, stated: a name resuming on day t is not a candidate on its resumption day. Using pre-open suspension / resumption announcements instead would need announcement timestamps, whose availability in the free data is UNVERIFIED (**[rule: tbv, RV7]**; A22); if the audit finds them, the refinement is a logged change made before any outcome is seen.
- **Suspended while held:** valued by branch P1 of the O1 tree for the label (§3.8 (iii)) and by the same rule in the ledger (OV / CV of §3.9: last close at which the name traded, restated for later corporate events); cannot be sold; LP re-submits the sale at each following auction.

- **Source of the suspension status (v0.6).** BaoStock's status flag only (A8, A22: the flag means whole-day suspension; intraday suspensions are trading days in the data). The Eastmoney suspension table is not used. The exchanges' rules fix no clock time for suspension announcements (RC-9), so the refinement "use pre-open announcements" is not available from the free data and the day-(t−1) rule stays.
- **Delisting-arrangement period (v0.6, V-9).** A name in a delisting-arrangement period must not be opened: individual buyers face suitability conditions (RC-10), the same reason for which ST names are excluded. BaoStock's ST flag is 0 inside the period (AUDIT_REPORT A9) and the free data carry no dated flag for the period. The exclusion is therefore implemented by a proxy that is a function of G_t: **a name is removed from C_t if its ST flag was 1 on any of the 60 market trading days ending t−1** (PLACEHOLDER U55; the period lasted 30 trading days before 2020-12-31 and 15 since, RC-10, plus the days between the delisting decision and the start of the period). Stated costs of the proxy: a name whose ST status was lifted is also excluded for 60 days; a name that enters an arrangement period without having been ST in that window is not caught (how often this happens is unknown; when the period starts after a suspension, its first day is excluded anyway by the suspended-on-(t−1) rule). The number of entries opened in names that the exchange delisting lists later show to have been in an arrangement period is reported — an ex-post count, never a filter.
- **"Suspension of listing" before 2020-12-31 (v0.6, V-9).** Until the delisting reform of 2020-12-31 a stock could be in "suspension of listing" for about a year: legally still listed, not trading (RC-10). In the O1 tree and in the ledger such a holding is **suspended — branch P1, carry-forward value — not outcome U**; it becomes U only if the stock is finally removed without S-d / S-e. The legal status cannot be read from the free data. Reported instead: P1 marks split by the length of the suspension spell at the mark day (at most 20, 21–60, more than 60 market trading days) and, as a tabulation labelled "hindsight", how many of the long ones ended in delisting without a resumption. Without this split a reader would assume that delisting risk is entirely in outcome U.
- **Rights-issue payment period (v0.6, V-11).** The stock is suspended during the payment period and ex-rights trading starts only afterwards (RC-13). Treatment: §3.8 (iii) note (2), §3.11.

### 3.6 Transaction cost model (defaults) and benchmark-price accounting (T4; I-§8.8; Lemma 3 of theorems.md)

| Component | Default | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Stamp duty | By trade date (RC-15): 2008-01-01 to 2008-04-23 **both sides 0.3%**; 2008-04-24 to 2008-09-18 **both sides 0.1%**; 2008-09-19 to 2023-08-27 seller only 0.1%; from 2023-08-28 seller only 0.05% | 2008 dates: news pages of the Ministry of Finance (official page, not the document). 2023: read on an official repost; the original page could not be opened — **unverified against official text**. No later change was found (search not exhaustive). The two 2008 rows precede the first simulated trade (2010-01-01) and are in the calendar for completeness |
| Transfer fee (both sides) | By trade date (RC-16). **Before 2015-08-01:** SSE names — charged on par value: 0.000375 yuan per share traded (0.375‰ of a par value of 1 yuan, the highest rate found for the period); SZSE names — 0.0255‰ of traded value. **2015-08-01 to 2022-04-28:** 0.02‰ of traded value on both exchanges. **From 2022-04-29:** 0.01‰ | 2015 change: official page. Before 2015: the SSE par-value rate is 0.3‰ in the 2015 official Q&A and 0.375‰ in a 2012 CSRC news item whose effective date was not recorded; the rates before 2012, the date of the change between the two, and the SZSE rate at dates other than 2015 are **unverified**; par value is taken as 1 yuan for every name (unverified; a name with a smaller par value is overcharged). 2022 change: search summary only — **unverified against official text**. `rule_verification.md` suggested a flat 0.003% of traded value before 2015-08; not adopted (Revision log V-4) |
| Brokerage commission (both sides) | 0.025% (2.5 bps) per side (placeholder adopted at Gate 2); minimum of 5 yuan per order ignored in the primary run and **applied in the lot-rounding robustness runs** | Broker-specific. Official bounds exist (RC-17): at most 3‰ of traded value, at least 5 yuan per trade; the exchange handling fee and the regulatory fee are part of the commission, so they are not booked separately |
| Modelled execution shortfall ("slippage"; both sides) | 5 bps per side of the benchmark-price trade value, PLACEHOLDER | **UNVERIFIED** — not estimated from data. Sensitivity in §11. |
| Tax on cash distributions | **Rate schedule by record date (U41, rewritten v0.6; RC-14): 10% for record dates before 2013-01-01; 20% from 2013-01-01** (the rate for holdings of one month or less). Fixed in advance; the dependence on the actual holding period is not modelled. Timing of cash and tax: §3.11 | The 2012 and 2015 notices were verified; the 10% before 2013 is confirmed only indirectly (**unverified against official text**). From 2015-09-08 the tax is not withheld at payment but collected when the shares are sold. Entries with H = 20 and delayed liquidations can in reality fall into the 10% bracket (holding longer than one month); the schedule overstates their tax. Sensitivity: 0% and 10% flat |
| Financing costs | None (no leverage) | — |
| Lot size | Ignored in the primary run (fractional shares); robustness run with lot rounding by board (RC-11): main board and ChiNext — buys rounded down to multiples of 100 shares; STAR Market — no order below 200 shares, otherwise rounded down to whole shares | Simplification. Odd lots are sold in one order, which agrees with "sell the whole lot" |

Round-trip cost at defaults after 2023-08-28: 2 x (2.5 + 5 + 0.1) + 5 = about 20 bps; before: about 25 bps. (v0.6: this arithmetic leaves out the per-share transfer fee of SSE names before 2015-08-01 and the tax on distributions, which is not a trading cost.)

**Benchmark-price accounting — one rule for every strategy, comparator and reference path (lead decision of 2026-11-15, `STATUS.md`).**
1. **Same instant, same price.** At every valuation instant all strategies (gate G, baseline B, the attribution references E-sched and E-const-expost, the standard orders of §8.3) value holdings, pre-trade holdings, cost bases, marks and returns at the **same benchmark price**: at the opening instant, the open valuation OV of §3.9 (the published daily open for a name that traded; the price rule of I-§3 Step 2 otherwise); at the end-of-day instant, the closing valuation CV of §3.9. **CV is defined by this plan, not by the theory files, and is used only to cut the additive attribution into an intraday and an overnight interval and to draw a daily wealth curve (§3.9, M-X1); no trade is ever booked at a close and no card C1–C4 depends on it.** No strategy has its own "execution price".
2. **Every economic cost exactly once, through the cost term C.** Explicit charges (stamp duty, transfer fee, commission) and the modelled shortfall are debited as costs. **Slippage is never also applied to a price**, a return, a cost basis or a mark. Execution-price accounting (kept in `theorems.md` only as an alternative under its condition (EX)) is not used.
3. **Trades are share-based.** trade_t = (s_t − s_{t−}) ∘ p_t, where s_{t−} is the vector of pre-trade share counts **after the corporate actions effective by that instant** (bonus shares, splits, consolidations: multiplier applied), s_t the post-trade share counts, p_t the benchmark price on the same (ex-entitlement) terms. **Cost base (v0.7, E-3).** trade_t is the *net* change of the share vector, as the theory files define it (I-§8 item 8), and is reported as such. **Costs are charged per executed order, on the order's own benchmark-price value |q_order| × OV_t** (ad-valorem rates), plus the per-share transfer fee where one applies (§3.7). A buy order and a sell order of one strategy in one name on one day are separate orders and are not netted (U42), so the order-level turnover TO_t = Σ_orders |q_order| × OV_t can exceed the net traded amount ‖trade_t‖₁; the two are equal on every day on which no name has both a filled buy and a filled sell of that strategy. v0.6 wrote "traded amount = |trade_t|; every cost rate is applied to it", which contradicts U42 on such days. The order-level base is the only one consistent with lot-level labels (the cost basis of an entry contains the charges of its own buy order, §3.8 (iii)), it is the higher of the two, and each order's cost is booked once, to its own lot. Lemma 3 holds for any cost term, so the identity is unaffected. Reported for every strategy: TO_t, ‖trade_t‖₁, and the "non-netting" amount TO_t − ‖trade_t‖₁ with the cost charged on it. **(v0.8, H-5; the implementer's formula, confirmed; reporting only.)** "Cost charged on the non-netting amount", per name and day with both a filled buy and a filled sell of the strategy: the costs of the two orders minus the cost of a single order for the net quantity at the same rates on the same day; when the two orders offset exactly it equals the sum of the two orders' costs. It changes no charge: costs are still those of the executed orders. The attribution reference h^E has no orders; its notional cost is computed from its net implied trades (§8.2). A cost is **never** computed from the difference of two consecutive days' position values. The return-based shortcut h_t − h_{t−1} ∘ (1 + R) may be used only with the ex-distribution, share-adjusted price return (I-§8.8); the code uses the share formula. The formulas are used for strictly positive prices and multipliers and for multiplier- and cash-type corporate actions only (round-3 item X11); anything else is class U (§3.8 (iii)).
4. **Cash distributions are booked separately** in the cash ledger by the entitlement rule of §3.8 (iii) and are **never a trade** (traded amount 0, cost 0). The tax on a distribution reduces the distribution (schedule U41, §3.11); it is not part of the cost term C.
5. **Costs are debited at the trade** (start of the period). P&L uses total returns.
6. **Wealth recursion with a non-zero cash return (robustness run only; round-3 item K1).** With costs debited at the trade, the ledger must satisfy W_{T+1} − W_1 = Π + Σ_t ρ_t (W_t − c_t), with Π, ρ_t and W_t (wealth at the start of period t, before that period's trade) as defined in Lemma 3 of `theorems.md`, which governs. **Not** Π + Σ_t ρ_t W_t. In the primary run the cash return is zero (W4) and the two coincide. **(v0.7, E-19.)** In that run the cash return is credited once per trading day, on the overnight interval (one rate covering the calendar days since the previous trading day); the intraday rate is 0. The base is the one the engine implements and tests against the recursion above — spendable cash plus receivables minus recorded tax liabilities, i.e. all wealth not held in securities, after the period's cost. That receivables earn the cash return before they are paid is a small overstatement, stated with the run. `theorems.md` governs the formula. **(v0.8, H-8; the implementer's convention, confirmed.)** The rate is an annual simple rate with day count ACT/365 (calendar days since the previous trading day). No rate series with a source has been fixed (U20: "money-market series UNVERIFIED"). The run is executed only if such a series, with its source, is written into the conventions before Freeze-1; otherwise it is dropped and the drop is logged (§19, KL-6). Nothing confirmatory depends on it.

### 3.7 Capital allocation, order specification, cash (T2, T6)
- **Sleeves: H + 1 (not H).** Sleeve j trades on days t ≡ j (mod H+1): it buys at the open of t, sells at the open of t+H, and its sale proceeds are cash at the close of t+H, available for its next purchase at the open of t+H+1. **No purchase is funded by proceeds of a sale in the same auction** (whether that is possible is UNVERIFIED; the plan does not rely on it). The H-sleeve design is a labelled sensitivity (§11).
- **Slots.** Each day the base strategy nominates its top-K names (K = 30, PLACEHOLDER). S_t = orders submitted, n_t = synthetic fills; S_t <= K, so M = K. One order per name per day (I-§8.3).
- **Pre-determined order quantity.** Slot cash = (the trading sleeve's cash at the close of t-1) / K. For a submitted name i the order is a limit order at the day-t limit-up price P_i^up (§3.4) for q_i = slot cash / (P_i^up (1 + κ_t) + f_t) shares **(v0.7, E-4)**, where κ_t is the sum of all buy-side ad-valorem rates in force on day t by the rule calendar — modelled shortfall, commission, ad-valorem transfer fee, and stamp duty on days on which the buyer pays it (before 2008-09-19) — and f_t is the per-share transfer fee in yuan (SSE names before 2015-08-01; otherwise 0). In runs that apply the 5-yuan minimum commission (the lot-rounding robustness runs) the slot cash is first reduced by 5 yuan, so that the order can be paid for whichever of the rate and the minimum binds; if nothing is left, no order is submitted (order-ledger reason "min_commission"). When several lots of one name are sold in one order (U42), the minimum applies once to the order and is allocated to the lots pro rata to shares. Quantities depend only on G_t and are always affordable. **(v0.8, H-14; behaviour of the v0.7 code, confirmed.)** On sells the commission is also max(rate × value, 5 yuan). If it exceeds the sale value of the order, the sale is still booked and the sleeve's cash can become negative; a sleeve whose cash net of liabilities is not positive submits no order (reason "no_cash", outside S_t) until it is positive again, so no purchase is ever funded by a negative balance and nothing is borrowed. Such sell orders are counted (C-5). With board lots of at least 100 shares this requires a price below 0.05 yuan; it is not expected to occur (§19, KL-5). Fractional shares in the primary run; 100-share lots as robustness.
- **Consequence, by arithmetic only:** an ungated sleeve is invested for H of every H+1 days and each filled slot invests about open / P^up of its cash, so the ungated strategy's invested fraction is of the order of (H/(H+1)) × 0.9 ≈ 0.75 at H = 5 on 10%-limit boards (ESTIMATE; depends on fills and opening gaps). The same rule applies to every strategy and comparator. An idealised "notional at the open" sizing (not implementable as a pre-auction order) is a labelled sensitivity.
- **Rejected or unfilled slots — primary ("cash") rule:** the money stays in the sleeve's cash until its next trading day. **"Refill" variant (secondary):** the sleeve's cash is split equally among submitted names, at most 3 slots per name (PLACEHOLDER), as **one** order per name; quantities are still fixed pre-auction.
- Cash return 0 in the primary run (W4 of Lemma 3). Non-filled exits tie up capital; the sleeve's next orders are sized from its actual cash. No leverage, no shorting.

**Order-and-fill policy: default values for the items I-§6.3 / I-§9 list as external inputs (T6). All were PLACEHOLDERS; they were adopted as defaults at Gate 2 (§17.1 A), except where §3.10 corrected them in v0.6; none is left open, because the backtest cannot be implemented otherwise. Under SF several of them do not change any simulated number (I-§6.1: "order type and limit price do not enter the simulation"); they are fixed anyway so that the simulated policy is a complete, nameable policy.**

| Item | Default (PLACEHOLDER) | Enters the simulation? | §17 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Buy order type and limit price | Limit order at the day's upper price limit P^up, opening call auction only | Through the quantity q_i (sizing at P^up) **and through (S2): the order price is what gives the full fill its basis in the rule text (§3.2)** | U38 |
| Sell order type and limit price | Limit order at the day's lower price limit P^down, for the entire remaining quantity of the lot, opening call auction only | Through the sell-side form of (S2) | U38 |
| Information cut-off and submission time | Inputs: data dated ≤ t−1 plus the pre-open public information held by the pipeline; nominal cut-off 09:00, submission 09:15 exchange time on day t. **Verified (RC-2):** the opening call auction takes orders from 09:15 to 09:25 and accepts no cancellation from 09:20 to 09:25, unchanged over the sample. With daily data the clock times only declare which data may be read | Only as the definition of G_t | U37 |
| Unfilled remainder | Cancelled at the end of the opening auction; **not** left in the continuous session. Buys are not retried; sells are re-submitted at the next opening auction | Yes | U39 |
| Partial fills | **None** under SF (all-or-nothing). If fill-level data are ever used: an entry exists if any shares fill, the lot is the filled quantity, sales allocated to lots first-in-first-out by entry day, charges and shortfall pro rata to value sold, a lot partly sold at a deadline counts as not sold (I-§6.3) — and the remaining external inputs must be fixed in writing first | Not in this plan | U39 |
| Board lots / odd lots | Fractional shares (primary). Robustness, by board (RC-11): main board and ChiNext — buy quantities rounded down to multiples of 100 shares; STAR Market — no order if the quantity is below 200 shares, otherwise rounded down to whole shares; sells of the whole lot | Robustness only | U39 |
| Capacity cap of (S3) / (E4) | **(Rewritten v0.7, E-5.)** Order value ≤ **1% of ADV_t**, where **ADV_t = the mean unadjusted traded amount (yuan) over the 20 most recent market trading days dated ≤ t−1 on which the name traded** (days without a trade are skipped, not counted as zero; fewer than 20 such days in the name's history: the mean of those that exist; none: cap 0). Order value: buys — quantity × the order's limit price P^up; sells — quantity (after the corporate actions effective by day t) × the day-t reference price `preclose`, which is pre-open public information (v0.6 wrote "previous close", which is on the wrong terms on an ex-date). "At notional capital W_0" means that the simulation itself is run at W_0 = 10 million yuan (U19) and the order values are the simulated ones. An order above the cap is recorded as not filled, reason "cap". *Why the definition was changed.* v0.6 did not say how no-trade days enter the average. Counted as zero they make the cap exactly 0 on the first day after a suspension of 20 or more trading days, so that no sell could ever be recorded as filled on a resumption day — an artefact of the formula, not a statement about liquidity (trial dry run: 21 such sell orders; a count of orders, not an outcome). The replacement uses pre-submission information only. Its cost, stated: after a long suspension the average is stale; fills whose ADV window reaches back more than 60 market trading days are counted ("stale-cap fills", U59). Sensitivities: no cap; one tenth of the cap (SF-strict); **"cap-zero" — the v0.6 wording read literally (no-trade days counted as zero)** | Yes; it is the only place where the notional capital affects fills | U40 |
| Same security, several lots | Each entry is its own lot, identified by (entry day, name, sleeve). A name already held may be nominated and bought again (new lot). A buy order and a sell order in the same name on the same day are separate orders and are **not netted** | Yes | U42 |
| Several lots of one security due for sale on the same day (a retried exit meeting a scheduled one) | The capacity condition is applied to their **total**; the synthetic fill is all-or-nothing for the total (I-§6.1), so no allocation among lots is needed; proceeds and costs go to each lot (and its sleeve) pro rata to shares sold | Yes | U42 |
| Minimum commission per order | Ignored (primary); included in the lot-rounding robustness run | Robustness only | U1 |
| Broker allocation and fee records | Not applicable (no live trading) | No | — |

The standard order of §8.3 (1 yuan of slot cash) is checked against the cap at the notional slot size W_0 / ((H+1) K), so that ρ_{ti} stays a function of the name and the day only.

### 3.8 Correspondence with the theory files (`setup.md` and `instantiation_ashare.md` govern)

**(i) Executed entry = synthetic fill (I-§8.3).** A_{ti} = 1 iff a buy order in i is recorded as filled by SF on day t; n_t, N_T count synthetic fills; A_{ti} ≤ O_{ti} (A1-G). Unfilled orders have no position and **no label**, and appear only in the **order ledger** (per day and gate: S_t, n_t, non-fills by reason: (S1), (S2), (S3)). **The denominator of every loss rate is the number of executed entries N_T, never the matured entries only. The fill rate is reported next to every loss rate and every trade-level table.**

**(ii) The executed set is the gate's selection composed with the synthetic fill filter.** μ_t is the expected loss count given F_t^syn; it is not the loss probability assessed with G_t when the order was chosen (setup §5 item 5). The fill filter can act differently on different gates. Reported, not corrected: fill rate by gate and reason; characteristics of filled versus unfilled orders; paper outcome of unfilled orders (labelled non-executable); the fill-determination sensitivity of §3.2. A per-submitted-order tabulation (non-fills counted with outcome 0) may be shown as a **descriptive table only**: submission-based entries are a different estimand that I-§7 does not specify, so no label of §3.8, no Theorem 1 quantity and no interval is computed for it.

**(iii) Label O1 — primary: the decision tree of I-§3, implemented exactly as written there (T5; I-§8.5). The text of I-§3 governs; the summary below is for orientation.**
- *Label.* L^mark_{ti} = 1 iff net proceeds at the mark < cost basis; ties are not losses. Cost basis = shares acquired × entry benchmark price (the day-t open) + buy-side explicit charges + modelled buy-side shortfall. Net proceeds at the mark = position value at the mark − sell-side explicit charges − modelled sell-side shortfall, as if the whole position were sold at the mark. O1 is a **valuation at the scheduled exit instant (the opening auction of day t+H)**; it does not say the position could have been sold.
- *Snapshot.* One data snapshot only: the data as of day t + d_label = t+H+1 (§3.3).
- *Step 1 — events and listing status over (t, t+H].* Share-and-cash tracking of the holding state (security j, shares q, cash D): S-a cash dividend to which the position is entitled (D += q × net cash per share); S-b bonus shares / capitalisation / split / consolidation with published multiplier φ > 0 (q := qφ); S-c rights issue (**not subscribed, valued at zero**; q unchanged; a conservative convention, RC-13); S-d removal against cash only on published terms (holding becomes cash); S-e exchange for exactly one listed successor at a published ratio (at most once per entry); **U — anything else, any unclassifiable event, any S-d / S-e event of a successor, or a security no longer listed at day t+H without S-d / S-e ⇒ L^mark := 1, flagged.**
- *Step 2 — price rule at the opening auction of day t+H* (four mutually exclusive cases): **P1** suspended for the auction → last close at which j traded before the suspension, restated to ex-entitlement terms by rule R-P1 of I-§3 (gross cash dividend and share multiplier; **convention P1-R** when the mark day lies after the record date of a rights issue and the stock has not traded since — note (2) below); **P2** auction match → that price, also at a price limit; **P3** no auction match, traded later that day → the official open as published; **P4** no trade at all that day → the day's pre-open reference price. **With daily bars P2 and P3 cannot be told apart; both are implemented as "the published open of day t+H when the stock traded that day", admissible because the published open equals the auction price whenever the auction matched (verified, RC-4; the tag "[rule: tbv, RV3]" of v0.6 was stale — v0.7, E-2).** Tentative value = q·m + D.
- *Step 3 — validation.* The tentative value is accepted only if every input of Steps 1–2 and of the cost model is in the snapshot. **Global override M ("missing input"): if any needed input is absent at any step, L^mark := 1, flagged; M overrides a tentative value.**
- *Cash distributions — by entitlement (I-§5; I-§8.9; RC-12, RC-14; reworded v0.6, V-6).* A distribution is attributed to a position iff the position holds the shares at the **close of the record date**, in the quantity held at that close, **whatever the payment date**. For cash dividends, bonus shares and capitalisations the ex-date is the trading day after the record date (verified, RC-12); rights issues are the exception (RC-13). Amount = announced gross cash per share × (1 − τ), where τ is the tax rate of schedule U41 for the record date. The total is the same whether the tax is withheld at payment or collected at the sale, and the label uses the total. An entitled but unpaid amount is a receivable. O1: record dates from day t to day t+H−1. O2 and the deadline labels: record dates from day t to the day before the sale. Ledger and attribution: the same rule; cash ledger, never a trade; timing of cash and tax in §3.11.
- *Fallback conventions, copied from I-§3 and labelled as what they are.* **"U ⇒ loss", "M ⇒ loss" and "rights not subscribed, valued at zero" are conservative conventions chosen by the theory task, not facts; they turn data problems into recorded losses.** No entry is dropped. No label is revised after day t + d_label. **Reported: the number of entries per branch (S-d, S-e, P1–P4) and, separately, of U and M (A24); if U + M are not rare, every result using O1 states that the label is driven by a convention.** **A23 failed in the smoke test (vendor factors include cash dividends, lack 11 events and have 5 anomalous rows): no vendor adjustment factor and no vendor-adjusted price is used anywhere in labels or accounting (§3.11).** The tree can detect a missing field, not an event missing from the vendor's calendar (A21).
- *Plan-level implementation notes added in v0.6. They do not change the text of I-§3, which governs; the points on which that text should be amended are the S-items of the Revision log.* **(1) P1 restatement** is computed by us from the last close at which the name traded and from the event table: divide by φ, and subtract the **gross (pre-tax)** cash per share, as in the exchange's reference-price formula (RC-5). I-§3 now says the same (rule R-P1 item 1, written in its post-audit sync, log item S-3; v0.6 recorded a difference here that no longer exists). The vendor's carried-forward price on suspended-day rows is not used: in the smoke test it had been adjusted during the suspension in 17 spells and not adjusted in the two rights-issue suspensions. **(2) Mark day inside a rights-issue payment-period suspension (rewritten v0.7, E-1; I-§3 rule R-P1 item 2 governs).** The carried-forward close m is restated by **convention P1-R**: m := min{m, (m + ρ·P_r) / (1 + ρ)}, with P_r the subscription price and ρ the **announced** number of rights shares per existing share, both taken from the issue terms in the snapshot. Nothing is added to q or D (S-c: not subscribed, no cash flow). P1-R applies only in branch P1 and only when c ≤ R ≤ t+H−1 (c = the last day on which the name traded, R = the record date); once the stock has resumed, the mark is the actual ex-rights open (P2 / P3) or the exchange's reference price (P4) and nothing is restated. It is a deliberately low valuation — a convention, not an estimate (I-§3 gives the reasons and the limits). Missing terms give outcome M. d_label = H + 1 is unchanged (I-§4). Entries marked under P1-R are counted separately, as are those among them whose issue later failed (a hindsight tabulation). The ledger's OV uses the same rule (§3.9), so label and ledger agree. v0.6 followed the earlier text of I-§3 (a cum-rights mark) and left the question open as FC-T2; the theory file has since decided it. **(3) Record dates.** For S-a / S-b the record date is the trading day before the ex-date (RC-12; 467 of 467 Eastmoney records in the smoke test). For S-c it is read from the rights-issue table and never derived from the ex-date (RC-13); a rights issue without a record date in the snapshot is outcome M. **(4) An unexplained reference-price step** (list URS of §3.11) inside (t, t+H] is "an event that cannot be classified from the snapshot": outcome U, as I-§3 prescribes. **(5)** P1 marks are reported by the length of the suspension spell (§3.5). **(6)** The tax rate in "net cash per share" is schedule U41.
- *Notes added in v0.7 (rulings on findings of the engine implementation; what I-§3 should state in addition is on the synchronisation list T-1 … T-8 of the Revision log).* **(7) Order of same-day events in R-P1 (E-12).** An S-a / S-b event takes effect at the open of its ex-date, an S-c event at the close of its record date; when the ex-date of the former and the record date of the latter fall on the same day, the dividend / bonus restatement is applied first and P1-R second. **(v0.8, H-4; the engine's correction, confirmed.)** In general the events are applied in the chronological order of their effective instants, not by calendar date: an S-c whose record date is the trading day before an S-a / S-b ex-date (close of day R before the open of day R+1) is applied first. The v0.6 code sorted that case the other way; the v0.7 code sorts by instant and has a hand-computed test (9.25 and 9.1667). **(8) Sell-side charges at the mark (E-13).** "As if the whole position were sold" refers to the security part q·m of the position value; the cash part D bears no charge and no shortfall. After a cash exit (S-d) nothing is sold, so no sell-side charge is deducted. Whether a cash exit carries a tax or fee for the holder was not checked (list of §3.10, item 18); the entries concerned are counted, and the label recomputed with the explicit sell-side charges applied to the cash amount is a pre-specified sensitivity. A recorded dividend-tax liability of a lot is debited when the lot ceases to exist — at its sale, at a cash exit or at a write-off. **(9) Events of a successor (E-14).** After a succession (S-e) effective on day d the entitlement rule applies to the successor as to any holding: its events with record date ≥ d apply; an event of the successor whose ex-date is d itself cannot be classified safely and gives outcome U. *(v0.8, H-6; the engine's reading, confirmed.)* In the ledger that event is not booked for lots that arrived by succession; they keep being valued at market prices with unchanged shares and cash, flagged — the convention used for URS (§3.9). A directly bought lot of the same security books the event normally. **(10) Rights issue with a missing record date (E-15).** Outcome M for every entry in the name whose window could contain the record date: (a) the ex-date e of the issue is known and lies in (t, t + H + 10 market trading days] (10 is a PLACEHOLDER, U58: in the two issues of the smoke test the ex-date was 7 trading days after the record date); or (b) neither date is known and the entry day is on or after the earliest date the record carries (announcement date; if it carries none, every entry in the name). The event table contains implemented issues only (§3.11 rule 10) and every rights issue is hand-checked, so the number of such records at the freeze should be zero; it is reported. **(v0.8, H-3; the engine's default, confirmed; supersedes "every rights issue is hand-checked".)** A rights row with neither a record date nor an ex-date does not enter the event table (it is listed and counted). Case (b) therefore applies only to a row in the table that has lost both dates, which rule 10 excludes, so (b) does not fire in the primary run; the switch `undated_rights="include"` is not used for any result. Backstop: if such an issue was implemented, its ex-rights step of `preclose` has no event, goes to URS and gives outcome U for every window containing it (note (4)). What is lost: convention P1-R cannot be applied to a mark day inside the payment-period suspension of such an issue, because its terms are not in the table (trial data: 0 such rows; §19, KL-3). **(11) Missing rows (E-16).** If a listed name has no row in the bar file on a market trading day (after the repair rule 12 of §3.11), the day is treated as a non-trading day of the name and flagged. Outcome M if the mark day t+H has no row, or if branch P1 would have to carry a close across a day without a row (it cannot then be established that the carried close is the last one). The ledger values the name by P1 on such a day, flagged. **(12) No row or no reference price on the order day (E-17).** P^up cannot be computed; no order is submitted (order-ledger reason "no_ref", counted separately; not part of S_t). The existence of the day-t row, its `preclose` and its ST flag are the only day-t fields that order generation may read; the truncation test A11 treats them as pre-open public information of G_t.
- *Note added in v0.8.* **(13) Events left unconfirmed by the mechanical event table (H-2; §3.11 "Mechanical rule").** An event that is neither confirmed by two sources nor confirmed by the reference price is not booked (the holder receives nothing) and is "an event that cannot be classified from the snapshot": outcome U for every entry whose window (t, t+H] contains its ex-date; in the ledger the name keeps being valued at market prices, flagged (the URS convention). Date-conflict pairs that rule 9 (b) cannot settle mechanically are applied on the step date, with outcome U for the windows that contain a day between the two dates. These are conservative conventions, not facts; their counts and shares are reported with every table that uses O1 or the ledger (§3.11).
- **There is no "O1-close" variant (T7).** It was removed from the theory (I-§7): a close-mark label would need its own price rule, availability assumption and P&L relation. The plan does not supply them and reports no close-marked label.

**(iv) Label O2 — executable liquidation under the liquidation policy LP (I-§6.1).** For entry (t,i) a sell order for the entire remaining quantity of the lot is submitted to the opening auction of day t+H and of every later trading day until the quantity is zero; under SF the day's order is filled in full at that day's open or not at all (single sale; same-day lots of one security: §3.7). L^liq_{ti} = 1 iff total net proceeds of the sale plus entitled distributions minus sell-side charges are strictly below the cost basis. **Completion convention: L^liq := 1 if the lot is never fully liquidated or if a class-U event occurs before it is.** Events during an extended liquidation: the Step 1 classification over the whole period from day t to the liquidation day. The liquidation delay has no deterministic bound: **nothing guarantees A3 for any fixed lag, and Theorem 1 is not applied to O2.** No non-informative-censoring assumption, no resolved-only estimator; unresolved positions at a reporting date are counted and bounded both ways. O1 = O2 for an entry if its scheduled sale is recorded as filled for the whole lot at the valuation price (branch P2) with the same cost basis, charges and distribution rule; the share with O1 ≠ O2 therefore measures non-filled exits **as defined by SF**, not exits blocked in the market (I-§6.2).
- **Deadline (bracket) labels for Corollary 1.3 — `草稿`; conditional on the result surviving review (I-§6.4; I-§8.11).** Deadline D = H + 10 (PLACEHOLDER, U16), d_label^D = d_D = D + 1, fixed in advance. S_{ti} = 1 iff LP has liquidated the **whole** lot at or before the opening auction of day t+D. **Certification** V_{ti} = 1 iff S_{ti} = 1 and the snapshot of day t + d_label^D contains the complete and final record of that liquidation (every sale, every event with its amounts, every entitled distribution, all cost inputs); L^cert = 1{net proceeds < cost basis} from the certified record. **L^lo := V·L^cert, L^hi := 1 − V·(1 − L^cert): a missing or incomplete record gives lower label 0 and upper label 1. The O1 fallback M ("missing ⇒ loss") is not used for these labels.** The interval is computed through a common revealed set J_T (A4-D) with P_T^D = N_T − |J_T|. Assumption CERT (a record that passes the completeness test is complete and final) is not audited; in the backtest the sale record is produced by the simulation, so CERT can fail only through wrong or revised market-data inputs. If the corollary survives review, the interval [max{0,(K^lo_T − B*_T)/N_T}, min{1,(K^hi_T + P_T^D + B*_T)/N_T}] (radius with d_D; exact form as in `theorems.md`, which governs) is reported with (K^hi_T − K^lo_T)/N_T. **No claim is made that this residual width is an identification limit or that it does not shrink.** If the corollary does not survive, only the deterministic counts are reported. **(v0.8, H-11; the engine's reading, confirmed.)** A cash exit (S-d) effective at or before the opening auction of day t+D counts as "the whole lot liquidated" on its effective day: the lot has become cash on published terms and its proceeds are determined. The record is "complete" only if every event of the holding period could be booked; an event with a missing amount, an unclassifiable event, a URS step, an event of a successor on the succession day or an event left to the pending rule of §3.11 makes the record incomplete, so the entry gets (L^lo, L^hi) = (0, 1).

**(v) Which label enters which quantity** (statuses: header; "synthetic stream" = synthetic fills under SF, filtration F_t^syn).

| Quantity | Label / outcome | Theory item |
|---|---|---|
| Monitoring interval, K_T, P_T (synthetic stream) | O1 (tree of I-§3), d_label = d = H + 1 | Theorem 1(a)–(c), Cor. 1.1 (three review rounds, no error reported; post-round-3 revisions not re-reviewed) |
| Deterministic bracket for the realised loss fraction | O1 | Cor. 1.2 (bracket needs A4 only; same status) |
| Width bound P_T <= dM | O1 with A5 (ledger records each frozen label when its snapshot is available; A18) | Cor. 1.1(b) |
| Dependence diagnostic (§9.5) | O1 | Prop. 2 (reference scenario only) |
| Training target of LOSS gates; frozen labels used by online rules | O1 (always defined; includes U / M fallbacks, counted) | — |
| Interval for the O2 target | L^lo, L^hi (certified) | **Cor. 1.3 — `草稿`; conditional on the result surviving review** |
| Deployed wealth, daily returns, attribution terms | Ledger cash flows (LP exits), benchmark-price accounting | Lemma 3, identity only, for the additive statistic (§8.2); wealth reading needs W1–W4 |
| Per-order return ρ of the confirmatory statistic (§8.3) | Round trip under SF and LP (O2 exit); unresolved at the data end: marked, flagged | none (empirical only) |
| Share with O1 ≠ O2; exit-delay distribution; share unsold at deadline | both | Cor. 1.3 (conditional) |

**(vi) Accounting for the monitor (A4, A5; I-§8.10).** K_T = executed entries through T whose O1 label is revealed and equals 1; P_T = executed entries whose O1 label is not revealed. **"Revealed" means that the frozen final value (computed from the snapshot of day t + d_label) is recorded in the ledger; provisional marks are never used, and a recorded label is never corrected.** Pending labels are counted both ways (P_T enters the upper endpoint). Computed from information available at T only (truncation test). The matured-only rate K_T/(N_T − P_T) is covered by no statement and is **not reported as an estimate of anything covered by the theory**; it appears only as the labelled not-valid comparator Cmp-N.

### 3.9 Two-interval accounting and reconciliation (P2; T4)

**Valuation rules at the two instants of a day (rewritten v0.5, M-X1).** Both are per-share values of the security currently held (after the share-and-cash tracking of §3.8 (iii) Step 1), the same for every strategy and reference path.

- **OV — open valuation (covered by the theory files).** The price rule of I-§3 Step 2, applied to every day t and every held name, not only at a scheduled exit: **P1** suspended for the opening auction → the last close at which the name traded before the suspension, restated by rule R-P1 of I-§3, **including convention P1-R after the record date of a rights issue (v0.7, E-1: label and ledger use one rule)**; **P2 / P3** traded on day t → the published daily open (merged with daily bars; admissible by RC-4, verified); **P4** not suspended, no trade all day → the day's pre-open reference price. All trades are booked at OV (which is the published open whenever a synthetic fill exists, by (S1)).
- **CV — closing valuation (this plan's rule; NOT covered by the theory results).** `instantiation_ashare.md` defines valuations at the opening auction only (I-§3, I-§8 item 8) and removed the close-marked label (I-§7). CV exists only so that each day can be cut into an intraday and an overnight interval and a wealth curve can be drawn at closes. Complete rule, four mutually exclusive cases for a held, listed name on day t:
  - **K1** the name traded on day t and the bar has a close → the published **unadjusted close** of day t;
  - **K2** the name did not trade on day t (whole-day suspension, or branch P4) → **CV := OV of day t** (intraday return exactly 0);
  - **K3** the name traded on day t but the close field is missing or non-positive in the file → CV := OV of day t, flagged and counted (a data defect, listed in the audit report);
  - **K4** the name is hit by a class-U event or is no longer listed (not handled by S-d / S-e) → the ledger's terminal-value convention below (U33).
  Corporate actions effective on day t are already reflected in OV of day t (ex-entitlement terms), so nothing is restated between the open and the close of the same day; everything that takes effect between the close of t and the open of t+1 (share multipliers, entitled cash) is in the overnight return. CV is evaluated from the data of day t only and is needed for decisions no earlier than day t+1 (it is in G_{t+1}).
- **What depends on CV, and what does not.** CV moves P&L between the intraday bin of day t and the overnight bin of day t+1 and fixes the exposure at the close; for a position held from one open to the next, the sum of the two bins does not depend on CV. **Depends on CV:** the intraday / overnight cut of the attribution terms, daily close-to-close returns r_t (hence AR, drawdown, Sharpe, CVaR, the volatility state of VT and the invested-fraction state of E-const-dev), the end-of-window mark. **Does not depend on CV:** every fill, entry price, cost, label (O1, O2, deadline labels), the per-order return ρ, θ_sel, ψ_H, ψ_O, **all four cards C1–C4**, and every monitoring quantity. (The capacity cap of (S3) for sells uses the day's reference price `preclose` as a size proxy (v0.7, E-5); that is a G_t-measurable input of the fill rule, not a valuation.)
- **Binding label.** Tables and figures that use CV carry the note "closing valuation used only to cut the additive attribution into intervals; outside the scope of the theory results". **Cross-check without CV (exploratory, §11):** the same attribution on single open-to-open periods (holdings after the auction trade of day t valued at OV_t, return OV_t → OV_{t+1}, costs at the trade), which uses no closing price at all; over the common span from the first to the last opening instant the total yuan P&L of the two versions must agree to the R2 tolerance, and both sets of terms are shown.
- *Why the two-interval scheme was kept rather than replaced by open-to-open periods (researcher's judgement).* Open-to-open periods would need no closing rule and match I-§8 item 8 literally. They were not made primary because (i) `research_question.md` v4 defines the descriptive attribution as the overnight / intraday two-interval one; (ii) wealth curves, drawdown and AR would move to an unconventional open-to-open time base; (iii) the two-interval reconciliation design is what the round-1 plan review asked for (item P2) and what the ARS methodology seat judged "resolved at design level"; (iv) nothing confirmatory depends on the choice. The open-to-open version is therefore the cross-check, not the primary. If the lead prefers the reverse, only descriptive quantities change.

**Intervals.** Each trading day t is split at the opening auction:
- *overnight* interval (close of t-1 → open of t): holdings h_t^{on} = positions carried from the close of t-1, valued at CV_{t−1}; return vector x_t^{on} = total return from CV_{t−1} to OV_t, including share changes effective on day t and cash distributions to which a holder at the close of the record date is entitled (net of the tax of schedule U41; the record date is the trading day before the ex-date for dividends and bonus issues — verified, RC-12 — and is read from the event table for rights issues, RC-13);
- *auction*: all trades of day t are booked at the benchmark price OV_t (the published open); costs c_t (fees, stamp duty, modelled shortfall) are debited here on traded amounts (§3.6 items 2–3, 5);
- *intraday* interval (open → close of t): holdings h_t^{id} = positions after the auction trades, valued at OV_t; x_t^{id} = CV_t / OV_t − 1.
A name that does not trade on day t has x_t^{id} = 0 (K2); a name that trades on neither day t−1 nor day t has zero total return in both intervals. **The same two return vectors and the same benchmark prices serve every strategy and reference** (§3.6 item 1), whether a given name is an entry, a continuing position or an exit in that strategy: an entry appears only in h^{id} of its entry day, an exit only in h^{on} of its exit day. Returns are rebuilt from unadjusted prices and the event table of §3.11, with net cash amounts. **No vendor-adjusted series is used, not even as a cross-check** (A5 / A23 failed). The cross-check at tax rate 0 is made, event by event, against the total return implied by the exchange's reference price (previous close / `preclose`, §3.11); the differences are listed.

**Daily identity.** With zero cash return, W_t − W_{t−1} = (h_t^{on})'x_t^{on} + (h_t^{id})'x_t^{id} − c_t for every strategy (wealth W at closes). Lemma 3 is applied **to each interval separately** (2T periods) and the terms are summed; because the lemma's identity holds for arbitrary holding vectors, this is an application of it, not an extension (the reading is the plan's, not a statement of `theorems.md`; it is enforced by tests R1–R2 and flagged to the lead, §16.2). With a non-zero cash return (robustness run) the ledger is tested against the recursion of §3.6 item 6.

**Terminal and special cases.** Positions open at the end of the window are marked at CV of the last day and reported as "open at end" (how few there should be: §8.3, end-of-window rule). In the **ledger**, a position hit by a class-U event or a delisting not covered by S-d / S-e is valued at its last traded price until the event / delisting date and at **0 thereafter (PLACEHOLDER U33, conservative convention; sensitivity: last traded price)**; free data on post-delisting recoveries are UNVERIFIED (A14, **[rule: tbv, RV8]**). Rights issues: not subscribed, valued at zero, in the ledger as in the label. **(v0.6)** A name on the list URS of §3.11 (unexplained reference-price step) is **not** given the terminal value 0 in the ledger: it keeps being valued at market prices with unchanged shares and cash, and is flagged. U33's zero applies only when the security ceases to be valued (delisting, or an unsupported transformation). This is a ledger convention of the plan; the label follows I-§3 (outcome U).

**Delisting versus merger; lots that arrive by succession (v0.7, E-18, E-14, G-3).** A name is "no longer listed" from the market trading day after its last row in the bar file (the bar source's delisting date is the last day with a row; trial data: 4 of 4 delisted names). **(v0.8, H-16.)** On the 100 delisted names among the 825 read in the engine's dry run, the vendor's `outDate` was the last day with a row for 89 and the first day without one for 11 (all with `outDate` after 2025-05). The definition therefore uses the last row only and never `outDate`; the loader already does so. **Disposition table columns (v0.8, H-7; the engine's reading, confirmed):** an optional `effective_date` for cash exits (if absent: the first day on which the name is no longer listed); for a share exchange without the first trading day of the new shares, the first day on which the absorbed name is no longer listed; a successor without a bar on that day, or incomplete terms ⇒ disposition `other` (class U), listed in the table's error list. Default of §17.4 item 1 (not collected) unchanged; the pilot list contains 109 delisted names (§13A), the ever-members among them are fewer. What then happens to a holding is read from the **delisting disposition table** of §3.11 rule 11: termination of listing ⇒ class U (label 1; ledger value 0, U33); cash-only exit on published terms ⇒ S-d; absorption by share exchange into exactly one SSE / SZSE A-share at a published ratio ⇒ S-e (the holder is assumed not to exercise a cash option); anything else, and **any name without a row in the table**, ⇒ class U. Between the last trading day of the absorbed name and the first row of the successor's shares the ledger carries the holding at the last traded value of the absorbed name (q × last close; flagged) — a ledger convention of this plan; the label follows I-§3 as written, where a successor that is not listed on day t+H gives outcome U. Lots that arrive by succession are carried in the attribution track (A) under a separate security identifier (the successor's code with a succession mark), with the successor's returns until an event that the tree treats differently for them (a further S-d / S-e of the successor is class U for them: "at most one succession"). The identifier is the same for every strategy and reference path, so Lemma 3 applies unchanged.

**Reconciliation — acceptance criteria (PLACEHOLDER tolerances).** Two computations are implemented in separate modules: (L) a **share ledger** (share counts × unadjusted prices + cash; dividends, splits and rights as explicit events); (A) the **return-based attribution track** above.
- **R1 (algebra).** Lemma 3 residual per interval and in total <= 1e-10 × W_0. A non-zero residual is a code error.
- **R2 (ledger).** For every strategy and day, |ΔW^{(A)}_t − ΔW^{(L)}_t| <= 1e-6 × W_{t−1}; cumulated over the window <= 1e-4 × W_0.
- **R3 (explanation).** Every day exceeding the daily tolerance is listed and traced to a specific event; an unexplained excess is a failure.
- **R4 (stress cases on constructed price panels, expected values computed by hand in the test file).**
  - **No-trade cases added in v0.4 (T4): (a) a position held unchanged across a cash-dividend record / ex-date; (b) a position held unchanged across a 2-for-1 split (share multiplier 2, price halves). In both, for every strategy and for the reference path h^E, the traded amount must be exactly 0 and the cost exactly 0 (v0.7, E-8: bitwise zero for every real strategy, whose trades are computed in share units; for the reference path h^E, which is defined in yuan and converted to shares, |traded amount| ≤ 1e-10 × W_0, the tolerance of R1); in (a) the cash ledger increases by q × net cash per share; in (b) q doubles and wealth is unchanged.** Also: (c) an unchanged position through a pure price move (traded amount 0).
  - **"Every cost once" (added v0.4):** one buy and one sell with non-zero shortfall — the wealth change equals the benchmark-price P&L minus fees minus shortfall, with the shortfall appearing once (not also in the entry or exit price).
  - **Non-zero cash return with costs debited at the trade (added v0.4; the round-3 counterexample):** initial cash 100, cost 1 debited at the start of the period, cash return 10%, no risky holding ⇒ end wealth 108.9 (not 109).
  - **Closing-valuation cases (added v0.5, M-X1):** a held name that does not trade on a day (K2: intraday return exactly 0, next overnight return carries the whole move); a traded day with a missing close (K3: flagged, counted); a 2-for-1 split effective on day t for a name suspended on day t; and a check that replacing every CV by an arbitrary positive number leaves the total P&L of a position held from one open to a later open unchanged.
  - **Rule-calendar cases (added v0.6):** a cash dividend with record date before 2013-01-01, between 2013-01-01 and 2015-09-07, and from 2015-09-08 (wealth identical to "net amount at the ex-date" in all three; spendable cash and the tax liability as in §3.11); a mark day inside a rights-issue payment-period suspension (branch P1 with convention P1-R: expected per-share value min{m, (m + ρ·P_r) / (1 + ρ)}, no cash flow, counted; rewritten v0.7, E-1); an SSE trade before 2015-08-01 (per-share transfer fee) and an SZSE trade on the same day; a trade on 2023-08-25 and on 2023-08-28 (stamp duty); limit prices at the rounding boundary and for a reference price below 0.10 yuan (± 0.01 rule); a ChiNext ST name on 2020-08-21 and 2020-08-24; a STAR Market order of 150 and of 250 shares in the lot-rounding run; an open above the computed P^up (not filled under SF and SF-strict, not filled under SF-loose).
  - **Cases added in v0.7:** a sell on the first day after a suspension of more than 20 trading days (filled under the primary cap; not filled under "cap-zero"); a buy and a sell of one name by one strategy on one day (costs on both orders, net traded amount 0, non-netting amount reported); an order in a run with the 5-yuan minimum commission on either side of the point where the minimum binds, and a slot too small to pay it; SF-strict fills as a subset of SF fills; a record date of 2015-09-08 under both readings of the 2015 notice (wealth, labels and cash net of liabilities identical); a dividend ex-date and a rights record date on the same day inside a suspension (order of note (7)); a cash exit (no sell-side charge; liability debited); a succession followed by an event of the successor, with a directly bought lot of the same successor held at the same time; a missing row on the mark day and inside a P1 carry (outcome M); a delisting with and without a row in the disposition table (§3.11 rule 11); an ex-date inside a suspension with the reference-price step at the resumption (§3.11 rule 9).
  - Rights issue (not subscribed, zero value); suspension spanning the scheduled exit (P1); limit-down sequence with non-filled exits; delisting / class-U event; a label hitting outcome M (label 1, flagged); **the same stock entered by one strategy and exited by another on the same day**; two lots of one name due for sale on the same day (§3.7); a stock held by one strategy only; positions open at the window end; a day with zero baseline exposure.
- R1–R4 must pass in `experiments/src/engine/tests` (constructed panels; v0.7, E-20) and again on the pilot panel (A20) before any experiment that reports attribution.
- **Cohort-level statement about PERF, qualified.** "PERF's cohort-level composition term is zero" is asserted by a test **only under its conditions**: the cohort's holdings in PERF and in the baseline are proportional over the cohort's whole life (same names filled, same exit dates). The test first checks proportionality; where it fails, a non-zero term is reported and explained, not classified as a bug.

### 3.10 Rule calendar (new in v0.6, V-1; source: `experiments/rule_verification.md`, sections A–F)

**Purpose.** The backtest applies, on each day, the rule in force on that day. The calendar is implemented as one data file (`experiments/src/engine/rule_calendar.py` — v0.7, E-20, F-20: **one** calendar, read by the engine and by the feature library; a second copy of a band, threshold or regime date anywhere in the code is a defect — rows with a start date, an end date and a value), read by the conventions module, tested at every boundary date (R4 of §3.9), and hashed into the freeze manifest. Intervals are stated for the raw-data period 2008-01-01 to 2026-06-30; no trade is simulated before 2010-01-01.

**Source status — fixed vocabulary.** *verified* = the official rule text was opened and read by the author of `rule_verification.md`; *official page* = an official site's news item, press Q&A or investor-education page, not the rule text; *indirect* = known only through a reference in another official document that was opened; **unverified against official text** = only a search summary or a repost was read, or nothing was found. Every row marked **unverified against official text**, in whole or in part, is on the list at the end of this section. Nothing in this section was checked by me; it is transcribed from that report.

The exchanges' 2017 implementing rules and the 2007 rule on directors' holdings: not opened |

**Items that are unverified against official text — to be re-checked before the freeze (FC-V2 of §18), or else accepted as stated limitations with the conservative value shown.** Priority "must": a number or date of the calendar that applies inside 2010-01-01 – 2026-06-30 depends on it.

| # | Item | Row | Priority | If it cannot be verified |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Announcement of the 2023 stamp-duty halving (original page) | RC-15 | must | Keep 2023-08-28 (several official reposts agree); state the source |
| 2 | Clearing-house notice of 2022-04-28 on the transfer fee | RC-16 | must | Keep 2022-04-29; if no source is found, use 0.02‰ throughout (the higher rate) |
| 3 | Transfer fee before 2015-08-01: SSE par-value rates and their dates, SZSE rates, par value per name | RC-16 | must | Highest rate found (0.375‰ of a par value of 1 yuan; 0.0255‰ of value), logged |
| 4 | Dividend-tax rule in force before 2013 | RC-14 | must | Use 20% throughout (the higher rate), logged |
| 5 | Rights issues: suspension during the payment period, ex-date timing, non-transferability | RC-13 | must | Record and ex-dates from the data only; every rights event hand-checked (A16) |
| 6 | Block-trade disclosure clause for the SSE before the 2023 text and for the SZSE before the 2013 text | RC-19 | must (it sets the BLK lag) | BLK lag = 2 (§5.6.2) |
| 7 | SSE notice of 2021 on the Dragon–Tiger list: text and effective date; the SZSE revision that changed 3 to 5; the ChiNext implementing document | RC-18 | must (dates of regime indicators) | Indicator dates as in RC-18, marked unverified; LHB lag is 2 in any case |
| 8 | Effective date of the 2023 main-board registration rules | RC-6 (f), (g)(1) | should | Not needed by SF (§3.4); only the ex-post classification of A7 uses it |
| 9 | STAR Market launch date | RC-6 (e) | should | STAR names enter with their first bar in the data |
| 10 | Delisting rules of 2012 and 2014; the SZSE Listing Rules' delisting chapter; bands in the arrangement period for ChiNext and STAR | RC-10, RC-6 (h) | should | Exclusion proxy U55 keeps such names out of new entries; held names are sold by LP |
| 11 | Early routine one-hour suspensions; maximum suspension durations; the Suspension Rules | RC-4, RC-9 | should | Stated limitation (such days cannot be identified in the data anyway) |
| 12 | SSE Trading Rules of 2006 and the 2020 revision; official text of the SZSE 2023 revision | RC-1 – RC-5 | should | "Unchanged" is stated as inferred for those versions |
| 13 | Date of the SSE change of bonus-share listing from R+2 to R+1; tax on bonus shares | RC-12, RC-14 | may | Not modelled; stated limitation |
| 14 | Share-reform resumptions and other no-limit cases | RC-6 (g)(5) | may | Not needed by SF |
| 15 | The exchanges' 2017 reduction rules; the 2007 rule on directors' holdings; disclosure before 2015-07-08 | RC-20 | may | Regime dates from the CSRC documents |
| 16 | Same-auction funding | RC-22 | may | Not relied on |
| 17 | Whether the 2015 dividend-tax notice applies to a record date of 2015-09-08 itself ("after" that day in the wording quoted in `rule_verification.md` B2; "from" that day in RC-14) | RC-14 | may | Kept as "from 2015-09-08" (v0.7, E-9). Only the split between cash paid and tax liability for that one record date depends on it; wealth, labels and order sizing (cash net of liabilities) do not |
| 18 | Whether a cash exit (tender offer, cash merger) carries stamp duty, transfer fee or commission for the holder | S-d of §3.8 | may | No sell-side charge on the cash amount; sensitivity with the explicit charges (v0.7, E-13) |
| 19 | Effective date of the SZSE rule text of November 2013 (the boundary of the top-5 indicator); why the upstream listing-reason wording shows "top three" and "top five" side by side for SZSE names from 2011-02 to 2014-12 (trial files) | RC-18 | must (belongs to item 7) | Indicator "unknown" over the uncertain intervals (§5.6.2a; v0.7, F-13) |
| 20 | Which index the "deviation" criterion of the Dragon–Tiger list refers to in each year ("the corresponding classification index") | RC-18 | should | Board proxy indices in R_O, labelled a proxy (§5.4) |
| 21 | First day on which a main-board new listing traded without a price limit under the 2023 registration rules (the feature library carried 2023-04-10, written from memory) | RC-6 (f) | should (same date as item 8) | Not needed: limit-count controls are NaN while their window contains any of a name's first 5 trading days (v0.7, F-20). **(v0.8, H-13)** The rows `ipo_nolimit5:*` stay in the hashed calendar, marked with this item; no computation of engine or features may read them (none does at v0.7) |
| 22 | Three-day cumulative deviation threshold of the Dragon–Tiger list (20% / 30%) and its board regimes (new in v0.8, J-1) | RC-18 | should | Values as in RC-18; they set a control feature only (no fill, label or cost) |
| 23 | That a block trade in a stock with a price limit must be priced inside the day's limits (basis of the plausibility rule of §5.6.3; new in v0.8, J-7) | RC-19 | should | The rule is applied as a data-quality filter fixed in advance whether or not the clause is verified; the count of excluded rows is reported |

**Archive.** Three SZSE Trading Rules PDFs (2013, 2021, 2026 versions) are stored in `literature/pdfs/`. The SSE rules (.doc / .docx), and the CSRC, Ministry of Finance and tax-administration pages, are not archived (the download script accepts https PDFs only); their URLs are in `rule_verification.md`. Archiving them by hand is item FC-V3.

### 3.11 Event table, bookkeeping of distributions, feature-level adjustment (new in v0.6, A-2, A-3, V-6)

**Principle.** Labels and the ledger never read a vendor adjustment factor or a vendor-adjusted price. They track **shares and cash explicitly** — trade_t = (s_t − s_{t−}) ∘ p_t at unadjusted benchmark prices, share multipliers applied to s, cash distributions booked in the cash ledger (§3.6 items 3–4; I-§8 item 8). Reason (A5, A23): the BaoStock and Sina factors both contain cash dividends (reinvested at the reference price), so a vendor-adjusted price is a total-return index and not an ex-distribution price; BaoStock's factor table lacks the 11 dividends of one code-change name before 2018-12-26 and has 5 rows with a factor step on a day without any event; the Eastmoney adjusted prices could not be tested.

**Corporate-event table — sources.**

| Source | Interface | Has | Lacks (smoke test) |
|---|---|---|---|
| EV1 | Eastmoney `stock_fhps_detail_em` (AKShare) | plan and latest announcement dates, record date, ex-date, cash per 10 shares, bonus / capitalisation ratios | pay date; rights issues; **delisted names (empty for 12 of 12)**; special and interim dividends in several cases |
| EV2 | Sina `stock_history_dividend_detail` (AKShare) | announcement date, record date, ex-date, listing date of bonus shares; a rights table with price, ratio, record date, payment period, ex-date, listing date; delisted names (3 of 3) | pay date |
| EV3 | BaoStock `query_dividend_data` | four announcement dates, record date, ex-date, **pay date**, cash per share before and after tax | rights issues (no field seen); tested on 6 queries only; delisted names untested |
| EV4 | Backstop computed by us: every BaoStock row whose `preclose` differs from the close of the name's previous row | completeness: it sees every adjustment the exchange made to the reference price | what the event was |

**Merge and acceptance rules (PLACEHOLDER tolerances, U56).**
1. Events are keyed by (code, ex-date); codes are mapped through a code-change table (OA9). **(v0.8, H-15; the engine's reading, confirmed.)** No code-change table exists. Each code of the bar file is its own security and events are keyed by the bar-file code; an event that a source files under another code is not found, and its `preclose` step goes to URS (outcome U). OA9 reports the names concerned; no mapping is added before the freeze (§19, KL-2).
2. A cash dividend / bonus / capitalisation event is **confirmed** if at least two of EV1–EV3 agree on the cash per share (within 0.0001 yuan), the share ratio and the record date. **(v0.8, H-9; confirmed.)** Share ratios agree within 1e-6. An EV3 row (no status field) with an ex-date counts as implemented. Two rows of one source with the same (code, ex-date), identical or not, make that (code, ex-date) ineligible for confirmation under rule 2 and rule 2a; it falls to the pending rule and is counted (trial: 1 of 241).
2a. **Confirmation by the reference price (new in v0.8, H-2).** A (code, ex-date) not confirmed under rule 2 — one source only, or sources that disagree — is **confirmed by the reference price** if exactly one of the source values implies, by RC-5 (dividend and share ratio, no rights term), a reference price that agrees with the `preclose` of the ex-date within 0.005 yuan (the flag tolerance of U56), the step is on the ex-date row (or, for an ex-date inside a suspension, on the name's first traded day after it, as in rule 9 (a)), and for S-a / S-b the record date is the trading day before the ex-date. The event is booked with that source's values and flagged "single source / disagreement, confirmed by the reference price". The bar source is the independent second source here, as it already governs the day of the price change (U61).
3. ~~An event found in one source only, or on which the sources disagree, is checked by hand~~ **(v0.8, H-2: replaced.)** An event that is confirmed neither under rule 2 nor under rule 2a falls to the **pending rule** (the engine's conservative rule, confirmed): it is not booked, and the windows containing its ex-date receive outcome U (§3.8 (iii) note (13)); in the ledger the name is valued at market prices, flagged. The same applies to confirmed events whose implied reference price differs from `preclose` by more than 0.02 yuan (rule 6) and to violations of the record-date relation (rule 10). The engine still writes the **hand-check list** (trial: 39 items, of which 8 rights issues); it is a diagnostic list, reported in `AUDIT.md`, and nothing on it is acted on in the primary run.
4. ~~Every EV4 step without a matching event in EV1–EV3 is resolved by hand~~ **(v0.8, H-2: replaced.)** Every EV4 step without a matching event in EV1–EV3 or the rights table, and not matched under rule 9 (a), goes on the list **URS** with its date (smoke test: 2 of 524); the label rule for it is §3.8 (iii) note (4), the ledger rule is in §3.9.
5. **Rights issues** come from the Sina rights table only. ~~Every rights issue … is hand-checked (A16).~~ **(v0.8, H-2, H-3.)** A rights row with a record date or an ex-date enters the table mechanically, with the price and the announced ratio as recorded, flagged "not hand-checked"; its ex-date step is explained by it whatever the amount (the realised ratio is unknown, so no amount check is made). A row with neither date is listed and does not enter (note (10)). Hand-checking rights issues (A16) is a diagnostic sample of the audit, not an input of the table.
6. **Cash amounts.** The amount booked is the announced gross cash per share of the confirmed event. As a consistency check, the reference price implied by the event (RC-5) is compared with `preclose`: a difference above 0.005 yuan is flagged and listed; above 0.02 yuan the event is hand-checked. (Smoke test: 438 of 467 events within half a cent, 17 off by one to two cents, 12 by more — four of those with the ex-date inside a suspension, the others with an Eastmoney amount that does not match the reference price.) The announced amount is not replaced by a value backed out of `preclose`: the issuer may have used a different formula (RC-5), and the holder receives the announced amount.
7. An event whose ex-date falls inside a suspension takes effect in the share-and-cash state on its ex-date, whenever the vendor's carried-forward row is adjusted.
8. The table is frozen and hashed with the first snapshot; the numbers of confirmed, hand-checked and URS events, by year and by listed / delisted status, are reported in `AUDIT.md` (A21).
9. **Date conflicts between an event source and the bar source (v0.7, G-1).** Trial dry run, one event source: 241 of 247 `preclose` steps had a same-day event; for 3 events the source's ex-date carries no step while an unexplained step of the same name lies on a later date; 1 step has no event; 2 steps were caused by missing rows. Pre-set precedence, applied mechanically: **the bar source governs the day on which prices change** (every price of the ledger comes from it); **the confirmed announcement governs amounts, ratios and the record date.** (a) *Ex-date inside a suspension.* If the source's ex-date e is a day on which the name did not trade, and the first unexplained EV4 step at or after e lies on the name's first traded day after e, and the step agrees with the reference price implied by the event (RC-5) within the hand-check tolerance of rule 6, the two are matched: the share-and-cash state changes on e (rule 7; R-P1 restates the carried price from e) and the step is recorded as explained. (b) *Any other pair* — an event whose ex-date is a traded day without a step, and an unexplained step of the same name within 250 market trading days that agrees with the event within the same tolerance (U61). **(v0.8, H-2: no hand check in the primary run, so every such pair is treated as "cannot decide".)** ~~After the hand check of rule 3: if the announcement confirms e, the event is kept and the step goes to URS; if it confirms the step date, the event is re-dated;~~ **the event is applied on the step date** (record date := the trading day before it, for S-a / S-b), flagged "date conflict", and every entry in the name whose window (t, t+H] contains a day between the two dates (inclusive) receives outcome U. (c) An event with no step anywhere and no confirming announcement is not booked (the holder receives nothing) and gives outcome U for entries whose window contains its ex-date. All three kinds are counted by year and listed in `AUDIT.md`. Nothing is decided case by case beyond reading the announcement.
10. **Implemented events only (v0.7, G-2, E-11).** A row of EV1–EV3 enters the table only if its status field says that the plan was implemented **and** it has an ex-date; rows with an ex-date but another status, and rights-issue rows without dates, go to the hand check. (The trial loader used "ex-date not empty" alone; on the trial files the two filters select the same rows.) For S-a / S-b the record date must be the trading day before the ex-date (RC-12); a confirmed event for which this fails is hand-checked and, if the announcement itself shows another relation, is "an event that cannot be classified" (outcome U; ledger as for URS). Trial data: 0 of 241. **(v0.8, H-2, H-3.)** Without a hand check: rows with an ex-date but another status, rows with status "implemented" but no ex-date (trial: one, `sz.000792`, 2020-03-25) and undated rights rows do not enter the table and are listed; a confirmed event that violates the record-date relation falls to the pending rule. If such an event was real, its `preclose` step is caught by URS.
11. **Delisting disposition table (v0.7, E-18).** The free scripted sources carry no record of cash exits or share-exchange absorptions; without further input every delisting falls into class U and is written off, which is wrong for an absorption by share exchange. For every name that left the bar file before 2026-06-30 and was ever a member of the universe, the table has one row: disposition ∈ {termination of listing; cash-only exit (cash per share, effective day); share exchange into one SSE / SZSE A-share (successor code, ratio, last trading day of the absorbed name, first trading day of the new shares); other}, with the URL of the delisting or merger announcement on the statutory disclosure platform. The rows are collected by hand; because this needs requests outside the scripted pull it is referred to the user (§17.4 item 1). **Fallback fixed now, applied to every name without a row:** class U (label 1, ledger value 0); the names and the numbers of entries and lots concerned are listed; and every table that uses the ledger or O1 carries the sentence "delistings are written off, including absorptions by share exchange". The U33 sensitivity "last traded price" is reported in either case.
12. **Missing rows of the bar file (v0.7, F-18, E-16).** A market trading day on which a listed name has no row (trial data: one day for each of two code-change names, on which the next row's `preclose` shows that the name traded) is first re-queried from the bar source; if the row is still absent, the cross-check source is queried for that name and day. If it shows a traded bar, the row is inserted from it with the flag "filled from the cross-check source" (`preclose` := the previous close unless an event is effective that day; turnover := volume ÷ the float implied by the name's previous traded row, i.e. that row's volume ÷ turnover). If neither source has a traded bar, the day stays a non-trading day of the name. No other filling is done, and no value is carried forward into a missing row. Inserted rows are listed in `AUDIT.md`; the engine and the feature panel carry the flag. The queries are made by the fetch pipeline within its budget, never by the engine or the feature library.

**Mechanical rule — the event table needs no hand work (new in v0.8, H-2; principle 11).** v0.6 / v0.7 prescribed what to do *after* hand checks, not what the table contains *before* them; the engine's trial run left 26 of 241 events (10.8%) unbooked pending a check and listed 39 hand-check items (engine report §8.3). The hand checks need requests to the statutory disclosure platform outside the approved budget, and a table that depends on which checks happen to be done would make the backtest depend on discretionary work. Therefore:
1. **The primary event table is a deterministic function of the snapshot**: rules 1, 2, 2a, the pending rule of rule 3, rules 4 – 12, with no resolution input (`event_table.build(..., resolutions=None, pending="U")`). It is built once, frozen and hashed with Freeze-1.
2. Why rule 2a was added rather than the pending rule alone: in the free data the Eastmoney table is empty for delisted names (12 of 12 in the smoke test), so a pending rule alone would make single-source events — and with them class U, hence O1 = 1, the training target of the LOSS gates — depend systematically on a name's data coverage (principle 5). The exchange's reference price confirms the existence and the amount of an S-a / S-b event independently of the event sources (principle 6).
3. **Sensitivities (exploratory; never C1–C4):** (a) "provisional booking" — unconfirmed events booked with the first available source in the order EV1, EV2, EV3 (`pending="provisional"`, as implemented), reported beside the counts of U and the ledger descriptives; (b) "with hand checks" — only if hand checks are carried out before Freeze-1 for the whole hand-check list (not a selection), their results in a hashed CSV (columns: code, date, kind ∈ {event, step}, status ∈ {announcement, unclassifiable, reject, explained}, gross cash per share, share ratio, record date, date choice ∈ {event, step, undecided}, URL); otherwise not run.
4. **Reported, by year and by listed / delisted status (`AUDIT.md`, and beside every table that uses O1 or the ledger):** numbers and shares of events confirmed by three sources, by two, by the reference price (rule 2a), left to the pending rule (by reason: single source, disagreement, duplicate rows, amount, record date, no step), rights rows in the table and listed; URS steps (and those after a missing row); date-conflict spans; the share of O1 labels that are U because of an event-table rule, and of executed entries whose window contains a pending-rule event.

**Cash and tax of a cash dividend in the ledger (rewrites the "withholding" of v0.5; RC-14).** g = gross cash per share, q = shares held at the close of the record date, τ = schedule U41 (10% for record dates before 2013-01-01, 20% from then).
- *Wealth.* At the open of the ex-date (in the overnight interval of that day) the position's wealth changes by q·g·(1 − τ), booked as a receivable. Wealth and every label are therefore identical to "net amount received at the ex-date", in every regime.
- *Spendable cash.* On the pay date (EV3; if absent, the ex-date, i.e. R+1) the sleeve receives the amount actually paid: record date before 2013-01-01 — q·g·(1 − 0.10) (that the 10% was withheld at payment is assumed, not checked); record date from 2013-01-01 to 2015-09-07 — q·g·(1 − 0.05), and a tax liability of q·g·(τ − 0.05) is recorded; record date from 2015-09-08 — q·g in full, and a tax liability of q·g·τ is recorded (the 2015 notice applies to payments whose record date falls after its effective date; the treatment of 2015-09-08 itself was not checked — **v0.7, E-9:** kept as written here, "record date from 2015-09-08", and entered as item 17 of the list in §3.10. `rule_verification.md` B2 quotes the notice as applying to record dates "after" that day; only the split between cash paid and liability for that single record date depends on the reading, because wealth and labels use the total and order sizing uses cash net of liabilities).
- *The liability is debited from the sleeve's cash on the day the lot is sold.* Order sizing uses the sleeve's cash net of outstanding liabilities, so the money owed in tax is never spent. (v0.7, E-13: a lot that ceases to exist without a sale — cash exit, write-off — has its liability debited on that day.) A lot unsold at the end of the window keeps its liability.
- *Not modelled, stated:* the actual holding-period brackets (entries with H = 20 and delayed liquidations may in reality pay 10%; a position held more than a year, nothing); first-in-first-out across lots of one name in one account; tax on bonus shares (not checked); the earlier SSE practice of bonus shares becoming tradable on R+2. Sensitivity: τ = 0 and τ = 10% flat (§11).
