**Feature-level adjustment (features only; never accounting).** Price-based features (B0, R, Z_H) need a price series that is continuous across ex-dates. It is built by us from the bar source alone:

  F_t = Π_{s ≤ t} (close_{s−1} / preclose_s), over all rows of the name (suspended-day rows included), with F = 1 on the name's first row;  adjusted price_t = unadjusted price_t × F_t.

The factor of day t uses `preclose` of day t, which is pre-open public information of day t; features for the day-t decision use adjusted prices through t−1 only. The series is anchored at the name's first row, so a value once computed never changes when a later event occurs (a forward-anchored series would rewrite its own past and is not used). The vendor's factor tables and adjusted prices are not used.

**Limits of this choice, to be stated wherever the features are described.**
1. The series is the **total-return index implied by the exchange's reference prices**: cash dividends enter gross of tax and as if reinvested at the reference price; a rights issue enters as if subscribed. It is not an ex-distribution price series and it is not the wealth of a holder under the conventions of §3.8 (no tax, S-c not subscribed). Features that are read as "the price holders paid" (capital-gains overhang, chip distributions; §5.1–5.2) are in these total-return units.
2. It inherits every error of the `preclose` field: the two unexplained steps and the one bad row found in the smoke test would enter the features of those names; URS dates are flagged in the feature panel.
3. Where the issuer obtained a different reference-price formula, the factor follows the exchange's price, not the announced amounts.
4. The vendor's carried-forward rows are adjusted during a suspension for dividends and bonus issues but were not for the two rights issues inspected; the day on which the factor steps (inside the suspension or at the resumption) can therefore differ by event type. The cumulative factor after the resumption is the same; features computed on suspended days of that spell are affected.
5. Close-based features of SSE names change meaning on 2018-08-20 (RC-8).
6. These limits apply identically to every strategy, gate and comparator. They change what a feature measures; they do not put future information into it (truncation test A11).
7. Not checked: agreement of this factor with a vendor's total-return factor beyond the smoke test's event-level comparison (BaoStock: 503 of 508 factor steps within 1e-3 of previous close / `preclose`).
8. **(v0.7, G-5.)** A feature value may not depend on how much history precedes its window. Rolling operators compute each window on its own and without BLAS matrix products (the look-ahead test of the feature library found last-bit differences with them), so that the truncation test A11 can require bit-identical values. This is a property of the code, tested there; it is not a statement about information.
9. **(v0.7, F-19.)** The flag for URS dates (limit 2) is a diagnostic column of the feature panel, never a model input; it can be built only when the list URS exists (FC-A3). No feature-level treatment is made for limit 5.

---

## 4. Base strategy

### 4.1 Features (implemented by us in pandas / numpy; **Qlib is not required at run time**)
- **Price inputs (v0.6, A-2).** Every price-based feature (B0, R, Z_H) uses the feature-level adjusted series of §3.11: our own factor built from `preclose`, a total-return index and not an ex-distribution price (limits listed there). Unadjusted prices are used where a feature needs them (distance to a limit price, traded amount). No vendor-adjusted price is read. Turnover is BaoStock's `turn` — in percent of float shares (A10) — divided by 100.
- **B0 — Alpha158-style price–volume features**, re-implemented from the public definitions in Qlib's `qlib/contrib/data/handler.py` (MIT; reference only, on `DOWNLOAD_LIST.md`). Families: candlestick shape ratios (KMID, KLEN, KUP, KLOW, ...), and rolling statistics over windows {5, 10, 20, 30, 60} of return (ROC), moving average ratio (MA), volatility (STD), regression slope / R-squared / residual (BETA, RSQR, RESI), rolling max / min / quantiles / rank, RSV, price–volume correlations (CORR, CORD), up / down day counts and sums (CNTP, SUMP, ...), and volume analogues (VMA, VSTD, WVMA, VSUMP, ...). The exact list is fixed in `experiments/src/features/registry.py` (158 columns; catalogue `experiments/results/feature_tests/feature_catalog.csv`) before training (v0.7; v0.6 named `experiments/src/base_strategy/features.md`). **(v0.7, F-16.)** Deviation from Qlib, declared: Qlib leaves suspended days missing and evaluates windows with `min_periods=1`; here suspended-day rows stay in the window (flat price, zero volume), only full windows are evaluated, and every B0 feature is NaN when the name did not trade on t−1. Consequence: features built on the ratio of a day's volume to the previous day's (CORD) are NaN for every window that contains the first traded day after a suspension; their missing rate is reported with the diagnostics of §5.5. No value is filled. Agreement with Qlib's own output cannot be tested locally (pyqlib has no Python 3.14 wheel); it is checked only by unit tests against hand-computed values. This is a stated limitation, not a claim of exact Alpha158 replication.
- **R — raw ingredients of Z** (§5.4). Always included whenever Z is excluded, so that "without Z" never means "without the price history and turnover Z is built from".
- Cross-sectional preprocessing per day: winsorise at the 1st / 99th percentile, then rank-transform or z-score (one choice, fixed before training). **(v0.8, U-PILOT.)** In the pilot round the cross-section is the names of the pilot list in the day's training or scoring population (§13A); percentiles and ranks are quantities within that list.
- **Storage of the feature panel (v0.8, J-13).** The panel of the names in use (pilot round: the 1,000 names of the pilot list) is written by `market_panel.build_market_panel` to `experiments/data/derived/feature_panel/` (one parquet per stock, `manifest.csv`, `panel_meta.json` with the catalogue hash, the reason-table hash and the conventions hash), never under `experiments/data/raw/`. Pilot size by the trial's rows: about 3.3 million rows (ESTIMATE; 65,515 rows for 20 names × 50), fewer after the cut at 2021-12-31 (§13A). Missing values: left as NaN for LightGBM; median-imputed plus a missing indicator for the linear model.
- Fundamentals (PE, PB) from BaoStock are **not** used in the primary run (point-in-time validity UNVERIFIED).

### 4.2 Target and models
- Target: the H-day forward return under the execution convention of §3 (open of t to open of t+H), cross-sectionally demeaned and ranked per day.
- **Model 1 (primary): LightGBM** regression on the cross-sectional rank target; LambdaRank variant only as a robustness check. CPU only.
- **Model 2: regularised linear model** (ridge on rank-transformed features; elastic net as part of its tuning budget). Purpose: show whether conclusions depend on the base learner.
- The base strategy takes the top-K by predicted score among tradable candidates each day (§3.7).

### 4.3 Frozen annual deployment algorithm (rewritten: P6)

One algorithm, written here, is used unchanged in the development evaluation, in the null simulation (§10.2) and in the hold-out. It separates four kinds of quantity.

| Quantity | When it is set | What it may use |
|---|---|---|
| **Hyper-parameters** (all of them: tree / regularisation parameters, number of boosting rounds, learning rate, EWMA half-lives, filter look-backs, feature preprocessing choices) | **Once**, in the tuning stage, then frozen for every later year including the hold-out | Tuning years only (below). **No early stopping at deployment**: the number of rounds is a frozen hyper-parameter |
| **Model parameters** (fit) | Refit at the start of each calendar year Y | Expanding window of samples whose label windows end before the cut-off; purge + embargo of H + 1 trading days |
| **Calibration maps** (isotonic map of CONF; probability calibration of classifiers) | Start of each year Y | The deployed model's out-of-sample scores on year Y−1 with **matured** labels only ("matured" throughout §4 and §6 = the label's frozen value, computed from the snapshot of entry day + d_label, is recorded by the time it is used, §3.3; a frozen label is used as recorded and never recomputed) |
| **Thresholds** (to reach target coverage c) | Start of each year Y; not adjusted within the year | Quantile of the deployed model's out-of-sample scores on the nominated names of year Y−1 (uses no labels) |
| **Online states** (PERF's EWMA, volatility estimates of VT / VOL / TREND) | Daily, by their fixed update rule | Data through the close of t−1; labels matured by then |

**Schedule (PLACEHOLDER years, §17 U7).**
1. *Base predictor.* Tuning: for v in {2011, 2012}, fit on data through v−1, score year v; choose the configuration by mean validation RankIC; freeze. Deployment: for each Y >= 2013, fit on data through Y−1 with the frozen configuration, predict Y. Base scores are converted to within-day ranks before any gate uses them, so that scores of different annual models are comparable.
2. *Gates and learned filters.* For deployment year Y: **fit on base out-of-sample data through Y−2; calibrate and set thresholds on year Y−1 (out of sample for that fit); deploy over Y.** Tuning: validation years v in {2015, 2016} (fit 2013..v−2, calibrate v−1, evaluate v); one configuration per method by the common criterion of §6.3; freeze.
3. *Development evaluation years:* 2017–2021. *Hold-out:* 2022-01 to 2026-06. In the hold-out the same refit / calibration / threshold updates continue; e.g. the 2024 thresholds use the scores of 2023 and the 2024 calibration uses 2023 labels that have matured. **That use of past hold-out-period labels is part of the frozen algorithm and involves no choice by a person; no hyper-parameter, search space, feature list or rule is changed after the freeze.** This removes the contradiction in v0.2 between "validate on Y−1" and "no re-tuning in the hold-out".
4. The tuning years 2015–2016 are reported separately and labelled "tuning years (selection-biased)"; they enter no development estimate, power calculation or test.
- **Purging and embargo:** samples whose label window overlaps a cut-off are removed; a further gap of H + 1 trading days (cf. `lopezdeprado2018advances`).
- **Training population of gates:** names ranked in the base top-K_tr on each day, K_tr = 3K (PLACEHOLDER), with labels / returns from standard counterfactual orders (§8.3); gates are applied to the top-K. Same population for LOSS, LOSS-LR and NET-K; NET-U trains on all of C_t.
- **Training rows whose standard order is not filled (added v0.5, M-S13).** A standard counterfactual order that rule SF records as not filled has no position and therefore no O1 label (§3.8 (i)). Rule: **such rows are dropped from the training and calibration sets of every learned gate** (LOSS, LOSS-LR, NET-K, NET-U). They are not labelled 0 and are not given a paper outcome. Whether a standard order fills depends on the name and the day only, so the dropped rows are **identical for all feature sets** (−Z, +Z_H, +Z_O, permuted variants) and all seeds; the contrasts of C3 / C4 are between gates trained on the same rows. For the remaining rows the training label is the O1 tree applied to the hypothetical position of the standard order (a training target, not an executed entry; it enters no theory quantity), and NET's target is ρ. At deployment a gate may still submit an order that then does not fill (ρ = 0, §8.3); the gate is not given the fill status. Reported: number and share of dropped rows by year.
- **Frozen settings and growing training sets (added v0.5, M-S11).** The gate fit of the first tuning year uses one year of rows (2013); later deployment fits use several times as many. Rule, fixed here: every hyper-parameter is frozen **at its absolute value** (number of boosting rounds, learning rate, tree depth / leaves, penalties), except minimum-leaf-size parameters, which are frozen **as a fraction of the training rows** (search space defined in fractions). No parameter is re-scaled by hand. Because absolute settings chosen on a small sample may suit the richer feature sets differently from the reference gate, a **development-only sensitivity** is pre-specified (§6.3 item 7): a second tuning stage on validation years {2018, 2019} (fit 2013..v−2), evaluated on 2020–2021 only, compared with the primary frozen settings on the same two years. Exploratory; never run on the hold-out; it does not change the primary settings.
- **Known weaknesses, stated:** tuning rests on two validation years each (2015 contains the mid-2015 crash) and on small early training sets; the deployed gate model is one year stale (fit through Y−2); thresholds assume the score distribution of Y−1 carries to Y — realised coverage is reported and will differ from c. **Consequence for H-A1 / H-A2:** a thinly tuned gate makes a small θ_sel easier to obtain, so a "negligible" verdict is a statement about these implementations (§1, §9.3).

### 4.4 Final frozen hold-out
- **2022-01-01 to 2026-06-30.** Not touched (no fit, plot or summary of strategy outcomes) until all of the following are done and recorded by the lead in `STATUS.md`: conventions (§3), feature lists, model classes, frozen hyper-parameters, the algorithm of §4.3, estimands and test cards (§8), every parameter of §17, and the analysis code are frozen and hashed; **the reconciliation tests R1–R4 pass; `synth_validation` passes; the complete-null pipeline simulation passes its criterion (§10.2)**; **the status of C1 / C2 has been fixed by the rule of §10.1 (`design_power`) and recorded**; `power_precheck` has been reported to the user.
- **Time stamp of the freeze (decided at Gate 2; rewritten v0.6).** The freeze is a manifest of hashes (conventions file, rule calendar, event table and first data snapshot, feature lists, frozen hyper-parameters, analysis code, this plan, §17 values, the list of Z_O classes, the status of C1 / C2). **No external service is used** (`STATUS.md` decision record of 2026-11-15, item (6): the conservative choice, because an external time stamp sends content outside the project). The manifest is stored locally and its hash is written into `STATUS.md`. A local record can be edited and is not independent evidence of the date; **the paper states that the freeze is attested by an internal record only.** The conditions that must hold before the freeze are listed in §18.
- The hold-out is run **once** per pre-specified configuration. Anything changed after unblinding is reported as post hoc.
- Caveat: the hold-out is frozen with respect to our code; the broad history of 2022–2026 is public knowledge.

---

## 5. Feature groups Z_H and Z_O, and their controls

Two groups, never merged in a confirmatory contrast. **Z_H = holder-state variables** (§5.1–5.3, definitions unchanged from v0.2). **Z_O = observable actions of other identifiable participants** (§5.6; the name is the one fixed by `research_question.md` v4 — in content, stock-day aggregates of public disclosure events, always accompanied by the listing-trigger controls R_O of §5.4). Every feature for the day-t decision uses data available by the close of t-1 under the timing rules stated for it.

**Z_H inputs:** P = close adjusted by the feature-level factor of §3.11 (v0.6: a total-return series built from `preclose`, so "the price holders paid" is in total-return units), V = daily turnover as a fraction of tradable shares (definition per audit A10).

### 5.1 Capital-gains overhang (Grinblatt–Han style; `grinblatt2005prospect`)
- Reference price: RP_t = (1/k_t) * sum_{n=1}^{N} [ V_{t-n} * prod_{tau=1}^{n-1} (1 - V_{t-n+tau}) ] * P_{t-n}, where k_t normalises the weights to sum to one.
- **Primary (v0.7, F-1 / F-2 — the original's timing): CGO_t = (P_{t-2} − RP_{t-1}) / P_{t-2}**, where RP_{t-1} is the reference price above evaluated one day earlier (prices P_{t-2}, P_{t-3}, …). Column `zh_cgo_250`. The working paper regresses the return of period t on g_{t−1} = (P_{t−2} − R_{t−1}) / P_{t−2}: the price is lagged one period "to avoid microstructure effects". Its period is a week; the formula is transcribed period for period, so the lag here is one trading day. **Variant (the v0.6 wording, no extra lag): (P_{t-1} − RP_t) / P_{t-1}**, column `zh_cgo_250_nolag`.
- **Checked against the working-paper version; the journal version was not opened (v0.7, F-1).** Text used: Grinblatt & Han, NBER Working Paper 8734 (October 2001 draft, "The Disposition Effect and Momentum"), eq. (25) and Section II; the comparison is in the feature report §2.3 and in the header of `experiments/src/features/zh.py`, and is to be written to `experiments/src/features/features.md`. The PDF text was extracted without a renderer; Greek letters and subscripts were lost and the formula was reconstructed from the extracted text and its context. **It has to be re-read on rendered pages before Freeze-1 (FC-A15).** Weights, normalisation, sign and denominator of the reference price above agree with eq. (25) as reconstructed. Declared deviations: (i) daily data with N = 250 trading days instead of weekly data over 260 weeks; (ii) turnover is volume over **tradable** shares (the bar source's field), where the original uses shares outstanding (§17.4 item 2); (iii) P is the total-return series of §3.11. Pre-specified variants (robustness definitions, not tuning choices; a variant never enters the primary feature set): N = 500 with the same one-day lag (`zh_cgo_500`); the no-lag form above; and a **literal weekly transcription** `zh_cgo_w260` — completed calendar weeks, weekly turnover = sum of the daily turnover capped at 1, weekly price = last adjusted close of the week, R from the 260 weeks before the last completed week w0, g = (P_{w0−1} − R_{w0}) / P_{w0−1}, constant over the decision days of a week (v0.6 code: no weekly lag and the daily price P_{t−1}; to be changed). If the journal version (Journal of Financial Economics, 2005) is obtained and differs, the journal definition becomes primary and the present one a labelled variant; that change is made before Freeze-1 or not at all.
- Fields: adjusted close, turnover. Suspended days contribute V = 0.

### 5.2 Chip-distribution statistics (turnover-decay method, own numpy implementation)
Algorithm as documented in `literature/resources.md` F1 (reference: `myhhub/stock`, `instock/core/kline/cyq.py`, Apache-2.0; read as reference after approval, re-implemented, source credited):
- Price grid of G cells (default 150) over the window's [min low, max high]; window W = 210 trading days (the reference defaults).
- Each day: existing chips c(p) <- c(p) * (1 - V_t); then add mass V_t spread over [low_t, high_t] with a triangular density peaking at (O+H+L+C)/4; a one-price day adds a point mass.
- Outputs at t-1: **profit ratio** PR = share of chip mass at prices below P_{t-1}; **average-cost gap** (P_{t-1} - mean cost) / P_{t-1}; **90% and 70% cost-band width** relative to price (concentration); **overhead supply** = share of mass in (P, 1.1 P].
- Fields: adjusted O/H/L/C, turnover. Validation: where permitted, compare with AKShare `stock_cyq_em` on its most recent ~90 days for a sample of stocks (needs `py_mini_racer`; Python 3.14 wheel UNVERIFIED; optional).
- Note: 5.1 and the average-cost gap are close relatives; their correlation is reported, and they are treated as one feature group, not as independent evidence.
- **Rulings of v0.7 (F-3).** The method has no academic source, so the choices follow the lead's rule for such constructions — point-in-time safe, simplest, reproducible — and were made without computing any relation to returns. (a) *Rolling window.* For each data day the distribution is rebuilt from the last W = 210 rows only; no recursion runs through the whole sample, so a value does not depend on where the history starts. (b) *Mass.* The chips of day j carry exactly the mass V_j and survive with weight V_j × Π_{k>j}(1 − V_k); cell masses are exact differences of the triangular distribution function (the reference implementation adds density heights, which makes the mass of a one-price day depend on the grid). (c) **Initial mass — primary: none; the distribution is divided by the mass that turned over inside the window** (`init = "renormalise"`). This is what the reference implementation does, and it is the normalisation of §5.1 (weights divided by their sum k_t). **Sensitivity: the v0.6 placeholder** — the never-traded share Π(1 − V_k) spread uniformly over the first window day's [low, high] (`init = "uniform_first_day"`), which gives an arbitrary location to chips about which the window says nothing. Both use the same past rows. They differ mainly in windows with little turnover (feature report §3.5, a feature-to-feature comparison only). The sensitivity is reported as a robustness run; it is not a second candidate to choose from after results. (d) *Average cost* is the mean (closed form); the median — what the reference implementation calls average cost — is the variant `zh_chip_cost_gap_median`. (e) *Band widths* are (q95 − q05) / P_{t−1} and (q85 − q15) / P_{t−1} as written above, not the reference implementation's (high − low) / (high + low). (f) Columns of the primary set: `zh_chip_pr`, `zh_chip_cost_gap`, `zh_chip_band90`, `zh_chip_band70`, `zh_chip_overhead`.

### 5.3 Abnormal turnover
- **Primary (v0.7, F-2): ATO_t = mean(V over the last 20 trading days) / mean(V over the last 250 trading days) — a ratio, not in logs** (`zh_ato_20_250`). Checked against Liu, Stambaugh & Yuan, NBER Working Paper 24458 (March 2018 draft, "Size and Value in China"), the factor-construction section and the appendix item "Turnover": "the ratio of its average daily turnover over the past 20 days to its average daily turnover over the past 250 days". **The journal version (Journal of Financial Economics, 2019) was not opened;** the re-reading requirement and the replacement rule of §5.1 apply. v0.6 wrote "in logs" from memory; by v0.6's own rule ("if the original differs, the original definition becomes primary and the one above a labelled variant") the log form is now the variant `zh_ato_20_250_log` (identical for trees and rank transforms, different for linear models). Declared deviation: the original's daily turnover is volume over **total** shares outstanding, ours is over tradable shares — the bar source has no share-capital series (§17.4 item 2). The ratio is NaN, not infinite, when the 250-day mean is zero; turnover above 1 is not capped here (no decay recursion).
- Short-window feature (in the primary set; no source in the literature is claimed for it): log( V_{t-1} / mean(V over the 20 days before t−1) ), `zh_ato_short_20`.
- **Primary Z_H feature set (v0.7; a frozen list of 8 columns):** `zh_cgo_250`, the five chip columns of §5.2, `zh_ato_20_250`, `zh_ato_short_20`. The variants (`zh_cgo_500`, `zh_cgo_250_nolag`, `zh_cgo_w260`, `zh_chip_cost_gap_median`, `zh_ato_20_250_log`, and the chip columns under `init = "uniform_first_day"`) are used only in robustness runs, each replacing its primary counterpart; they never enter the feature set of a gate of cards C1–C4.
- Fields: turnover.

### 5.3a Implementation details of §5.1–5.3 that must be fixed before coding (P12; renumbered from 5.2a in v0.5 so that it follows the sections it refers to)
- **Turnover outside [0, 1].** The decay weights (1 − V) are meaningless for V > 1. Rule (PLACEHOLDER): V is capped at 1 inside the recursions only, the number of capped stock-days is reported (A19), and a sensitivity drops those stock-days' windows instead. No other clipping.
- **Initial chip mass (rewritten v0.7, F-3): §5.2 (c).** Primary: no initial mass, renormalised; sensitivity: uniform on the first window day's range. Features are NaN until W rows have elapsed since the first traded day and for 60 traded days after a suspension longer than W/2 rows (PLACEHOLDER, unchanged); a window with a missing input is NaN.
- **Warm-up of variants:** §2.2.
- **Status of the formula check (rewritten v0.7, F-1).** §5.1 and §5.3 were compared with the NBER working-paper versions; the journal versions were not. v0.6's condition "implementation is blocked until they are checked" is replaced by: the working-paper definitions are implemented as primary; before Freeze-1 the two formulas are re-read on rendered pages of the working papers (FC-A15), and the journal versions are compared if the user supplies them (§17.4 item 3). If they are not supplied, the working-paper definitions are frozen and the paper says which version was used. The record is kept in `experiments/src/features/features.md` (v0.6 named `experiments/src/base_strategy/features.md`).

### 5.4 Control groups (rewritten: P8)

"Without Z" never means "without the raw material of Z". The control set is R = R_H ∪ R_O and is in **every** feature set of every learned method.

- **R_H-sum (as v0.2):** returns over {1, 5, 20, 60, 120, 250} days; price relative to rolling means and to the undecayed turnover-weighted average price; position in the rolling range; turnover levels, means, standard deviations, cumulative turnover; high–low range statistics — same fields and look-backs as Z_H. **(v0.7, F-15.)** Frozen list — 34 columns, as in the feature catalogue: returns over {1, 5, 20, 60, 120, 250}; price to rolling mean over {5, 20, 60, 120, 250}; price to the undecayed turnover-weighted average price over {210, 250}; range position over {20, 60, 120, 250}; turnover — last day, means over {5, 20, 60, 120, 250}, standard deviations over {20, 60, 250}, sums over {20, 210, 250}; (high − low) / close — last day and means over {5, 20, 60, 250}. The windows not named in v0.6 were chosen by the implementer before any modelling and are confirmed here.
- **R_H-hist (new, matched-window history control):** for each window length used by a Z_H feature (250 and 210 days in the primary definitions), the window is cut into 10 consecutive equal bins (PLACEHOLDER) and, per bin: cumulative return, mean turnover, cumulative turnover, bin volume-weighted price relative to P_{t-1}, mean (high − low)/close. This gives a learner the time-ordered price / turnover path over exactly the windows Z_H consumes, at bin resolution. **(v0.7, F-15.)** In bins of equal length the mean and the cumulative turnover are exactly collinear; only the mean is kept: 4 quantities × 10 bins × 2 windows = 80 columns (v0.6 code: 100).
- **R_O (new, event-trigger and coverage controls for Z_O), computed from daily bars for every stock** on the same look-backs as Z_O ({1, 5, 20} days): daily return and its deviation from the index return, amplitude (high − low)/preclose, turnover, closed-at-limit-up / limit-down flags, counts of days in the look-back meeting each proxy trigger threshold (thresholds as in the exchange rules once OA1 has archived them; until then UNVERIFIED), largest one-day traded amount relative to its 60-day mean. Purpose: the Dragon–Tiger list is published only for stocks with extreme price / turnover on the day, so "being listed" must be separated from the price–volume event that causes the listing. **(v0.7, F-12, F-20.)** Two amplitude definitions are used on purpose: the control `ro_amp_*` is (high − low) / `preclose`, as written here; the trigger proxy `ro_trig_amp_*` uses the rule's own definition, (high − low) / low (`rule_verification.md` C1). The trigger proxies are proxies and are called so: they do not reproduce the ranking "top N of the exchange"; the index of the deviation criterion is a board proxy (list of §3.10, item 20); the turnover denominator is the bar source's; the separate thresholds for ST names are not implemented. Limit-count controls are NaN while their window contains any of the name's first 5 trading days (no date of the registration reform is needed). All bands, thresholds and regime dates are read from the one rule calendar (§3.10), not from a copy. **(v0.8, J-1.)** This now includes the three-day cumulative threshold of `ro_trig_cum3_*` (RC-18 element `cum3`, list item 22); the copy in the feature configuration is deleted. **(v0.8, J-14; implementer conventions, confirmed.)** If a name's listing date is unknown, its first 5 rows are treated as its first 5 trading days; the 0 / 1 diagnostic `diag_filled_xsrc_in_window` covers the last 251 rows (the longest primary window) and is not a model input. **(v0.8, U-PILOT.)** The trigger proxies are computed per stock; the "top N of the exchange" ranking (TODO-F5) cannot be built from a list of 1,000 names and is not approximated in the pilot.
- **What the controls do and do not license (interpretation narrowed).** A Z contrast is a **pipeline feature intervention**: "adding the group to this pipeline, which already contains R, changed the statistic". It shows incremental value **over these summaries**, not over arbitrary functions of the same raw history; R_H-hist reduces but does not close that gap (bin resolution; variants with longer windows).

### 5.5 Diagnostics reported before any gate uses Z
Missingness and coverage by year; cross-sectional distributions; correlation of each Z feature with |base score|, with R, and between the two groups. If a group is related to |score|, contrasts are additionally reported with the group residualised on |score| and R; **the residualising regression is fitted on the training window of the same annual fit only** and applied forward (never on the full sample).

### 5.6 Z_O — observable actions of other identifiable participants (group confirmed: free data only, user decision of 2026-11-15; lags and regimes revised in v0.6)

**What the name does and does not mean (added v0.5, M-S1).** The features are stock-day aggregates of disclosed events (an indicator, days since the last event, scaled net amounts). Names and seat-level detail are excluded by design (§5.6.1), and block-trade counterparties are mostly an anonymous institutional label. The features therefore record **that a disclosure-triggering event occurred and its aggregate size, not who acted**; "identifiable participants" is read in this plan as referring to the legal category of the discloser (listed seats, block-trade parties, insiders and large shareholders), not to an identity used by the model. The ARS editor seat recommended the plainer name "public disclosure-event variables"; the name is fixed by `research_question.md` v4 and is not changed in this file (see Revision log, M-S1). Wherever Z_O is introduced the control set R_O (price–volume triggers of a listing, §5.4) is named with it.

Evidence base, stated with its depth: `literature/opponent_action_variables.md` reports published A-share studies for each class below, **all read at abstract or bibliographic level only** (no full text); `data_feasibility_opponent_actions.md` reports that the interfaces answered for single dates / stocks, with **no check of value correctness**. Neither file is an externally reviewed result. The plan makes no directional prediction for any class (the literature file itself records mixed directions, e.g. for insider sales).

#### 5.6.1 Classes (free data; AKShare)
| Class | Source table | Raw fields allowed (whitelist, OA2) | Not used |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LHB — Dragon–Tiger list** | `stock_lhb_detail_em` (daily summary), `stock_lhb_jgmmtj_em` (institutional seats, daily) | listing date; net buy, buy, sell amounts of the disclosed seats; their share of the day's traded amount; number of buying / selling institutional seats; institutional net buy amount; listing reason code | "上榜后 N 日" returns, "解读", names; seat-level detail (not fetched) |
| **BLK — block trades** | `stock_dzjy_mrtj` (per stock-day), `stock_dzjy_mrmx` (per trade) | trade date; price premium / discount to the close; traded amount and its share of float market value; number of trades | broker-office names (mostly "机构专用" in recent years per the probe) |
| **INS — executed share changes of large shareholders and insiders** | `stock_ggcg_em` | announcement date; direction; change as a share of total and of float shares; change start / end dates | "最新价", "涨跌幅" (current snapshot); `stock_hold_management_detail_em` (no announcement date) |

INS: **only information available after the announcement date is used; the event is dated by its announcement, never by the transaction dates.** The table was back-filled (OA6); the announcement-date field cannot be verified from free data except on the OA5 sample.

**(v0.8, U-PILOT, J-12.)** All three classes were pulled by date for the whole market; features use only the rows of the codes in use (pilot round: the pilot list) (after the code mapping of the feature library; rows of B-shares, the Beijing exchange and other instruments are outside it in any case). BLK features use the per-trade table `stock_dzjy_mrmx` only; the per-stock-day table is a cross-check (trade counts equal, amounts in units of 10,000 yuan; it lacks 10,157 stock-days of the per-trade table, unexplained; §19, KL-9).

#### 5.6.2 Availability rule (rewritten v0.6, V-17; one lag per class for the whole sample, fixed before any modelling)
An event of trading day s (LHB, BLK), or announced on day s (INS), is usable for decisions of day t ≥ s + ℓ. v0.5's criterion is kept: ℓ = 1 only if an archived rule text shows publication after the close of s and before the next open, for the whole analysis period of the class; otherwise ℓ = 2.

| Class | ℓ | Basis (§3.10) |
|---|---|---|
| LHB | **2** | RC-18: the conditions of the disclosure are verified, but the rule fixes no clock time of publication. The file times of 16:21–17:05 seen on three SZSE files are collateral evidence, not a rule |
| BLK | **1 if** the block-trade disclosure clause ("after the end of each trading day") is archived for both exchanges for the whole analysis period before the freeze (item 6 of the list in §3.10); **otherwise 2** | RC-19: opened so far — SZSE 2013 and 2026 versions, SSE 2023 and 2026 versions |
| INS | **2** | Date-only stamp in the table. Time stamps of the statutory disclosure platform exist for part of the sample and are not used to shorten the lag |

The other lag is a labelled sensitivity (§11). A lag is one number per class, so that a feature has one definition over the whole sample. **(v0.7, F-8, F-9.)** State at this revision: the condition for ℓ = 1 is not met (archived: SZSE 2013 and 2026 texts, SSE 2023 and 2026 texts), so **BLK runs with ℓ = 2**; it becomes 1 only if the missing texts are archived before Freeze-1 (FC-V4). An INS announcement dated on a non-trading day is first rolled **forward** to the next market trading day, and ℓ is counted from there (later than necessary, never earlier). INS rows are dropped, and counted, if the announcement date is missing, if the direction is neither an increase nor a decrease, or if the announcement date precedes the end date of the change (such a row may have been overwritten with later information). The upstream entry-time field serves the back-fill diagnostic only and never enters a feature (unit-tested). **(v0.8, J-10, J-11; confirmed on the full INS table, feature report §7.4.)** 15.4% of the retained rows are announced on a non-trading day and are rolled forward (none backward; every first usable decision day lies strictly after the announcement date); 41 rows are dropped by the cleaning rule. Back-filled rows (41.2% of retained rows; nearly all rows announced before 2019) are used like every other row, timed by the rolled announcement date plus ℓ; changing every entry time and every change-start date leaves the features bit-identical on the 20 trial names. Whether the announcement date of a back-filled row is right cannot be checked from free data; that is what OA10 tests (§2.4). 39.5% of the rows have an entry time before the announcement date (mostly the evening before, from 2020 on); collateral only (§19, KL-7).

#### 5.6.2a Regimes of the disclosure rules (new in v0.6, V-12, V-13)
The rules that generate two of the three classes changed inside the sample. The same feature value therefore does not mean the same thing in every year. The confirmatory statistic ψ_O is unchanged (full sample); the following is added.

- **LHB (RC-18).** The number of names listed per criterion rose from 3 to 5 (SSE: 2021; SZSE: between the 2006 and the November 2013 rule texts), the thresholds differ by board (ChiNext and STAR: ±15% / 30% / 30%), and the SSE turnover denominator changed. "Being listed" is thus a different event before and after, and across boards. Treatment: (i) the control set R_O receives regime indicators — exchange × {top-3, top-5} and the board-threshold regime — switching at the dates of RC-18, which are marked unverified where they are; (ii) the listing-reason code stays on the whitelist (the upstream field carries the rule wording of its time: "top three" in 2010, "top five" in 2024); (iii) no statement pools listings from different regimes as one kind of event; (iv) ψ_O without the LHB class is part of the "each class alone / without a class" sensitivity of §11. **(v0.7, F-13.)** The dates of the change from 3 to 5 are unverified, so the indicator `ro_lhb_top5` does not assert them. SZSE names: "unknown" (NaN) before 2013-12-01, 1 from then (the effective date of the November 2013 text is itself unverified: list of §3.10, item 19). SSE names: 0 before 2021-09-17 (the date of the notice), "unknown" from 2021-09-17 to 2021-10-07, 1 from 2021-10-08. The wording of the upstream listing-reason field is collateral evidence only and sets no date (trial files: for SSE names "top three" last appears on 2021-09-30; for SZSE names both wordings appear from 2011-02 to 2014-12, unexplained). This qualifies (ii): the field's wording cannot be relied on to date a regime.
- **INS (RC-20).** Five regimes: before 2015-07-08; 2015-07-08 to 2016-01-08 (sales through the secondary market prohibited for controlling shareholders, holders of 5% or more, directors, supervisors and senior managers); 2016-01-09 to 2017-05-26 (large shareholders pre-disclose sales by centralised bidding 15 trading days ahead); 2017-05-27 to 2024-05-23 (extended to directors, supervisors and senior managers; completion announced within two trading days); from 2024-05-24 (large shareholders' block-trade sales also pre-disclosed). From the third regime on, an **executed-sale** announcement of a covered person through a covered channel was preceded by a published plan. The plan is not in our data (out of scope, §5.6.4), so in those regimes the executed-sale record is in part information that the market already had, and the covered set widens at each break. The provisions quoted concern reductions; purchases are not described there. Treatment: (i) R_O receives the five regime indicators; (ii) sell-side and buy-side INS features stay separate (§5.6.3); (iii) INS-only analyses and the "ψ_O without INS" sensitivity are tabulated by regime, as exploratory tables; (iv) no statement treats "insider selling" as one signal across regimes; (v) the hold-out (2022-01 to 2026-06) contains the break of 2024-05-24, and every gate is trained on earlier years, i.e. across regimes — stated beside card C4.
- **BLK (RC-19).** No change of the disclosure rule was found in the sample; no regime indicator.

#### 5.6.3 Encoding (events are sparse; Z4)
For each class and each look-back W in {1, 5, 20} trading days (PLACEHOLDER), per stock-day:
- **event indicator** 1{at least one usable event in window(t, W)}, where **window(t, W) = the event days s with t − ℓ − W + 1 ≤ s ≤ t − ℓ**, i.e. the W most recent days whose events are usable on day t (v0.7, F-4: under the reading "the last W days ending t−1" the window for W = 1 would always be empty when ℓ = 2);
- **days since the last usable event** (v0.7, F-5): t − s for the most recent usable event day s within the last 250 market trading days (U60), capped at 60; 0 when there is none (the true minimum is ℓ, so 0 is unambiguous). With it, the indicator **`*_has_prior`** = 1{a usable event within those 250 days}. The fixed look-back replaces "at any time since the start of the raw data", whose meaning would drift with the distance from the start of the pull. Both are NaN until the class has 250 + ℓ trading days of coverage (early 2009 with raw data from 2008-01); **(v0.8, J-8; the implementer's reading, written as a formula)** with trading-day indices, the look-back set is {s : t − 250 ≤ s ≤ t − ℓ}, and both columns are NaN unless the class's coverage start c_0 satisfies c_0 ≤ t − 250 − ℓ (ℓ days more than the look-back strictly needs; conservative, kept);
- **intensity**, exactly 0 when there is no event (window sums, never differences of running sums): **LHB** — signed net buy of the disclosed seats and signed institutional net buy, summed over the window and divided by **ADV_t**, the mean traded amount over the 20 most recent traded days ending t−1 (the definition of U40; v0.7, F-7 — v0.6 did not say where the 20 days end, and an average that counts suspended days as zero gives a denominator near zero after a suspension); **BLK** — traded amount / float market value of the trade day (float market value = unadjusted close × volume ÷ turnover from our own bars; the vendor's ratio column, whose point-in-time status is unknown, is not used — F-11), and the amount-weighted premium / discount; **INS** — signed change as a share of float shares, summed over the look-back, buys and sells separately. A missing denominator or amount with an event in the window gives NaN, not 0. **(v0.8, J-6; confirmed.)** "Exactly 0 when there is no event" does not mean "0 only when there is no event": `CHANGE_FREE_RATIO` has two decimals (in per cent), and 9.4% of the retained INS rows are exactly 0 (changes rounded to 0.00). A rounded zero is a value, not a missing amount; the event indicator separates it from "no event". The total-share column is not substituted (it would change the definition; F-10 / OA5 govern). The share of events with intensity 0 is reported. **(v0.8, J-7; changed — block-trade premium, U64.)** The premium of a block trade is recomputed from our own bars: block price ÷ the name's unadjusted close of the trade day − 1 (the vendor's premium column is not used, as for the float value, F-11). A trade is **implausible** if its price lies outside [P^down, P^up] of the trade day, computed from our `preclose` by RC-5 / RC-6 with the engine's limit-price function (applied on every day, as SF does, §3.4), or if the name has no traded bar that day. Implausible trades are excluded from the amount-weighted premium only; they count for the indicator, the days-since column and the amount intensity. If every trade of a window is implausible, the premium is NaN. Reason: 710 rows of the vendor's column have |premium| > 30% (range −92% to +80%; feature report §7.4), beyond any price limit of the universe; the rule was fixed from that structural fact before any feature was related to any return. That a block trade in a stock with a price limit must be priced inside the day's limits is item 23 of §3.10 (unverified); the rule is applied as a data-quality filter either way. Counts of implausible trades, by year and exchange, are reported.
- **LHB reason table (v0.8, J-4, J-5; confirmed).** The reason table is frozen **by string** with the first snapshot, together with the generating rule (`build_lhb_reason_map.py`, two regular expressions), both hashed; a string not in the table is "unclassified" (indicator only). First snapshot: 659 strings → single-day 397, multi-day 254, unclassified 8; unclassified rows 6,980 (2.8%), stock-days with unclassified rows only 6,339 (2.9%). The 8 unclassified strings (among them "no price-limit security" and the delisting-arrangement strings) are not assigned by judgement. The shares of unclassified rows and stock-days are reported for every snapshot (first snapshot, OA6 second pull) and year, with the list of new strings in the second pull.
- **Last decision day (v0.8, J-9; confirmed).** The rule calendar ends on 2026-06-30, the last data day; the feature panel's row for 2026-07-01 has NaN regime indicators and is not used: no decision day after the last data day enters any analysis (end-of-window cut U50). The calendar is not extended.
- **Several LHB rows for one stock-day (v0.7, F-6).** In the files on disk at test time 14.8% of the listed stock-days have several rows and 19.7% have only rows with a multi-day reason. Each listing reason carries its own seat amounts, and the amounts of a "consecutive trading days" reason are cumulative over those days. Rule: the event indicator counts any row; **intensities use single-day-reason rows only** — one row per stock-day, the one with the largest seat traded amount, ties broken by the larger absolute net buy and then by the reason string — so that a cumulative amount is never added to the daily amounts it contains; a separate indicator `zo_lhb_multi_W` = 1{a multi-day-reason row in the window}. Reason strings are mapped to {single-day, multi-day, unclassified} by a table built from the strings alone and frozen with the first snapshot; unclassified rows count for the indicator only and are reported. The institutional table is treated in the same way. (v0.6 code: when a stock-day has only multi-day rows, their cumulative amounts enter the intensity; to be changed.)
No value is imputed: "no event" is the value 0 with indicator 0. Before a class's measured start date (OA3) all its features are NaN and the class's analysis starts there. A per-class "data exist on this date" indicator is part of R_O. **(v0.7, F-14, F-10.)** "Data exist" means coverage: the class's analysis start (the later of the OA3 start and the first day of the raw pull, 2008-01-01) is ≤ t − ℓ. In production a file that is missing from the snapshot inside the analysis period is an error that stops the pipeline, not a NaN (the test code, run while the pull was in progress, returned NaN). The column mapping of the INS table rests on column positions in the upstream response; `CHANGE_FREE_RATIO` (taken as the change as a share of float shares) must pass the value check OA5 before Freeze-1, otherwise the INS intensities are dropped and the INS indicators kept. `CHANGE_RATE`, `CLOSE_PRICE`, `REAL_PRICE`, `TRADE_AVERAGE_PRICE` and the entry time are blacklisted.

#### 5.6.4 Classes not included, with reasons
- **Northbound per-stock holdings:** daily disclosure ended in 2024-08 (data file: series 2017-03-16 to 2024-08-16; verified in v0.6, RC-21: the exchanges' notices of 2024-07-26 move the per-stock disclosure to a quarterly schedule from 2024-08-19), i.e. inside the hold-out. Not used.
- **Margin trading:** not in Z_O. The literature file reports (abstracts only) no stable incremental predictability once turnover is controlled, and classifies it as a leverage / crowding state rather than an action of an identifiable informed party. **Researcher's judgement on the suggested negative control: not in round 1.** Reasons: it would cost about 8,000 requests plus a coverage-restricted sub-sample analysis; it is not a known null (one abstract-level study), so a non-null outcome would be uninterpretable as a pipeline check; the false-positive behaviour of the pipeline is tested directly by §10.2. It is listed as an optional, unscheduled exploratory item (§17 U31).
- **Shareholder counts:** a holder-state variable, and the probed table starts in 2013 and failed for a delisted name. Not used in either group in round 1.
- **Pledges, site visits, buy-backs:** not used (snapshot / coverage problems in the data file).
- **Restricted-share unlock schedules and pre-disclosed selling / buying plans: out of scope for this paper; possible future extension if licensed data become available** (user decision relayed by the lead, 2026-11-15: free data only). Free data cannot support them point-in-time: no structured interface for plan announcements; the unlock table lacks an announcement date and looked incomplete for a delisted name. No experiment in this plan uses them.

#### 5.6.5 Consequences of sparsity for inference (Z4)
- Results for Z_O are reported on **(a) the full sample** (the confirmatory statistic, §8.4 card C4) and **(b) the event sub-sample** — nominated stock-days with at least one Z_O indicator equal to 1, a set fixed by pre-decision information — which is exploratory.
- Share of nominated stock-days in the event sub-sample, by year and class, is reported first (UNVERIFIED; no estimate is offered).
- Power: the two gates of card C4 can submit different orders only where their scores differ. If they differ on a share q of orders, the full-sample statistic equals q times the mean difference on the changed orders; a small q therefore shrinks the detectable full-sample effect. q and the resulting minimum detectable effect are measured in `power_precheck` on development data and reported before the freeze. No value of q is assumed here.

---

## 6. Gates, filters and baselines

Feature sets: **"−Z" = B0 + R + base score**; "+Z_H" adds Z_H; "+Z_O" adds Z_O; "+Z_H+Z_O" adds both. The nominated list of the confirmatory analysis is always the top-K of the **base(−Z)** predictor (features B0 + R).

### 6.1 Round 1 (extended: P4, P5)

"Within-batch?" = the method chooses a strict subset of the day's nominated names on some days, so the Q-select statistic of §8.3 is informative for it.

| ID | Method | Definition | Within-batch? | Enters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G0 | Ungated | Submit all K nominated orders | no | reference |
| SCORE | **Top-⌈cK⌉ by base score** (new) | Each day submit the ⌈cK⌉ highest-ranked nominated names | yes (fixed count) | Q-total, Q-select |
| CONF | Calibrated-confidence filter | Submit a name if the calibrated probability of a positive net H-day return (isotonic map of the base rank score, §4.3) exceeds a global threshold. Differs from SCORE through the global threshold (daily count varies) and ties | yes | Q-total, Q-select, **card C1** |
| LOSS-LR(−Z) | **Regularised logistic loss classifier** (new) | L2-penalised logistic regression for L^{(1)} = 1 on the −Z features (rank-transformed, median-imputed with missing indicators); submit if predicted loss probability <= threshold | yes | Q-total, Q-select |
| LOSS(−Z) | Generic loss predictor | LightGBM classifier for L^{(1)} = 1 on −Z features | yes | Q-total, Q-select, **cards C2, C3, C4** |
| LOSS(+Z_H), LOSS(+Z_O) | Same class, grid and budget; feature group added | — | yes | **card C3; card C4** |
| LOSS(+Z_H+Z_O); LOSS(+perm Z_H), LOSS(+perm Z_O) | Both groups; capacity-matched nuisance controls (§7.3) | — | yes | exploratory |
| NET-K(−Z / +Z_H / +Z_O) | **Net-return selection on the base top-K** (new) | LightGBM regression of the standard order's net return ρ (§8.3; already cost-adjusted) on the same features, **same training population (top-K_tr), same nominated list, same coverage denominator K, same randomisation pool** as LOSS; submit if prediction >= threshold | yes | Q-total, Q-select (architecture comparison with LOSS at matched opportunities) |
| NET-U(−Z / …) | **End-to-end net-return selection on the full candidate set** | Same regression trained on all of C_t; each day submit the names of C_t with prediction >= threshold, at most K. Coverage denominator: K (threshold set so that mean orders per day = cK). Randomisation pool: C_t, stratified as in §8.3 | own pool | **Reported separately as an end-to-end benchmark; never in a contrast with top-K gates other than Q-total** |
| VOL, LIQ | **Rule filters** (new) | Submit a nominated name unless its 20-day return volatility is above (VOL) / its 20-day average traded amount is below (LIQ) a threshold set to reach coverage c | yes | Q-total, Q-select |
| TREND | **Market-trend filter** (new) | Submit the full list if the trailing L-day return of the equal-weight index of the universe (§2.3) is >= a threshold, else nothing; L and the threshold are frozen hyper-parameters | no (all-or-nothing) | Q-total, exposure-schedule attribution |
| PERF | Matured-performance gate | EWMA of the daily cross-sectional RankIC of the base score from labels matured by t−1; full list if EWMA >= threshold, else nothing (after `sanderink2026alpha`; the paper must be re-read before coding) | no (all-or-nothing) | Q-total, exposure-schedule attribution |
| VT | **Feasible exposure control** (new) | All K orders, each slot scaled by s_t = min(1, σ* / σ̂_{t−1}), σ̂ = realised volatility of G0's daily return over the last 60 days through t−1; σ* frozen in the tuning stage so that mean s_t = c on the tuning years. Never above 1 (no leverage) | no | Q-total, exposure-schedule attribution |
| E-const-dev | **Implementable constant scaling** (new) | All K orders, each slot scaled by λ_Y = min(1, mean invested fraction of gate g / that of G0 over year Y−1), fixed at the start of year Y (first year: tuning years). One per gate | no | Q-total comparator |
| E-const-expost | Ex-post constant scaling | G0's holdings scaled by λ = Σ_t e_t^G / Σ_t e_t^B over the evaluation window. **Uses the evaluation window: an attribution reference, not a strategy an investor could have chosen; if λ > 1 it violates the no-leverage rule and is shown as a reference only** | no | level / timing split (§8.2) |
| E-sched | Exposure-schedule reference (Lemma 3, h^E = e^G w^B) | Need not be feasible | — | attribution |
| RND | Stratified random selection at matched order opportunities | Monte-Carlo draws (200 repetitions, PLACEHOLDER) of the gate's per-stratum submitted counts from the nominated list, passed through the same sizing and fill rules. **Used only for path-dependent portfolio metrics; the confirmatory statistic uses the exact expectation of this draw (§8.3)** | — | descriptive |

Coverage: thresholds target c in {0.3, 0.5, 0.7} of nominated orders (primary c = 0.5, PLACEHOLDER) by the rule of §4.3; realised submission and executed coverage are reported.

### 6.2 Round 2 — postponed until round 1 is reported and the user agrees
SGR threshold (`geifman2017selective`); ISA (`zhang2023abstain`); SelectiveNet (`geifman2019selectivenet`); Deep Gamblers (`ziyin2019deepgamblers`); evidence gate (`mohammadpourazari2026safe`); CSA-style calibration (`khosravi2026csa`) and A-RCPS (`xu2024arcps`); ACI / online CRC (`gibbs2021aci`, `angelopoulos2022crc`); PPO / DQN agents; DEUP-style error predictor. Each needs its source re-read before implementation. The reviewer's priority (cheap controls and the checks of §10.2, §12.1 before any neural / RL baseline) is adopted.

### 6.3 Tuning: rules, and what is and is not claimed (rewritten: P6)
1. Same information cut-offs, purge, embargo and deployment algorithm (§4.3) for every method.
2. **Search.** Every learned method gets T = 30 random-search configurations (PLACEHOLDER) from a search space committed to the repository before the first trial; methods with fewer meaningful configurations (SCORE: none; CONF, VOL, LIQ, TREND, PERF, VT: small grids) are searched exhaustively over at most T points.
3. **One selection criterion for all methods:** mean, over the two tuning years and five seeds, of the per-order statistic θ_sel of §8.3 at the target coverage for within-batch methods, and of the annualised arithmetic return AR (§8.1) for all-or-nothing / scaling methods (PLACEHOLDER; frozen at Gate 2). Validation log-loss is **not** a common criterion (it is undefined for regressors and rule filters) and is logged only as a diagnostic.
4. Seeds {0,…,4} for every stochastic method; none dropped. No method-specific rescue.
5. **What "equal budget" does not establish, stated in the paper:** equal trial counts are not equal optimisation effort or equal coverage of search spaces of different size. Two-stage systems use a tuned base model plus a tuned gate (the base is tuned once and shared by all top-K methods); NET-U has one tuned stage. The return-rank base objective plus a loss-classification gate differs from NET in objective as well as in architecture; NET-K exists to hold the opportunity set fixed, not to equalise objectives.
6. **Reported for every method:** the search space, the number of configurations actually distinct, CPU time of tuning and of one refit, the chosen configuration, and the validation score distribution over trials.
7. **Tuning-budget sensitivity (development years only, exploratory):** the whole tuning stage repeated with T in {10, 30, 60} (PLACEHOLDER) and NET-U additionally with 2T; reported: whether the ordering of methods on the development evaluation years changes. Never run on the hold-out. **Also (v0.5, M-S11):** the late-tuning sensitivity of §4.3 (validation years {2018, 2019}, evaluation 2020–2021) for CONF, LOSS(−Z), LOSS(+Z_H), LOSS(+Z_O). Both sensitivities are run **after** the status of C1 / C2 has been recorded (§10.1), because they produce gate outcomes.
8. Trial logs are kept in `experiments/results/<name>/tuning/`.

---

## 7. Factorial design for Z_H and Z_O (rewritten: Z5, P8)

### 7.1 Cells
Base predictor feature set b ∈ {−Z, +Z_H, +Z_O}; gate (LOSS) feature set g ∈ {−Z, +Z_H, +Z_O, +Z_H+Z_O}. **Confirmatory cells use b = −Z only**, so all three gates choose from the same nominated lists:

| Cell | Base | Gate | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| (−,−) | −Z | LOSS(−Z) | reference of both Z contrasts; also card C2 |
| (−,H) | −Z | LOSS(+Z_H) | **confirmatory, card C3** |
| (−,O) | −Z | LOSS(+Z_O) | **confirmatory, card C4** |
| (−,HO) | −Z | LOSS(+Z_H+Z_O) | exploratory: are the groups substitutes or complements in the gate |
| (H,−), (O,−) | +Z_H / +Z_O | LOSS(−Z) | exploratory: group in the base predictor. The gate sees the +Z base score, so "gate without Z" means "no direct Z features", not "no Z information"; nominated lists differ from the reference |
| (H,H), (O,O) | +Z | LOSS(+same group) | exploratory |
| N-K, N-U | — | NET-K / NET-U with −Z, +Z_H, +Z_O | exploratory: one-stage selection |
| Ungated(+Z_H), Ungated(+Z_O) vs Ungated(−Z) | — | — | exploratory: cross-sectional return information with no abstention |

### 7.2 Contrasts
| Contrast | Question | Status |
|---|---|---|
| (−,H) − (−,−) | Does adding Z_H to the gate change within-batch selection at fixed nominations? | **Confirmatory (C3)** |
| (−,O) − (−,−) | Same for Z_O | **Confirmatory (C4)** |
| (−,HO) − (−,H) − (−,O) + (−,−) | Interaction of the two groups in the gate | Exploratory |
| (H,−) − (−,−), (O,−) − (−,−) | Group in the base predictor (changes nominations; Q-total and descriptive only) | Exploratory |
| Fixed-nomination placement (§7.3 b) | Same information in the score versus as gate features, nominations held fixed | Exploratory |
| NET-K(+Z) − NET-K(−Z); NET-K − LOSS at the same feature set | One-stage value of the group; architecture at matched opportunities | Exploratory |
| All of the above with R_H-hist or R_O removed | How much of an apparent effect the stronger controls absorb — a warning about weaker controls, not evidence for Z | Exploratory |

### 7.3 Controls required by P8 — applied to both groups
(a) **Matched-window history controls** R_H-hist and R_O are in every feature set (§5.4).
(b) **Fixed-nomination placement experiment.** Nominations from base(−Z); gate LOSS(−Z) receives, as one extra feature, the within-day rank score of the base(+Z_H) (resp. +Z_O) predictor. Compared with (−,H) (resp. (−,O)) and with (−,−): the group's information routed through a score versus given to the gate directly, on identical nominated lists.
(c) **Capacity-matched nuisance control.** LOSS(+perm Z_x): the gate receives the Z_x columns after an independent permutation across the nominated-training population within each day (for Z_O, within day and class, so that the number of events per day is preserved), re-drawn per seed. Same number of features, marginal distributions and missingness as Z_x, no stock-specific content. Reported: θ_sel of LOSS(+perm Z_x) minus that of LOSS(−Z). If this nuisance contrast is itself distinguishable from zero, the corresponding confirmatory contrast is reported with the warning that feature count / tuning, not content, may drive it. **Since v0.5 the same construction, re-drawn in every replication, is also a mandatory null world (W-Zperm-H, W-Zperm-O, §10.2); the real-data contrast here stays exploratory (one draw per seed).**
(d) **Residualisation** only with regressions fitted on training data (§5.5).

### 7.4 Interpretation rules fixed in advance
- A contrast is described as "adding the group to the gate of this pipeline changed / did not detectably change within-batch selection, given controls R". It is not described as evidence about private information, about a game, or about arbitrary uses of the raw history.
- An effect present under Q-total but not under Q-select is reported as an exposure effect. An effect present only when a control set is removed is reported as "not incremental to the controls".
- For Z_O, full-sample and event-sub-sample results are shown side by side (§5.6.5).

---

## 8. Estimands (rewritten: P1, P2, P3)

Three different quantities are defined and never interchanged. A = 242 trading days per year is a fixed annualisation constant (convention).

### 8.1 Deployed-strategy outcomes (Q-total)
For a feasible strategy a with wealth W_t^a at closes (W_0 common) and daily return r_t^a = W_t^a / W_{t−1}^a − 1:
- **(D-wealth) terminal wealth ratio** TW^a = W_T^a / W_0 and **CAGR^a** = (TW^a)^{A/T} − 1. Descriptive; block-bootstrap intervals from resampled daily returns, compounded. **Not decomposed and not used in any test.**
- **(D-add) annualised arithmetic return** AR^a = (A/T) Σ_t r_t^a. Additive over days; its scale does not drift as wealth compounds. This is the Q-total statistic: ΔAR = AR^G − AR^{comparator}, comparators G0, E-const-dev (feasible), SCORE, VT.
- Q-total compares **feasible strategies only**, with the same initial capital, execution rules, cost model and dates. Q-total contrasts are descriptive (unadjusted intervals); none is in the confirmatory family.

### 8.2 Additive attribution of ΔAR (Lemma 3 on two intervals per day)
- Wealth-normalised holdings h̃_t^{a,•} = h_t^{a,•} / W_{t−1}^a for • ∈ {on, id} (§3.9), exposures e_t^{a,•} = 1'h̃_t^{a,•} (invested fraction), weights w_t^{a,•} = h̃_t^{a,•} / e_t^{a,•}, cost rate c̃_t^a = c_t^a / W_{t−1}^a. Then r_t^a = (h̃_t^{a,on})'x_t^{on} + (h̃_t^{a,id})'x_t^{id} − c̃_t^a exactly, and Lemma 3 applied to the 2T intervals gives
  Σ_t (r_t^G − r_t^B) = Σ_{t,•} (e_t^{G,•} − e_t^{B,•}) (w_t^{B,•})'x_t^{•} [exposure-schedule] + Σ_{t,•} e_t^{G,•} (w_t^{G,•} − w_t^{B,•})'x_t^{•} [aggregate composition] − Σ_t (c̃_t^G − c̃_t^B) [cost].
  Multiplying by A/T gives a split of ΔAR. Lemma 3's wealth reading uses the sufficient conditions W1–W4 (self-financing, total returns, all costs, zero cash return); the primary ledger is written to satisfy them and this is **tested** by R2–R4, not assumed. Holdings, returns and costs follow benchmark-price accounting (§3.6: same price for all strategies at each instant, every cost once through the cost term, share-based trades, distributions in the cash ledger). With a non-zero cash return (robustness) the split is reported as "additive excess return as defined" and the ledger is checked against W_{T+1} − W_1 = Π + Σ_t ρ_t (W_t − c_t) (§3.6 item 6).
- **Conventions (fixed; Lemma 3 qualifications 1 and 5):** interaction booked under composition (reference h^E = e^G w^B); the other ordering as a labelled sensitivity. When e_t^{B,•} = 0: w_t^{B,•} = equal weight on the baseline's nominated lists of the cohorts it would hold in that interval; if there is none, 0. Days with zero baseline exposure are counted. **(v0.7, E-21.)** The nominated lists are strategy-layer information; the attribution function receives them through a callback. The convention is tested with real nomination lists once the strategy layer exists (so far only with stand-in lists).
- **The reference's notional cost** C^E is computed from the trades implied by the path h^E with the share-based traded-amount formula of §3.6 item 3 (pre-trade shares after corporate actions; cash distributions are not trades) and the same cost rates as every real strategy; h^E need not be feasible, and its trades are never executed (convention fixed in advance, I-§8.13). **(v0.7, E-7.)** The path h^E = e^G w^B changes at both instants of a day, also at the close, where no real strategy trades. `theorems.md` computes C^E from the share changes at the start of every period, and the periods here are the 2T intervals; C^E is therefore the sum of C^{E,open} (implied trades at opening instants, valued at OV) and C^{E,close} (implied rebalancing at closes, valued at CV), with the same cost rates. The two components are reported separately, with the note that the second has no counterpart in any executed strategy. The three-term split of ΔAR above uses the costs of G and B only; neither it nor any card depends on C^E.
- **"Aggregate composition" is not the Q-select estimand.** It contains every difference between w^G and w^B: within-batch selection, the mix of overlapping entry cohorts (which changes whenever a gate skips or thins a day), sector / size / liquidity tilts. It is reported descriptively under that name. A cohort-level version (identity applied per entry cohort and summed; exact by additivity) is exploratory, with the qualification of §3.9 for PERF.
- **Level / timing sub-split of the exposure-schedule term** (exploratory, ex post): with λ of E-const-expost, level = (λ − 1) Σ e^B (w^B)'x, timing = Σ (e^G − λ e^B)(w^B)'x.
- Not decomposed: CAGR, Sharpe ratio, drawdown, CVaR, loss frequency.

### 8.3 Within-batch selection at matched order opportunities (Q-select)
- **Standard order and its return.** For every nominated name i on day t, ρ_{ti} = net P&L, per yuan of slot cash, of one standard order of 1 yuan of slot cash placed by the rules of §3 (pre-determined quantity from G_t, synthetic fill rule SF, exit by LP with re-submission after non-filled exits, benchmark-price accounting with the full cost model, entitlement-based distributions). ρ_{ti} = 0 if the order is recorded as not filled (the yuan stays in cash). ρ_{ti} depends on the name and the day only, not on the gate or on any strategy's wealth: **a standardised cohort comparison at fixed initial yuan.**
- **End-of-window rule (added v0.5, M-S14).** The decision days that enter θ_sel, ψ_H, ψ_O end **H + 10 trading days before the last day of the window's data** (10 = the retry allowance of the deadline D = H + 10, U16; PLACEHOLDER U50), so that every counted order has its scheduled exit and ten further opening auctions inside the window. This applies to the hold-out (last data day 2026-06-30) **and to the development window (last data day 2021-12-31): exits of development-window orders are never valued with hold-out prices.** An order still unresolved at the last data day (an exit not filled for more than ten auctions) is valued at OV of the last data day, flagged and counted; if such orders exceed 0.5% of counted orders (PLACEHOLDER) the result is reported with and without them. The same cut-off applies to Q-total series used beside the cards. Day counts elsewhere in this plan (about 1,090 and 1,215) are before this cut of 15 days and are approximations in any case.
- **Matched order opportunities, not matched exposure.** The comparator has the same number of submitted orders, the same yuan per order and the same per-stratum counts on each day. It does **not** equalise invested exposure of deployed strategies (fills, blocked exits and sleeve wealth differ); the term "matched exposure" is not used for it.
- **Strata (PLACEHOLDER, fixed before results):** liquidity tercile × volatility tercile within the day's nominated list, from data through t−1.
- **Per-day excess.** With Sub_t^g the names gate g submits, S_t^g = |Sub_t^g|, S_{t,s}^g its count in stratum s, and ρ̄_{t,s} the mean of ρ over all nominated names of stratum s:
  u_t^g = Σ_{i ∈ Sub_t^g} ρ_{ti} − Σ_s S_{t,s}^g ρ̄_{t,s}.
  The second sum is the exact expectation of the first under a uniformly random choice of S_{t,s}^g names from each stratum, so no Monte-Carlo draw and no coarsening of strata is needed. u_t^g = 0 by construction on days with S_t^g ∈ {0, K}; such days carry no selection information and are counted. The same holds cell by cell: a stratum cell with a single nominated name, or one from which the gate submits all names or none, contributes exactly 0 to u_t while its orders count in the denominator. How much of the statistic is diluted in this way is measured by the choice share ι below and reported with every θ_sel.
- **Scale of the statistic: choice share and attainable range (added v0.5, M-R7; pre-specified reporting items, shown beside cards C1 and C2 and for every within-batch method).** With n_{t,s} the number of nominated names in stratum s on day t:
  - **Choice share** ι^g = Σ_t Σ_s S_{t,s}^g · 1{0 < S_{t,s}^g < n_{t,s}} / Σ_t S_t^g — the share of the gate's orders that come from a cell in which a different choice of the same size was possible. Also reported: the share of decision days with S_t^g ∈ {0, K}; the distribution of n_{t,s} (with K = 30 and nine cells, singleton and two-name cells are to be expected; their frequency is UNVERIFIED); θ_sel^g / ι^g (the excess per order with a real choice), labelled descriptive.
  - **Perfect-foresight benchmark at the gate's own counts** θ_PF^g = Σ_t Σ_s [sum of the S_{t,s}^g largest realised ρ in the cell − S_{t,s}^g ρ̄_{t,s}] / Σ_t S_t^g, and the mirror-image **worst-choice benchmark** θ_WF^g (the S_{t,s}^g smallest). [θ_WF^g, θ_PF^g] is the range θ_sel^g can take given the strata, the coverage and the per-cell counts the gate actually used. Neither is a strategy (both use realised outcomes); they calibrate the scale and nothing else.
  - **Gate-free version** θ_PF^prop, θ_WF^prop: the same with counts S_{t,s} = c·n_{t,s} rounded by the largest-remainder rule to ⌈cK⌉ orders per day. It needs no gate and is computed in `design_power` (§10.1) before any gate is fitted.
  - **SCORE** (top-⌈cK⌉ by base score): θ_sel^{SCORE} under the same strata and the same target coverage, with its unadjusted interval, ι and θ_PF.
  - **Reading rule, fixed now.** Δ_ord is reported as a fraction of θ_PF^g and next to θ_sel^{SCORE}. **If max(θ_PF^g, −θ_WF^g) < Δ_ord (the statistic could not have left the bound whatever the gate chose), an equivalence verdict for that gate is reported as "implied by the design", not as a finding about the gate.** The planted look-ahead gate of §13 is a pipeline check and is not used as a scale.
- **Statistic.** θ_sel^g = Σ_t u_t^g / Σ_t S_t^g over decision days of the evaluation window: the order-weighted mean net return of the gate's orders in excess of a stratified random choice from the same nominated list, in return units per order (reported in bps). A ratio of sums; inference resamples the pairs (u_t, S_t) by date (§10).
- **Translation to capital (descriptive only):** θ_sel × (realised coverage) × A/(H+1) approximates the annual additive contribution per unit of capital; at c = 0.5 and H = 5, 5 bps per order ≈ 1.0% per year.
- Variants (exploratory): per filled yuan instead of per ordered yuan; gross of costs; capital-weighted by actual slot cash; unstratified; fill-matched pool.
- Stated limitation: by construction this statistic gives no credit for avoiding whole days. Timing value is assessed only under Q-total and the exposure-schedule term.

### 8.4 Cards of the pre-specified tests

**Primary statistic of the paper: θ_sel (§8.3)** — for C1, C2 its level, for C3, C4 its difference between two gates. The descriptive metrics of §9.1 are not primary statistics of any test.

All four: hold-out window (decision days cut by the end-of-window rule of §8.3), primary H, K, c, cost model and conventions; seed-averaged series (u_t and S_t averaged over the five seeds); nominated lists from base(−Z); return ρ from simulated round trips — synthetic fill under SF, exit by LP (O2 exit), benchmark-price accounting; label O1 enters only as the LOSS training target; date-block bootstrap of §10; one Holm family. **All four cards are statements about the backtest under the synthetic execution model of §3.2; the fill-determination sensitivity (§3.2, §11) is reported beside them.** None of them uses a theory result.

**Status of C1 and C2 (added v0.5, M-R6).** C1 and C2 are written below as equivalence tests. Whether they are run as **confirmatory equivalence tests** or reported as **pre-declared estimation targets** is decided by the rule of §10.1, from a calculation that uses no gate output, and recorded before any gate is fitted on the development evaluation years. As estimation targets they have the same estimand, comparator, weighting and bootstrap; the point estimate and the 90% and 95% intervals are reported, Δ_ord is shown as a reference line, no p-value enters the Holm family and no "negligible" verdict is issued (§9.3).

**Card C1 — H-A1, CONF (as implemented in §6.1, tuned by §6.3)**
- *Estimand:* θ_sel^{CONF} (§8.3). *Comparator:* exact stratified-random expectation from the same nominated list, same per-stratum order counts. *Weighting:* per ordered yuan, order-weighted over days.
- *Null hypothesis (equivalence test):* H0: |θ_sel^{CONF}| >= Δ_ord, with Δ_ord the per-order equivalence bound (§9.3). *Alternative:* |θ_sel^{CONF}| < Δ_ord.
- *Test statistic:* t_L = (θ̂ + Δ_ord)/ŝe, t_U = (Δ_ord − θ̂)/ŝe; p_eq = max{1 − Φ(t_L), 1 − Φ(t_U)} (§10). *Labels used:* ρ from O2 exits.

- *Reported beside it (pre-specified, §8.3):* ι, θ_PF, θ_WF, θ_sel^{SCORE}, Δ_ord as a fraction of θ_PF.

**Card C2 — H-A2, LOSS(−Z) (as implemented in §6.1, tuned by §6.3)**
- As C1 with θ_sel^{LOSS(−Z)}. Additional label use: O1 as the classifier's training target (rows with an unfilled standard order dropped, §4.3).

**Card C3 — H-ZH, holder-state group in the gate**
- *Estimand:* ψ_H = θ_sel^{LOSS(+Z_H)} − θ_sel^{LOSS(−Z)}, both gates on the same nominated lists, each relative to its own exact stratified expectation. *Comparator:* LOSS(−Z), same model class, grid, budget, coverage target.
- *Null hypothesis (difference test, two-sided):* H0: ψ_H = 0. *Test statistic:* z = ψ̂_H / ŝe(ψ̂_H); p = 2(1 − Φ(|z|)). *Labels:* O1 for training both gates; ρ from O2 exits.

**Card C4 — H-ZO, other-participant-action group in the gate**
- As C3 with ψ_O = θ_sel^{LOSS(+Z_O)} − θ_sel^{LOSS(−Z)}, **full sample** from the later of the hold-out start and the Z_O analysis start (OA3). Included only if at least one Z_O class passes OA1–OA5 **and the signed date audit OA10** before modelling; the classes included are fixed at the audit. *Reported beside it (pre-specified, exploratory):* ψ_O with the INS class removed (§2.4, SD-O).

**Size of the confirmatory family (Holm at family-wise level 5%).** Fixed before any gate outcome is seen, by two recorded facts: the status of C1 / C2 (§10.1) and whether any Z_O class passed the audit.

| | C4 in (at least one Z_O class passed) | C4 dropped |
|---|---|---|
| C1, C2 confirmatory equivalence tests | m = 4 | m = 3 |
| C1, C2 pre-declared estimation targets | m = 2 (C3, C4) | m = 1 (C3) |

Nothing else is confirmatory: not Q-total, not the aggregate composition term, not the exposure-schedule or cost terms, not NET, SCORE, LOSS-LR, VOL, LIQ, PERF, TREND, VT, not the perfect-foresight benchmarks, not the event sub-sample, not any monitoring quantity.

**What v0.2 tested instead, and why it changed:** v0.2 put the *aggregate* Lemma 3 composition term (which mixes cohort mix and tilts with selection) into the H-A tests and four differently readable Z contrasts into the family (m = 6). The cards above test the within-batch quantity that Q-select is defined as.

---

## 9. Metrics (pre-specified)

### 9.1 Primary descriptive metrics
| Metric | Definition |
|---|---|
| P1 Net performance of the deployed strategy | **AR (annualised arithmetic return, §8.1) — the Q-total statistic**; terminal wealth ratio and CAGR beside it, never substituted for it |
| P2 Loss magnitude, trade level | Mean of min(net trade return, 0) and expected shortfall (mean of the worst 5% of net trade returns) over executed trades, **in three separate columns: resolved O2 returns only (with the count of unresolved trades); a "marked hybrid" (resolved at their exit, unresolved at the last mark) named as such; O1-marked returns.** Each in equal-weight and **capital-weighted** form. The pooled tail statistic is recomputed on each bootstrap resample of **date blocks of trade records**; it is never an average of daily tail statistics |
| P3 Maximum drawdown | Of the daily net wealth curve (empirical; interval labelled indicative only) |
| P4 Turnover | Annualised one-way traded amount (change in corporate-action-adjusted share count × benchmark price, §3.6 item 3; cash distributions are not trades) / average wealth; total cost paid |
| P5 Return of abstained and of unfilled orders | Mean ρ of nominated names the gate did not submit; separately, the paper outcome of submitted-but-unfilled orders (non-executable, descriptive) |

### 9.2 Secondary
θ_sel for every within-batch method; aggregate attribution terms (§8.2); loss fraction among executed trades (O1 and O2 separately), with its uncertainty computed for the ratio Σ_t X_t / Σ_t n_t by resampling the pairs (X_t, n_t); realised submission and executed coverage; invested fraction; synthetic fill rate by reason ((S1), (S2), (S3)), shown next to every loss rate; number of entries per O1 branch (S-d, S-e, P1–P4) and of outcomes U and M; Theorem 1 interval over time for the synthetic fill stream — form (b) primary, forms (a) and (c) beside it, δ = 0.10 (PLACEHOLDER); bracket of Cor. 1.2; Cor. 1.3 interval with the count of uncertified records (`草稿`; conditional on review); P_T; §9.5 diagnostics; Sharpe and Sortino ratios; CVaR(5%) of daily returns; holding time including non-filled exits (as defined by SF); share with O1 ≠ O2; base RankIC series; participation rate at a notional W_0 (PLACEHOLDER 10 million yuan).

### 9.3 Economic equivalence bounds and wording (rewritten: P10)
- **Bound for cards C1, C2 (rewritten v0.5, M-R7): investor-anchored rule only.** Δ_ord = Δ_cap × (H + 1) / (A × c), where **Δ_cap is the smallest additive annual return difference per unit of capital that would change the user's decision between a gate and a random choice of the same orders** — a number the user supplies at Gate 2 (§17 U3). **PLACEHOLDER: Δ_cap = 1.0% per year**, which gives Δ_ord = 4.96 ≈ 5 bps per order at H = 5, c = 0.5, A = 242 (arithmetic of §8.3). The placeholder is not a recommendation.
  - *Why the cost-anchored rule of v0.4 is withdrawn.* The gate's orders and the random comparator pay the same cost rates, so an error in the assumed round-trip cost largely cancels in the excess θ_sel; "smaller than a plausible cost error" therefore does not define a tolerance for this statistic (review item R1-W4).
  - *Why the bound is not set as a fraction of the attainable range.* A fraction of a perfect-foresight value has no economic meaning of its own, and the range depends on development data. The attainable range is instead a **pre-specified reporting item** (§8.3: ι, θ_PF, θ_WF, θ_sel^{SCORE}), and Δ_ord is always printed as a fraction of θ_PF so that a reader can judge whether the statistic could have exceeded it. The capital translation counts all orders, including those from cells without a choice, so the dilution of §8.3 is part of what the investor-anchored number means and is not a separate distortion of the bound.
