  - *Order of events.* Δ_cap (hence Δ_ord) is fixed at Gate 2, **before** `design_power` is run; it is not changed after `design_power`, after `power_precheck` or at any later time. What `design_power` decides is not the bound but whether C1 / C2 are run as tests at that bound (§10.1).
- **Equivalence p-value:** p_eq = max of the two one-sided p-values (cards C1, C2). It enters the Holm procedure together with the two difference-test p-values of C3, C4. A (1 − 2α)-interval inside the bound is an *individual* level-α TOST and is **not** by itself a family-adjusted verdict; intervals are shown at the unadjusted level and at the level implied by the test's Holm step, both labelled.
- **Wording, binding for RESULTS.md and the paper:**
  - "economically negligible (within ±Δ)" — **only** when the equivalence null is rejected after the Holm adjustment;
  - "a difference was detected" — only when a difference null is rejected after adjustment (direction then reported as estimated);
  - "no detectable difference; inconclusive" — in every other case. "Z adds nothing", "no incremental value" and similar are not used for a failure to reject.
  - **If C1 / C2 are estimation targets (§10.1):** only "the estimate of θ_sel was … with a 95% interval of …; the pre-set reference ±Δ_ord lies inside / across / outside this interval". The words "negligible", "equivalent", "no selection value" and "contradicted" are not used.
  - **Scope clause, mandatory in every verdict sentence (added v0.5, M-S3):** "… for [gate] as implemented here, on the universe and hold-out period actually used (as planned in v0.6: the liquidity-screened universe U-LIQ of §2.3 — or, if the user chooses the fallback, vendor-reported CSI 300 ∪ CSI 500 membership — 2022-01 to 2026-06), with one price–volume base signal, long-only, daily, in a backtest under the synthetic execution model". A verdict may not be stated without it, and may not be generalised to other gates, markets, periods or to executable trading.
- **An estimate outside the bound is not a verdict (M-S12).** v0.4 said that an adjusted interval lying entirely outside ±Δ_ord "contradicts H-A". No test in the family has the null "|θ_sel| ≤ Δ_ord", so that statement had no controlled error rate. It is now **descriptive**: "the estimate and its unadjusted 95% interval lie outside ±Δ_ord", reported as evidence against H-A1 / H-A2 in plain words, without a verdict label and without the word "confirmed". (A controlled alternative — adding the reverse test per card by a partition argument — is described in §16.2 item 9 and is not adopted.)
- Exploratory bounds for other statistics (ΔAR within ±1.0% per year; expected shortfall within ±25 bps) are shown with unadjusted TOST results and labelled exploratory.
- Operational form of H-A1 / H-A2: for CONF and LOSS(−Z) as implemented here, the outcome of C1, C2 (verdict or estimate, by their status) with the scale items of §8.3; next to them, **descriptively and under RQ-3**, ΔAR versus G0 and E-const-dev with the three attribution terms. The descriptive terms are not evidence for or against H-A1 / H-A2. PERF and TREND (no within-batch selection by construction): Q-total and exposure-schedule terms only, descriptive.

### 9.4 Expected width of the monitoring interval (ESTIMATE — formulas of theorems.md evaluated; no data; re-evaluated for v0.4: **unchanged**)

**Inputs (assumptions).** One sleeve buys per day, at most K = 30 orders: M = 30; n_t = 30 (synthetic fill rate set to 1; UNVERIFIED) or 15 at c = 0.5. **d_label = d = H + 1** (fixed, §3.3), label O1 by the tree of §3.8 (iii). Days (242 per year; calendar counts UNVERIFIED): hold-out ≈ 1,090; development evaluation 2017–2021 ≈ 1,215; a monitor run continuously over both ≈ 2,305. N ≈ 32,700 executed (synthetic) entries in the hold-out (ungated). **What the interval is about:** the target r_T^entry of the synthetic fill stream under SF, filtration F_t^syn (§3.1–3.2), not an executable strategy.

**What changed against v0.3 (T1–T3): nothing in the inputs, so the numbers are unchanged.** d = H + 1 and M = K = 30 are as before; the introduction of d_label = H + 1 changes when labels are frozen, not the lanes; the assumed N rests on a fill rate of 1, as before. The formulas were re-evaluated in a throwaway script for this revision and reproduce every cell of the table below. Two things could move the *realised* widths, neither of which is estimable now: the capacity cap (S3) and the fill-determination variants of §3.2 lower n_t (at constant n_t the half-width hardly depends on its level: 0.219 / 0.173 at n_t = 15 against 0.221 / 0.175 at n_t = 30; irregular n_t widens it, see the all-or-nothing line below). **Deleted in v0.4:** the information row "d = H = 5" and the remark that d = H would be valid under OA-open — O1 as defined does not support lag H (§3.3; I-§4).

**Formula.** Half-width B_T/N_T (form (a)) or B*_T/N_T (form (b)), B_T = Σ_j b(Q_{j,T}), Q_{j,T} = Σ_{t ≡ j (mod d)} n_t², b(q) = sqrt(2^{⌊log₂ q⌋} L_k), b*(q) = min_k (1/2) sqrt(2^k L_k)(1 + q/2^{k+1}), L_k = log(2 d c_0 (k+1)²/δ). With constant n_t the half-width depends on T and d, not on K.

**Estimated half-widths, δ = 0.10, constant n_t = 30, form (a) / form (b).**

| H (d = H + 1) | Hold-out, T ≈ 1,090 | Development evaluation, T ≈ 1,215 | Continuous 2017–2026H1, T ≈ 2,305 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (2) | 0.099 / 0.096 | 0.127 / 0.091 | 0.067 / 0.066 |
| **5 (6), primary** | **0.221 / 0.175** | **0.198 / 0.167** | **0.149 / 0.121** |
| 10 (11) | 0.293 / 0.243 | 0.263 / 0.231 | 0.197 / 0.169 |
| 20 (21) | 0.404 / 0.345 | 0.363 / 0.325 | 0.272 / 0.238 |

- Evaluated with a throwaway script (not in the repository; this revision may change only this file); **`synth_validation` must reproduce the table as its first output.** **Requirement added in v0.5 (M-S16):** before Gate-2 numbers are relied on, every planning number of this file — this table, the multipliers and the grid of §10.1, the binomial limits of §10.2 and of the signed date audits — is recomputed by scripts committed under `experiments/src/planning/` (first task of the implementation stage; not done here because this revision may change only this file). The arithmetic added in v0.5 was evaluated in a scratch script outside the project.
- n_t = 15 instead of 30 changes the primary cells by at most 0.003 (the small change comes from the dyadic epochs of the radius). δ = 0.05: 0.228 / 0.180 in the primary hold-out cell.
- All-or-nothing stream (n_t = 30 on a random half of the days; five simulated patterns): primary hold-out cell 0.31–0.33 / 0.24–0.25.
- Form (b) over time at the primary setting: half-width >= 0.5 until about day 128, >= 0.25 until about day 525, 0.175 at day 1,090. Pending-label term: P_T/N_T ≈ d/T ≈ 0.006 (under A5).
- Corollary 1.1's closed-form bound at the primary hold-out setting: 0.25. Proposition 2's lower bound on the full width in its block environment with these (d, M, N): about 0.026, against a full width of about 0.36 for form (b); the gap is constants and logarithms and is not shown to be necessary.

**Consequences (unchanged in substance from v0.2).**
1. Total width at the end of the hold-out about 0.36 (form (b)) to 0.45 (form (a)); wider at every earlier date; [0,1] for about the first half-year of a run.
2. **The interval is not used to compare gates, to rank methods, or in any test.** No claim of the form "the gate lowers the monitored loss probability" will rest on it.
3. Its role: (a) how much a daily trade stream can say without dependence assumptions (with Proposition 2 as the worst-case reference); (b) a validity contrast with the matured-only Wald interval and with valid comparators (§12.1); (c) bookkeeping through Cor. 1.2.
4. Positioning consequence for the lead / user: an appendix-level, "limits of evidence" role.
5. Realised widths are computed from the ledger and reported whatever they are.

### 9.5 Dependence diagnostic (experiment `dependence_diag`; corrected: P11)
**Purpose.** To describe the dependence of the O1 labels on the real stream. Independence (N effective trades) and Proposition 2's block environment (N/(Md)) are **two reference scenarios, not bounds between which the empirical value must fall**.
**Quantities.**
1. *Same-day dependence:* one-way random-effects intraclass correlation of the labels with days as groups, **on the raw labels (not day-demeaned)**, using the ANOVA estimator for unequal group sizes; overall and by year. Day-level over-dispersion of X_t given n_t.
2. *Cross-day dependence:* autocorrelation of X_t/n_t at lags 1..2d; label correlation between entries with overlapping versus non-overlapping holding windows.
3. *Effective sample size:* N_eff = p̂(1 − p̂)/V̂ with p̂ = Σ X_t / Σ n_t and V̂ the variance of that **ratio**, from a date-block bootstrap of the pairs (X_t, n_t) at block lengths {d, 2d, 4d, 8d} (shown as a function of block length) and from a delta-method long-run variance (Bartlett kernel) as a cross-check.
4. *Hypothetical width* z·sqrt(p̂(1 − p̂)/N_eff), labelled as not valid, next to the Theorem 1 half-width.
**Limits printed with the results.** Time variation of μ_t inflates measured dependence and cannot be separated from it; the diagnostic cannot certify a restricted dependence class and **licenses no narrower interval**; block-bootstrap variances assume approximate stationarity. No size or direction is predicted.

---

## 10. Statistical inference

- **Unit of resampling: the trading (decision) date.** Every statistic is a function of date-indexed records — daily returns r_t, per-day attribution contributions, pairs (u_t, S_t), pairs (X_t, n_t), or the day's trade records for pooled trade-level statistics — so within-day dependence is preserved. Trade-level quantities are attached to their entry date; individual trades are never resampled.
- **Method:** paired stationary block bootstrap over dates, applied jointly to all strategies' records, 10,000 resamples (PLACEHOLDER). Ratio statistics (θ_sel, loss fractions) are recomputed as ratios of resampled sums. Path-dependent metrics are recomputed on each resampled path.
- **Block length (corrected: P10).** Mean block length ℓ(H) = 4(H + 1) trading days: 8, **24**, 44, 84 for H = 1, **5**, 10, 20 (PLACEHOLDER). Sensitivity: 2(H + 1) and 8(H + 1) (12 and 48 at H = 5). Every length used satisfies the stated minimum 2(H + 1) for its H; v0.2's fixed set {10, 20, 40, 60} did not. At H = 20 the hold-out contains about 13 blocks of mean length 84: intervals for H = 20 are labelled unreliable.
- **Standard errors and p-values.** ŝe = bootstrap standard deviation of the statistic; one-sided and two-sided p-values from the normal approximation with ŝe (cards, §8.4); percentile intervals are shown beside them. Whether this procedure has its nominal error rates on this kind of data is **not assumed**; it is checked in §10.2, and the procedure may be changed there (before the hold-out) if it fails.
- **Limitations, to be written into the paper:** block bootstrap assumes approximate stationarity and weak dependence beyond the block length, which regime shifts and rule changes violate; drawdown intervals are indicative only; there is one realised market history.
- **Random seeds.** Five seeds for every stochastic component. Seed variation measures optimisation and sampling randomness only; mean ± standard deviation over seeds is reported in its own column and never merged with a bootstrap interval. All seeds are shown; the seed-averaged series is the unit of bootstrap inference.
- **Multiple comparisons.**
  - **Confirmatory family: the pre-specified tests of §8.4 (C1, C2 equivalence, if their status is "confirmatory"; C3, C4 difference). Holm step-down on their p-values (p_eq for C1, C2) at family-wise level 5%; m ∈ {4, 3, 2, 1} by the table of §8.4,** fixed before any gate outcome is seen.
  - Everything else is exploratory: labelled, reported in full, unadjusted intervals. A data-snooping-robust comparison across all methods (reality-check / SPA type) is optional; references not in the bibliography.
- The hold-out confirmatory analysis is run once. Development and hold-out results are never pooled for a confirmatory test. (Bootstrap references — Politis & Romano; Künsch — are not in `paper/references.bib`; literature-scout must add them before they are cited.)

### 10.1 Design-stage variance calculation and the status of C1, C2 (experiment `design_power`; added v0.5, M-R6)

**Purpose.** To state, before any gate is fitted on the development evaluation years, what standard error θ_sel can have in the hold-out, what the smallest equivalence bound with 80% power is, and — by a rule that leaves no discretion — whether C1 and C2 are run as confirmatory equivalence tests or reported as pre-declared estimation targets. **"Development outcome"** means any number that depends on the decisions of a gate, filter or comparator method on the development evaluation years (θ_sel, ψ, ΔAR, their standard errors). Nothing in this section uses one.

**Reference model.** A selector without skill that, on day t, submits S_{t,s} names drawn at random from the n_{t,s} nominated names of stratum s. Conditionally on the realised outcomes, u_t has mean 0 and (variance of a sum sampled without replacement)

  V_t = Σ_s S_{t,s} (1 − S_{t,s}/n_{t,s}) σ²_{t,s},  σ²_{t,s} = (n_{t,s} − 1)^{−1} Σ_{i in cell} (ρ_{ti} − ρ̄_{t,s})²  (0 if n_{t,s} = 1).

Write Var(Σ_t u_t) = κ Σ_t V_t. κ = 1 exactly if the selector draws independently every day. A real gate's choices persist from day to day while the H-day outcomes of a name on neighbouring days overlap, so consecutive u_t are dependent and κ is not 1; its value is not known in advance. Then

  SE(θ̂_sel) ≈ sqrt(κ Σ_t V_t) / Σ_t S_t,  and with proportional counts S_{t,s} = c·n_{t,s}:  **SE = σ_w · sqrt(κ (1 − c) / (c K T))**,  σ_w² = (K T)^{−1} Σ_t Σ_s n_{t,s} σ²_{t,s}

(σ_w = the size-weighted within-day, within-stratum dispersion of the standard order's net return among nominated names). At the primary c = 0.5, K = 30 and T ≈ 1,090 hold-out days: SE ≈ 0.00553 × σ_w × sqrt(κ) (0.00557 with the 15-day cut of §8.3).

**Smallest bound with 80% power.** Normal approximation, true effect zero, level α/m at the strictest Holm step: Δ_80 = k_m × SE with k_m = z_{1−α/m} + z_{0.90} = 2.93 (m = 1, i.e. unadjusted), 3.24 (m = 2), 3.41 (m = 3), 3.52 (m = 4). Hence **Δ_80 = k_m · σ_w · sqrt(κ (1 − c) / (c K T))**.

**Arithmetic grid (formula evaluated at c = 0.5, K = 30, T = 1,090; not data, and no column is claimed to be the real one).**

| σ_w · sqrt(κ), bps | 200 | 300 | 400 | 600 | 800 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SE, bps per order | 1.11 | 1.66 | 2.21 | 3.32 | 4.42 |
| Δ_80, unadjusted (k = 2.93), bps | 3.2 | 4.9 | 6.5 | 9.7 | 12.9 |
| Δ_80, strictest step of m = 4 (k = 3.52), bps | 3.9 | 5.8 | 7.8 | 11.7 | 15.6 |

Read the other way: Δ_ord = 5 bps has 80% power only if σ_w · sqrt(κ) is at most about 257 bps (m = 4) or 309 bps (unadjusted). The methodology reviewer's indicative figure (dispersion 4%, κ = 1, unstratified, hence SE ≈ 2.2 bps) is the 400 column; the reviewer labelled it an assumption, and so does this plan. **This plan states no value of σ_w or κ and therefore no power.**

**Inputs.**

| Input | Available when | Note |
|---|---|---|
| c, K, H, A, α, the multipliers k_m | now (design; values are the placeholders of §17) | fixed at Gate 2 |
| Δ_cap, hence Δ_ord | Gate 2 (user) | before any data |
| m (4 or 3) | after the Z_O audit | §8.4 table |
| T_ho | when the trading calendar is loaded | about 1,090 − 15; UNVERIFIED |
| cell sizes n_{t,s} | development data, no gate | from the base predictor's nominated lists and the strata rule (U14) |
| within-cell dispersion σ²_{t,s} of ρ over **all** nominated names; fill rate (through ρ = 0 for unfilled orders) | development data, no gate | needs prices, rule SF, the cost model |
| κ | not available without a gate | bracketed by the two gate-free references below |
| dispersion in the hold-out relative to the development years | never, before the hold-out is opened | assumed equal; stated as an assumption |

Nothing that determines σ_w can be bounded before the development data are loaded, and no bound is offered. What is known in advance is the functional form: the standard error falls with sqrt(K T) and rises with σ_w and sqrt(κ).

**Two gate-free references for κ (Monte-Carlo on the development evaluation years, conditional on the realised outcomes; R_DS = 2,000 draws, PLACEHOLDER).** Each name i receives a latent score z_{i,t} = φ z_{i,t−1} + sqrt(1 − φ²) ε_{i,t} with independent standard normal ε; on each day the selector takes, in each stratum, the names with the highest z, with counts c·n_{t,s} rounded by the largest-remainder rule. SE_φ = standard deviation over draws of Σ_t u_t / Σ_t S_t. φ = 0 is the independent-draw case (it must reproduce the closed form with κ = 1: a code check). φ ∈ {0.9, 0.99, 1} are persistent selectors (φ = 1: one random ranking of names kept for the whole window). **SE_lo := SE_{φ=0}; SE_hi := the largest SE_φ on the grid.** Both are scaled to the hold-out by sqrt(T_dev / T_ho).

**Outputs of `design_power` (none is a development outcome).** σ_w by year; the distribution of n_{t,s}; SE_lo and SE_hi; **Δ_80^lo = k_m · SE_lo and Δ_80^hi = k_m · SE_hi — the range in which the smallest bound with 80% power lies under the reference model**; the reference power at Δ_ord under both; θ_PF^prop, θ_WF^prop and Δ_ord / θ_PF^prop (§8.3).

**Status rule for C1 and C2 (PLACEHOLDER rule, §17 U45; to be confirmed by the user at Gate 2, before any data are loaded; afterwards applied mechanically).**
- If **Δ_ord ≥ Δ_80^hi**: C1 and C2 are **confirmatory equivalence tests** in the Holm family.
- **Otherwise**: C1 and C2 are **pre-declared estimation targets** (§8.4, §9.3); the family is C3 (and C4).
- k_m uses the m the family would have with C1 and C2 in it (4, or 3 if C4 was dropped at the audit).
- *Why SE_hi.* A confirmatory equivalence test is kept only if it has 80% reference power even when choices are as persistent as the grid allows; the price is that a test which would have had adequate power under a less persistent gate may be given up. Alternatives the user may choose at Gate 2 instead, all before any data: (i) SE_lo in place of SE_hi (C1 / C2 stay confirmatory more often, possibly with low power, and are then labelled "low reference power"); (ii) a 50% threshold in place of 80% (k_4 = 2.92 in place of 3.52); (iii) a design with a smaller standard error — a larger nominated list or a longer window (in the symmetric formula K = 50 instead of 30 multiplies SE by 0.77; this changes U6 and everything that depends on it).
- **The status is recorded by the lead in `STATUS.md` and is not revisited** — not after the pilot, `power_precheck`, `null_pipeline` or any development outcome. Δ_ord is never changed after Gate 2.
- The researcher does not know which branch will result and makes no prediction.

**Timing and blinding.** `design_power` runs after `base_strategy` has produced the nominated lists and ρ for 2017–2021 and **before** `gates_round1`, `z_factorial`, `power_precheck`, `null_pipeline` or the tuning sensitivities are run on those years. The pilot (§13) runs earlier, on another panel, and contains real gates: its report therefore **does not show θ_sel, ψ, ΔAR or their standard errors for any real gate** (they are written to a file that stays unopened until the status is recorded) and shows the gate-free quantities of this section for the pilot panel as a rough preview.

**Limits.** (i) A reference model, not the test: a real gate may concentrate on names with more or less dispersion than average and may be more persistent than the grid, so its standard error can lie outside [SE_lo, SE_hi]. (ii) The references are conditional on one realised history. (iii) Development dispersion is assumed to carry over to the hold-out. (iv) Normal approximation.

### 10.1b Gate-based power report (experiment `power_precheck`; development evaluation years only; reported before the freeze; **changes no status, bound or family**)
For each card, on 2017–2021, with the frozen algorithm and the **same statistic and the same bootstrap as the test**:
1. Compute the statistic's bootstrap standard error SE_dev at block lengths 2(H+1), 4(H+1), 8(H+1); use the largest.
2. Predict SE_ho = SE_dev × sqrt(T_dev / T_ho) (T_dev ≈ 1,215, T_ho ≈ 1,090; ESTIMATES of day counts). For C4, T_ho and T_dev are counted from the Z_O start date, and the share q of orders on which the two gates differ is reported (§5.6.5).
3. Normal-approximation requirements, by arithmetic: equivalence with 80% power at a true effect of zero needs Δ_ord >= 2.93 SE_ho unadjusted and 3.52 SE_ho at the strictest Holm step (α/4); the minimum detectable difference at 80% power is 2.80 SE_ho unadjusted and 3.34 SE_ho at the strictest step.
4. Report per card: SE_ho, minimum detectable effect, the smallest bound with 80% equivalence power, and the power at the bound of §17.

Illustration by arithmetic only: with Δ_ord = 5 bps, 80% equivalence power needs SE_ho <= 1.7 bps per order (1.4 bps at the strictest step of m = 4). The actual standard error is UNVERIFIED; the design-stage reference for it is §10.1.

Limits: assumes development-period dependence and volatility carry over; normal approximation; the development years also informed the design (not the hyper-parameters, §4.3). A planning aid, not a result.

**What this report may and may not change (rewritten v0.5, M-R6).** v0.4 let the user choose, after seeing this report, between tests "with low prior power", estimation without verdicts, or more data, under the threshold "below 50% for most of the family". That choice would have been made by someone who had seen development estimates of the test statistics, and the threshold was vague (review item R1-W3). It is withdrawn. **This report changes nothing:** the status of C1 / C2 was fixed by §10.1 before it, the bound at Gate 2, the family by the table of §8.4. The report is printed in the paper beside the results (gate-based SE_ho, minimum detectable effects, power at Δ_ord, and how they compare with [SE_lo, SE_hi] of §10.1). A test whose gate-based power is below 50% (U18) is run as planned and its result carries the label "low prior power". **"Inconclusive" is a possible outcome of every test and is reported as such.**

### 10.2 Complete-null simulation of the full pipeline (experiment `null_pipeline`; **mandatory before the hold-out is opened**)

**Question.** Do tuning, calibration, thresholding, the matched-opportunity statistic, the block bootstrap and the Holm step, run end to end, reject true null hypotheses about as often as advertised?

**Data.** Development evaluation years 2017–2021 with their tuning years; real prices, real nominated lists, the frozen algorithm of §4.3 including the gate tuning stage, re-run inside every replication. The base predictor and hence the nominated lists are held fixed (all four cards are within-nomination statements); this is a stated limit of the simulation's scope. The hold-out is not used.

**Null worlds (rewritten v0.5, M-R4, M-R5).** Three kinds of world. "True value" below always refers to the numerator: θ_sel is a ratio Σ_t u_t / Σ_t S_t, and "0 by construction" means E[Σ_t u_t] = 0 exactly, so that the ratio-of-expectations value of θ_sel is 0; the finite-sample bias of the ratio estimator is part of what the simulation measures. All randomisation is conditional on the one realised price history.

- **W-swap (redefined; preserves all dependence of outcomes).** In each replication and **for each calendar year Y separately**, an independent uniformly random bijection π_Y of the names that are candidates at any time in year Y is drawn. For the dates of year Y, **the whole feature row that any gate sees** for name i — the −Z features, Z_H, Z_O **and the base score as a gate input, including the score that CONF calibrates and thresholds** — is replaced by the row of π_Y(i) (missing where the partner has no row on that date; LightGBM receives it as missing, the linear and isotonic steps by their stated imputation). **The real base score still determines the nominated lists, and real data determine the strata.** Outcomes, nominations, strata and their time-series dependence are untouched.
  - *Change 1 (review item R1-W1, accepted).* v0.4 left the base score in the gate's feature set. The gate could then still learn the relation between the within-list rank and the outcome, which is the open empirical question SCORE and CONF exist to answer; θ_sel was not zero by construction and the "+Δ" version did not sit on the boundary for C2.
  - *Change 2 (researcher's addition; not raised by the reviewers).* v0.4 used one bijection for the whole history. Swapped features then remain a persistent identifier of the stock, and a flexible gate fitted on earlier years can learn any name-level persistent difference in outcomes that the strata do not remove, and carry it forward. Swapping the score does not close that channel. With an independent bijection per calendar year it is closed: the model, calibration map and threshold in force in year Y are functions of data of earlier years and of earlier bijections only (§4.3), hence independent of π_Y; relabelling the names inside a (day, stratum) cell does not change the law of π_Y; so every name of a cell is submitted with the same probability, and E[u_t] = 0 **exactly**, for every LOSS gate and for CONF. (This is the plan's own argument and has not been reviewed.)
  - *What is preserved and what is not.* Preserved: every dependence among outcomes; the persistence of a gate's choices within a calendar year. Not preserved: persistence of choices across the five year boundaries of the simulated window; any relation between the gate's inputs and the outcomes.
  - True values: θ_sel = 0 for CONF and every LOSS gate, ψ_H = ψ_O = 0 — **by construction**.
- **W-perm (outcomes permuted).** Within each cell **day × (nominated / not nominated) × stratum** of the nominated-training population (cells made explicit in v0.5 so that the nominated names of one stratum are always permuted among themselves), the outcome records (ρ and the O1 label) are permuted among names, in training, calibration and evaluation data alike, independently across days. The day-t permutation is independent of the features and of the model in force (fitted on earlier years), so the outcomes attached to the submitted names of a cell are a uniform random draw from the cell: E[u_t] = 0 exactly. True values: θ_sel = 0 for every gate including CONF, ψ_H = ψ_O = 0 — **by construction**. Limitation: independent daily permutations break the dependence between a name's outcomes on neighbouring days, so this world may be more favourable to the bootstrap than reality; W-swap does not have this defect.
- **W-Zperm-H and W-Zperm-O (new, M-R5): the reference gate is informative, only the added group is not.** The −Z panel (including the real base score), the outcomes, the nominations and the strata are **real**. In W-Zperm-H the Z_H columns — in W-Zperm-O the Z_O columns — are permuted across the names of the nominated-training population **within each day** (for Z_O within day and class, the whole block of a class's features of a name moving together, so that the number of events per day and class is preserved), in training, calibration and evaluation data alike, independently across days, **re-drawn in every replication**. Gates: LOSS(−Z), which is the real gate and identical in every replication (fitted once), and LOSS(+permuted Z_x), refitted with its full tuning stage in every replication.
  - True values, stated as they are. **C1, C2:** θ_sel of CONF and of LOSS(−Z) are the real, unknown values; no null of C1 or C2 is instantiated, and this world is **not** in their pass criterion. **C3 (W-Zperm-H), C4 (W-Zperm-O):** the permuted columns carry no stock-specific information, but **ψ is not zero by construction**: the gate with the extra columns is a different fitted model (more inputs, same tuning budget), and that alone can change how it uses the real features — the capacity effect of §7.3 (c). The world's true value ψ_0^W (the expectation of ψ̂ over permutation draws, given the realised history) **has to be estimated**; it is estimated by the mean over replications, and the test is centred on it (pass criterion below).
  - What this world can and cannot show. Replications share one realised history and one reference gate; they differ only in the permutation. The check compares the bootstrap standard error of ψ̂ with the variability of ψ̂ over permutation draws, in the configuration in which C3 and C4 will be run (two informative gates that agree on most orders). It can show that the standard error is too small. It does **not** measure a rejection frequency over market histories. Within-day permutation also removes the day-to-day persistence of a name's Z values (the real Z_H features are persistent), so the set of orders on which the two gates differ is less persistent than under the realistic null — the feature-side analogue of W-perm's defect. A persistence-preserving variant (the Z block swapped by a per-year bijection, as in W-swap) would change the distribution of Z among nominated names; it is not included (§16.2 item 10).
- **Boundary versions W-swap+Δ, W-perm+Δ.** The per-order bound Δ_ord is added to ρ of every order submitted by a gate (u_t ← u_t + Δ_ord S_t), which puts θ_sel exactly on the boundary of the equivalence nulls of C1, C2 and leaves ψ_H, ψ_O at 0. Needs no additional fits. **In both boundary worlds all four nulls are true by construction** (v0.4: only C2–C4 in W-swap+Δ, and those not by construction). Used only if C1 / C2 are confirmatory (§10.1).

**True value of each card's parameter in each world.**

| World | C1: θ_sel^{CONF} | C2: θ_sel^{LOSS(−Z)} | C3: ψ_H | C4: ψ_O | In the pass criterion of |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W-perm | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | if C1 / C2 are estimation targets: C3, C4, and interval coverage for θ_sel of C1, C2. Otherwise: power by-product only |
| W-perm+Δ | Δ_ord, by construction (boundary) | Δ_ord, by construction (boundary) | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | if C1 / C2 are confirmatory: C1–C4 |
| W-swap | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | as W-perm |
| W-swap+Δ | Δ_ord, by construction (boundary) | Δ_ord, by construction (boundary) | 0, by construction | 0, by construction | as W-perm+Δ |
| W-Zperm-H | real value, unknown; not a null | real value, unknown; not a null | ψ_0^W: **to be estimated** (not 0 by construction) | — | C3 only, centred on the estimate |
| W-Zperm-O | real value, unknown; not a null | real value, unknown; not a null | — | ψ_0^W: **to be estimated** | C4 only, centred on the estimate |

"By construction" rests on the two exchangeability arguments given above, which are the plan's own and unreviewed; the simulation's code must include a direct check (the mean of Σ_t u_t over replications within Monte-Carlo error of 0 in W-perm and W-swap, reported).

**Degenerate replications.** In a null world a gate may submit all or none of the list on every day (for example CONF with an almost flat calibration map), giving u_t ≡ 0 and a bootstrap standard error of 0. Rule fixed now: tie-breaking for thresholds is written in the experiment's README before running; a replication with ŝe = 0 for a card counts as "no rejection" for that card and is tallied separately; if more than 20% of replications (PLACEHOLDER) are degenerate for a card in a world, that world is reported as **uninformative for that card** rather than as passed.

**Replications.** B_null = 400 (PLACEHOLDER) for each of W-perm, W-swap, W-Zperm-H, W-Zperm-O (the boundary versions reuse the fits of W-perm and W-swap), seeds fixed in advance; inside each replication the bootstrap uses 2,000 resamples (PLACEHOLDER; fewer than in the real analysis, stated).

**One seed versus the five-seed average (added v0.5, M-S9).** The cards use series averaged over five seeds; the simulation uses one seed per replication (five would multiply its cost by five). **The pass criterion therefore applies to the one-seed version of the inference procedure, and is reported as such.** Cross-check: B_5 = 50 replications (PLACEHOLDER) of W-swap run with five seeds and the seed-averaged series. Reported for the one-seed and the five-seed version: the ratio of the standard deviation of the statistic across replications to the mean bootstrap ŝe, and the rejection counts with exact intervals. With 50 replications the count check is weak (limit 5 of 50 at nominal 5%; a true rate of 10% is detected with probability about 0.38), and this is stated. **Pre-fixed consequence:** if the five-seed ratio exceeds 1.15 (PLACEHOLDER; ŝe understating the spread by more than 15%) while the one-seed ratio does not, the user is told before the hold-out is opened and the confirmatory series becomes **seed 0 alone** — the version of the procedure that was actually checked — with the five-seed average reported beside it.

**Recorded.** Per replication and card: the p-value; rejection at nominal 5% unadjusted and after Holm; coverage of the nominal 90% interval for the true value (for W-Zperm: for the estimate ψ̄^W of the true value). Across replications: per-test rejection rate, family-wise rate, each with an exact binomial (Clopper–Pearson) 95% interval; the mean of Σ_t u_t (construction check); the power of the equivalence tests at θ = 0 (from the worlds without Δ), as a by-product; the share of degenerate replications.

**Pass criterion (fixed now). All parts that apply must hold.**
- **(a) Worlds in which every null of the family is true.** If C1 / C2 are confirmatory: W-perm+Δ and W-swap+Δ, family C1–C4. If C1 / C2 are estimation targets: W-perm and W-swap, family C3 (and C4). In each such world: (i) the number of replications with at least one Holm-adjusted false rejection is **at most 27 of 400** — 28 or more is the smallest count that an exact one-sided binomial test at 5% treats as evidence that the family-wise rate exceeds 5%; (ii) the same count limit for each test's unadjusted rejections at nominal 5%.
- **(b) Interval coverage for estimation targets** (only if C1 / C2 are estimation targets): in W-perm and in W-swap the nominal 90% interval for θ_sel of CONF and of LOSS(−Z) fails to cover 0 in **at most 50 of 400** replications (51 is the smallest count that the same exact test treats as evidence of a non-coverage rate above 10%).
- **(c) Informative reference gate (C3 in W-Zperm-H, C4 in W-Zperm-O).** With ψ̄^W_{(−b)} the mean of ψ̂ over all replications other than b, the centred statistic z_b = (ψ̂_b − ψ̄^W_{(−b)}) / ŝe_b exceeds 1.96 in absolute value in **at most 27 of 400** replications. (Leave-one-out centring keeps a replication from being compared with a mean that contains it; with 400 replications the estimation error of the centre is one twentieth of the spread.)
- With B_null = 400 a count check of this kind detects a true rate of 10% with probability about 0.98 and a true rate of 8% with probability about 0.79 (binomial arithmetic); passing therefore does not establish a rate of at most 5%, it fails to find a rate that is clearly above it. For B_null = 200 the limit would be 15 (27 for the 10% check) and those probabilities about 0.86 and 0.54.

**Reported with (c) but not part of the pass rule: the uncentred test.** The number of replications in which the test of H0: ψ = 0, exactly as it will be run on the hold-out, rejects in W-Zperm-H / W-Zperm-O; and the standardised capacity effect ψ̄^W / (mean ŝe) with its Monte-Carlo standard error. If the centred check passes and the uncentred count exceeds 27 of 400, the inference procedure is not at fault: adding columns without stock-specific content moves the statistic, in this history, by an amount the test can detect. That is not a failed attempt in the sense of the next paragraph. **Pre-fixed consequence (§17 U52):** the user is told before the hold-out is opened and chooses between (α) making the capacity-matched gate LOSS(+permuted Z_x), averaged over a fixed number of permutation draws, the reference gate of C3 / C4 — a change of the registered estimand that must first be written into `research_question.md` — and (β) keeping ψ as defined, with the binding sentence "a detected difference may reflect the number of added inputs rather than their content" attached to any "difference detected" verdict and the nuisance contrast of §7.3 (c) printed beside it. If the user gives no instruction, (β).

**If part (a), (b) or (c) fails (rewritten v0.5, M-S10): a fixed ladder, at most three attempts, every attempt reported.**
- Attempt 1 — **P0**, the procedure of §10: normal approximation, ŝe from the stationary bootstrap with mean block length 4(H + 1).
- Attempt 2 — **P1**: as P0 with ŝe := the largest of the bootstrap standard errors at mean block lengths 2(H + 1), 4(H + 1), 8(H + 1).
- Attempt 3 — **P2**: P1's ŝe with Student-t quantiles in place of normal ones, degrees of freedom = (number of non-overlapping blocks of length 8(H + 1) in the evaluation window) − 1.
- The order and content of the ladder (PLACEHOLDER, §17 U49) are fixed now; no other procedure may be tried. Each new attempt uses **fresh replications** (new seeds, B_null = 400; 200 with limit 15 only if compute forces it, decided before the attempt). To make a lucky pass less likely, a procedure tried at attempt k must pass **both** on its fresh replications **and** on the pool of all replications generated so far (the stored series of earlier attempts are re-evaluated with it at no refitting cost): pooled limits 50 of 800 at attempt 2 and 73 of 1,200 at attempt 3 for the 5% checks (exact binomial; these detect a true rate of 8% with probability about 0.96 and 0.99).
- What remains, stated: a single attempt passes a procedure whose true rate is 8% about one time in five; the pooled rule lowers the chance of passing by repetition but does not remove it, and the later procedures are more conservative by design, which costs power. Every attempt, with its counts, goes into RESULTS.md and the paper.
- If attempt 3 fails, the user is told and chooses between reporting the hold-out as estimation without confirmatory verdicts, or stopping. The hold-out stays closed throughout. The procedure that passes is the one used for every card on the hold-out.

**Cost (ESTIMATE, unmeasured).** W-perm and W-swap: about 200 gate fits per replication (three LOSS gates × 30 configurations × 2 tuning years, plus 15 deployment refits; one seed) × 400 × 2 ≈ 160,000 fits. W-Zperm-H and W-Zperm-O: only the gate with the permuted group is refitted, about 65 fits per replication and group × 400 × 2 ≈ 52,000 fits. Five-seed cross-check: 50 × 5 × 200 ≈ 50,000 fits. **Total about 260,000 small LightGBM fits** on roughly 50–150 thousand rows each (v0.4: 160,000): of the order of **150–730 CPU-hours** at an assumed 2–10 s per fit (v0.4: 100–400), to be run in the background over days to weeks and parallelised over cores; a second or third attempt of the ladder adds the same again. If the pilot's measured fit time puts this beyond the local machine, the fallback, chosen before any null result is seen, is B_null = 200 with the full pipeline (not a reduced pipeline), and the user is asked (§17 U29).

---

## 11. Robustness (all pre-specified; all exploratory)

| Dimension | Variants |
|---|---|
| Costs | Slippage 0 / 5 / 10 / 20 bps per side; commission 1 / 2.5 / 5 bps; stamp duty held at 0.10% and at 0.05% throughout; gross run |
| Execution timeline | Synthetic fill at the open of t (primary); at the open of t+1; **VWAP entry of §3.2 — outside the theory: separate table, Q-total and Q-select only, no label of §3.8 and no monitoring quantity** |
| **Synthetic fill determination (T1)** | SF (primary) versus **SF-strict** (conservative) versus **SF-loose**, as defined in §3.2, applied to the frozen orders of the primary run; reported: fill rates, θ_sel, ψ_H, ψ_O, ΔAR and attribution, Theorem 1 interval at the window end, and whether any card's verdict category differs between strict and loose. Capacity cap: none / 1% / 0.1% of the 20-day average traded amount. Retried versus cancelled buys. **All three determinations are retrospective; the gap to real auction fills is not measured by this row** |
| **Hindsight diagnostic (not a strategy)** | Entries whose day-t **close** is at limit-up, or one-price limit days, removed after the fact. **This uses fields of the day-t bar that SF may not read (close, high, low) and is outside F_t^syn. It is not a strategy, no Theorem 1 quantity is computed on it, and it is never called "conservative execution"**; it only shows how sensitive trade statistics are to such trades |
| Funding and sizing | H + 1 pre-funded sleeves with pre-determined quantities (primary); H sleeves with same-auction funding (feasibility UNVERIFIED); notional-at-open sizing (idealised) |
| Holding period | H in {1, 5, 10, 20}, always d_label = d = H + 1 (each H defines its own entries and its own frozen labels; this is not a change of lag on a given label) |
| Market state | Regimes fixed from data through t−1: trailing 60-day index return sign × trailing 60-day index volatility above / below its expanding median; calendar sub-periods |
| Liquidity / universe | Liquidity terciles; N_U in {500, 800, 1,000}; descriptive split by vendor-reported CSI 300 / CSI 500 / neither membership, lagged 20 trading days (not validated, §2.3); the fallback universe U-IDX as a whole (exploratory, labelled); ST included; ST look-back 0 versus 60 days (U55); listing-age filter 20 / 60 / 120 days |
| Coverage / allocation | c in {0.3, 0.5, 0.7}; cash rule versus refill rule; K in {20, 30, 50} |
| Base learner | LightGBM versus ridge |
| Z_H definitions | CGO window 250 / 500 / weekly (each from its first valid date, §2.2); chip window 120 / 210; turnover definition (A10); turnover-cap rule (§5.3a) |
| Z_O definitions | Availability lag s+1 versus s+2; look-backs; each class alone; full sample versus event sub-sample; with and without R_O |
| Controls | With / without R_H-hist; permuted-Z nuisance control; fixed-nomination placement (§7.3) |
| Labels | O1 (decision tree, primary) versus O2; O1 with entries of outcome U / M shown separately (the label itself is not changed); tax on distributions: schedule U41 (primary) versus 0% and 10% flat; per-submitted-order tabulation (descriptive table only, §3.8 (ii)); deadline D in {H, H+5, H+10, H+20}, each with its own pre-fixed d_D = D + 1 (Cor. 1.3, `草稿`, conditional on review). **No close-marked label (O1-close was removed from the theory, §3.8 (iii)).** |
| Attribution conventions | Interaction under composition versus under exposure; zero-baseline-exposure convention versus w^B = 0; aggregate versus cohort-level; ledger terminal value after delisting / class-U events 0 versus last price; **two-interval attribution (primary; uses the closing valuation CV, outside the theory) versus single open-to-open periods (no closing valuation), §3.9** |
| Q-select statistic | Per ordered versus per filled yuan; net versus gross; equal versus capital weights; stratified versus unstratified; fill-matched pool; **per order with a real choice (θ_sel / ι, §8.3)**; with and without orders unresolved at the last data day |
| Z_O dating | ψ_O without the INS class (reported beside C4, §2.4 SD-O); a class that failed the signed date audit, with a longer lag (exploratory, labelled) |
| Monitoring | δ in {0.05, 0.10}; forms (a), (b), (c); run continuously from 2017 versus restarted at the hold-out start. **Neither d nor d_label is varied; labels are never recomputed with another deadline** |
| Tuning | Budget sensitivity of §6.3 item 7 (development years only) |
| Lots / capital | Fractional shares versus lot rounding by board (RC-11: 100-share multiples on the main board and ChiNext; at least 200 shares, then whole shares, on the STAR Market) with the 5-yuan minimum commission, at W_0 = 1 / 10 / 100 million yuan |
| Bootstrap | Block lengths 2(H+1), 4(H+1), 8(H+1) |

Robustness runs never change which specification is primary.

**Parallelism.** `synth_validation` and `common` are independent of the data work. After `base_strategy`, **`design_power` runs first and alone** (minutes; no fits); only when the status of C1 / C2 is recorded can `gates_round1`, `z_factorial`, and the G0 parts of `monitoring_real` and `dependence_diag` be run by separate instances writing to their own directories. `power_precheck` and `null_pipeline` follow the development runs; `holdout_final` is last and single-instance.

### 12.1 Synthetic validation protocol (rewritten as an executable protocol: P9)

The existing scripts in `theory/checks/` already test any-time violation over whole paths and outcome-dependent revelation times; they do not have adaptive selection, variable n_t, binomial uncertainty statements or valid comparators (lead's note in `plan_review_1.md`). This revision cannot change them (only this file); `synth_validation` is the paper-grade version and the lead may ask the theory task to back-port the additions.

**Environments (each has an analytically known μ_t; all satisfy A1–A3 with the stated d; labels are generated once and frozen, d_label = d, so A2(b) holds by the simulator's construction; in a simulation F_t is the simulator's own filtration, so the synthetic-fill qualifier of §3.2 does not arise).**
| ID | Construction | Known target |
|---|---|---|
| E1 | Given F_t, labels of day t independent Bernoulli(p_t) | μ_t = n_t p_t |
| E2 | Same-day common shock: ξ_t | F_t ~ Beta with mean p_t and intraclass correlation ρ ∈ {0.2, 0.6, 1}; labels conditionally i.i.d. Bernoulli(ξ_t) | μ_t = n_t p_t |
| E3 | Block environment of Proposition 2 (all labels of a d-day block equal one Bernoulli(p) draw, unknown until the block ends), p ∈ {0.3, 0.5} | μ_t = n_t p |
| E4 | Overlapping horizons: L_{ti} = 1{Σ_{s=t}^{t+H−1} ε_s + η_{ti} < θ_t}, ε_s i.i.d. N(0, σ_m²) market shocks disclosed day by day, η_{ti} i.i.d. N(0, σ_η²), θ_t F_t-measurable; σ_η = 0 gives identical same-day labels and strong cross-day dependence | μ_t = n_t Φ(θ_t / sqrt(H σ_m² + σ_η²)) |
| E5 | All-loss and all-win environments | μ_t = n_t, 0 |
| E6 | (Cor. 1.3, `草稿`; conditional on review) E4 plus a day-level "stuck" event of F_t-measurable probability π_t; stuck positions are unsold at the deadline and have L^liq = 1, others have L^liq = the E4 label and are sold before the deadline at an outcome-dependent time. **Added in v0.4 (certified labels, I-§6.4): each sold entry's record is independently "not certified" with probability 0.05 (PLACEHOLDER), giving L^lo = 0, L^hi = 1 while L^liq is unchanged. The withdrawn "missing ⇒ loss" fallback is not used for bracket labels anywhere** | μ_t^{liq} = n_t [π_t + (1 − π_t) q_t], q_t the E4 probability |

p_t, θ_t, π_t follow a two-state Markov regime or are chosen by the policy.

**Selection policies (all F_t-measurable; n_t ≤ M).** Π1 constant n_t; Π2 n_t uniform on {0,…,M}; Π3 history-adaptive (n_t = M after a favourable revealed lane outcome, 1 otherwise; regime of p_t chosen from revealed outcomes); Π4 range-adversarial (n_t = M whenever the policy's own running deviation Σ(μ − X) over revealed days is in the top decile of its past values, else 1); Π5 zero-entry stretches of geometric length after revealed loss clusters; Π6 permanent stop at the first time the running interval excludes a pre-set reference value.

**Revelation patterns (all within the lag bound).** Rv1 every label at lag d; Rv2 wins as soon as determined, losses withheld to lag d; Rv3 the reverse.

**Grid.** d ∈ {2, 6, 11, 21}; M ∈ {1, 30}; horizon T_max ∈ {250, 1,090, 2,305}; δ ∈ {0.05, 0.10}. A list of at most 60 configurations (environment × policy × revelation × d × M × T_max), written to a file committed before the first run; it must include E3 and E4 with σ_η = 0 at every d, and Π4, Π5, Π6 at the primary (d, M).

**Replications.** R = 10,000 per configuration for T_max ≤ 1,090 and 2,000 for T_max = 2,305 (PLACEHOLDER), seeds fixed. Monte-Carlo standard error of a failure frequency at the nominal level: 0.003 (R = 10,000, δ = 0.10), 0.007 (R = 2,000).

**What is measured, per replication.** The indicator that **at any T ≤ T_max with N_T > 0** the target lies outside the interval — separately for form (a), form (b), the upper bound alone and the lower bound alone (each against δ/2), and for the Cor. 1.3 interval in E6; the first failure time; the Cor. 1.2 bracket (must hold on every path); width paths.

**Reported.** Failure frequency with an exact (Clopper–Pearson) 95% interval per configuration; the §9.4 table; realised width against Cor. 1.1(a) and, where A5 holds, (b).

**Decision rule.** A configuration is **flagged** if the lower end of its exact 99.9% interval (PLACEHOLDER; an allowance for looking at up to 60 configurations and several interval forms) lies above the nominal level. A frequency above nominal but within Monte-Carlo error is not a failure. A flag triggers, in order: code review, an independent re-implementation of the interval, and — if it persists — escalation to the lead as a high-priority theory issue. Frequencies far below nominal are reported as conservativeness.

**Comparators.**
- *Cmp-V1 (valid at a fixed time under A1–A3):* lane-wise Azuma–Hoeffding bound with the deterministic range M at one pre-specified T*, radius Σ_j M sqrt(m_j log(2d/δ)/2) with m_j the number of lane-j days up to T*. Its validity follows from the conditional Hoeffding lemma of proofs §1.2 (part of the proof of Theorem 1; status: header) with a constant range; it is checked for coverage at T* only. It shows what time-uniformity and the predictable range clock cost or gain.
- *Cmp-V2 (time-uniform; validity to be confirmed):* lane-wise normal-mixture boundary on the same range clock (method of mixtures; Howard et al., arXiv:1810.08240). **Not a result of theorems.md; the boundary formula is to be taken from the source after literature-scout has verified it, and the theory task must confirm that it applies to our lane martingales before the comparator is labelled valid.** Until then it is labelled "candidate".
- *Cmp-N (not valid under A1–A3; shown as such):* matured-only rate with a Wald interval; day-clustered Wald interval.

**Proposition 2.** In E3 the realised width of our interval is plotted against sqrt(Md/N) and the variance identity is checked. This **illustrates the construction; a simulation cannot establish a lower bound over all procedures.**

**Lemma 3 and accounting.** Constructed price panels with the stress cases R4 of §3.9; a planted exposure-timing rule and a planted within-batch selection rule; expected: R1–R4 pass, the planted timing rule has θ_sel statistically indistinguishable from zero and the planted selection rule does not (frequencies over 1,000 panels with binomial intervals).

**Cost (ESTIMATE):** several CPU-hours; run in the background.

---

## 13. Pilot (first step after Gate 2)

**Purpose:** test the pipeline and the accounting and measure what the full plan needs; not evidence for or against any hypothesis.

**Scope.** Universe U-LIQ with N_U = 300 (under the fallback: vendor-reported CSI 300 membership lagged 20 trading days); 2012–2019 (inside the development segment; the hold-out is not touched); H = 5, K = 20; LightGBM with default hyper-parameters, no search; seeds {0, 1, 2}; methods G0, SCORE, CONF, LOSS(−Z), LOSS(+Z_H), LOSS(+Z_O), E-const-dev, VT at c = 0.5; Z_H: CGO (N = 250) and chip profit ratio; Z_O: whichever classes have passed OA1–OA3 by then.

**Success criteria (correctness and feasibility only).**
1. Audit items needed for this subset pass, or fallbacks are in place.
2. Reconciliation R1–R4 (§3.9) pass on the pilot panel.
3. Truncation tests pass (no feature, order quantity, gate decision or monitored quantity changes when future data are removed).
4. Planted-effect test on real prices: a synthetic gate with look-ahead selection gives θ_sel clearly different from zero; a synthetic rule that only scales exposure at random gives θ_sel = 0 exactly (it submits all or none) and a composition term whose size is reported.
5. Measured: synthetic fill rates by method and reason ((S1), (S2), (S3)) under SF, SF-strict and SF-loose; entries per O1 branch and counts of outcomes U and M (A24); realised invested fraction against the arithmetic of §3.7; share of days with S_t ∈ {0, K}; frequency of non-filled exits; share with O1 ≠ O2; share unsold at deadline D and share of uncertified records; Z_O event frequencies; realised Theorem 1 half-widths against §9.4; run times and fit times (input to the cost estimate of §10.2); **gate-free design quantities of §10.1 on the pilot panel (σ_w, cell sizes n_{t,s}, SE_lo, SE_hi, θ_PF^prop) as a rough preview**.
6. **Outcome blinding (added v0.5, M-R6).** The pilot runs real gates to test the pipeline. Their θ_sel, ψ, ΔAR and the standard errors of these are written to a results file but are **not printed in the pilot report and not opened** until the status of C1 / C2 has been recorded (§10.1). v0.4 listed "bootstrap standard errors (rough input to §10.1)" among the pilot's measurements; that is withdrawn, because a gate-based standard error of the test statistic is a development outcome.

**Stop / rework.** Any unfixable failure of 1–4 ⇒ stop and report. Fallback F2 / F3 needed, or estimated full-run cost beyond the local machine ⇒ ask the user. **Outcome-based results are not stop criteria** ("no detectable Z effect in the pilot", "gates do not differ from G0"). Pilot outcome numbers are not used to choose the confirmatory specification except through the feasibility items above; any change prompted by the pilot is logged.

**Disclosure:** the pilot period lies in the development segment; the paper states that pilot data were seen first.

*(v0.8.)* For the round now being run, this section is superseded by §13A where the two differ (list of names, K, period, outcome blinding).

## 13A. Exploratory pilot round (new in v0.8, U-PILOT — user decision 2026-11-15, relayed by the lead)

**What it is.** An **exploratory pilot** that runs the whole pipeline once on a convenience list of 1,000 names, **on development-period data only**, and whose results the user and the lead will look at before the user decides how to continue. **Every result of the pilot is exploratory. It is not confirmatory evidence for C1–C4 and enters no confirmatory conclusion of the paper.** It may be used to find pipeline defects, to check that every module behaves as specified, and to estimate variances and other gate-free design quantities as input to `design_power` (§10.1).

**What it may not be used for.** The estimands, comparators, null hypotheses, equivalence bounds (U3) and primary feature definitions of C1–C4 (§5, §8.4) are **not** changed on the basis of pilot results. If the user decides, after seeing the pilot, to change any of them, the change is made as a new plan version, logged, and stated in the paper as made **after the pilot results were seen**. The same holds for the choice of the confirmatory population (§2.3), which is made after the pilot.

**Hard constraint on data (the lead's).** **The hold-out period 2022-01-01 – 2026-06-30 is not read, not computed on and not looked at** in the pilot — no bar, event, Z_O row, feature, label, order or outcome dated after 2021-12-31. The loaders of the engine and of the feature library take a data cut-off date (2021-12-31) and refuse later rows; raw files of later months that are on disk are not opened (C-8). Labels and round trips need data after the decision day, so pilot decision days end H + 10 market trading days before 2021-12-31 (the end-of-window rule U50 applied with 2021-12-31 as the last data day).

**Pilot list.** `experiments/data/universe_pilot_v1.csv` / `universe_pilot_v1_codes.txt` (SHA-256 of the CSV `10abefede7b7c6facebf3f3f9adf71e757f3abd02860f037ee30855bade28fdd`, checked for this revision; 1,000 rows): the 889 names already fetched (fetched in code order, almost all SSE main board) plus 111 names taken from the random sample (B) of §2.3 with a fixed seed. Composition (as relayed): SSE main board 898, ChiNext 45, SZSE main board 38, STAR Market 19; 109 delisted names (10.9%). **A convenience sample, not a random sample; not representative of the market** (§19, KL-23, KL-24).

**Pilot universe screen U-LIQ-P (proposal of the researcher, fixed before any pilot outcome; lead to confirm).** The U-LIQ rule of §2.3 applied inside the pilot list, with **N_U^P = 300**:
- eligible on screen date s: names of the pilot list with at least 120 market trading days since listing, traded on at least 80 of the 120 market trading days ending s, ST flag 0 on s;
- ranking: median daily traded amount over the 120 market trading days ending s (no-trade day = 0), ties by code; the 300 highest-ranked eligible names are members for the following calendar month (all eligible names if fewer, counted);
- candidate set C_t: members ∩ listed ≥ 60 trading days ∩ not suspended on t−1 ∩ ST flag 0 on the 60 market trading days ending t−1 (§2.3, §3.5, unchanged); if fewer than K names are candidates, all are nominated and the day is counted.
- *Why 300.* It is the size at which the screen was already exercised in the engine's dry run (engine report §8.5 B, on 825 names of the same code-ordered pull: 615 – 735 eligible names per screen date, 300 members on every screen date; the candidate filters removed 1,051 + 96 of 64,800 member-days, about 1.8%). With 300 the ranking selects on every screen date (about 40–50% of the eligible names), while 800 would admit every eligible name and no liquidity screen would act (the dry run's observation). Sensitivity: N_U^P = 200 and 500, exploratory.
- *Feasibility (arithmetic on listing and delisting dates of the pilot list only; no price, volume or outcome read).* Names of the pilot list listed on 30 June: 840 (2010), 859 (2012), 870 (2015), 887 (2017), 913 (2020), 918 (2021); listed at least about six months: 835 – 907. On a typical day the candidate set has about 295 names. **K = 30 (U6) is kept** — 30 nominations from about 295 candidates; no separate pilot K is needed. H = 5, c = 0.5, M = K, the sleeves, the strata of §8.3 and the lags are unchanged. Since K is unchanged, the planning numbers of §9.4 are unchanged.
- *Cross-sectional operations* (screen ranking, the gate training population U28, the strata of §8.3, cross-sectional preprocessing of §4.1) are computed over the pilot list. Ranks and percentiles are therefore relative to a list dominated by SSE main-board names.

**Corporate events in the pilot.** Two sources, EV1 (Eastmoney) and EV2 (Sina dividend and rights tables), merged by the rules of §3.11 as they stand in v0.8: rule 2 needs EV1 and EV2 to agree; rule 2a (confirmation by the reference price) applies otherwise — in particular to delisted names, for which EV1 was empty in the smoke test; the pending rule for the rest; pay dates (EV3 only) are absent, so cash is paid on the ex-date (§3.11). The missing third source (EV3) is a limitation of the pilot (§19, KL-25); the fourth source EV4 (the `preclose` backstop) is computed from the bars as before.

**Other pilot settings.** Periods: the development segment of U7 — base tuning 2011–2012, gate tuning 2015–2016, development evaluation 2017–2021 — with the frozen annual deployment of §4.3. Methods: those of round 1 (§6.1) with the tuning of §6.3; any reduction of budget or seeds applies to every method and is logged. Z_O: all three classes, **not date-audited** (OA10 has not been carried out), labelled so. Conventions: the v0.8 conventions with their hash in every result file. The design quantities of §10.1 computed on the pilot are a preview, not the recorded status of C1 / C2.

**Pilot run 2 — fixed-count gate rule (added 2026-11-16, user decision; a change made AFTER the results of pilot run 1 were seen).** Pilot run 1 (`experiments/results/pilot/run/`, summary `experiments/results/pilot/PILOT_RESULTS_SUMMARY.md`) used the threshold rule of §6.1. Its diagnostics showed that the LightGBM-based gates submitted all or none of the nominations on about 30–50% of evaluation days and that only 25–40% of their submitted orders came from strata in which a within-batch choice was made, so θ_sel exercised within-batch selection weakly. Pilot run 2 (`experiments/results/pilot/run_fixedcount/`, option `--gate-rule fixed_count`) repeats run 1 with one change: LOSS, LOSS-LR, NET-K, VOL and LIQ submit, on every decision day, the ⌈c · |nominations|⌉ nominations ranked best by the gate's score (ties by code). CONF and NET-U keep the threshold rule (under a fixed count CONF coincides with SCORE, because the isotonic map is monotone in the base rank). Everything else — list, data cut-off 2021-12-31, schedule, budgets, seeds, estimands — is unchanged. **Status: exploratory, like run 1. This paragraph does not change §6.1 or C1–C4.** Whether the confirmatory gates use the fixed-count rule is a separate user decision; if taken, it is a new plan version and is disclosed in the paper as made after pilot results were seen.

**Consequences for the confirmatory design, stated.** (i) Item 6 of §13 (outcome blinding) does not apply to this round: development-period outcomes on the pilot list will have been seen before any freeze. The status rule U45 remains a mechanical function of gate-free inputs, but it can no longer be described as fixed before any development outcome was seen; the paper says so. (ii) The protection of the confirmatory tests is the untouched hold-out, together with the rule above that nothing of C1–C4 is changed on the basis of the pilot without a logged new version. (iii) If the confirmatory population overlaps the pilot list, its development-period results are not new; this is disclosed.

## 14. Compute and dependencies

### 14.1 Local machine (Windows 11, Python 3.14, CPU)
Round 1 needs no GPU. **No GPU or cloud compute is requested.** Installed in `experiments/.venv` on Python 3.14.4 by the smoke test (every install succeeded): `baostock` 0.9.4, `pyarrow` 26.0.1, `scipy` 1.18.1, `scikit-learn` 1.9.1, `matplotlib` 3.11.2, `lightgbm` 4.7.0; installed earlier: `akshare` 1.20.63, `pandas` 3.1.1, `numpy` 2.5.4, `jsonschema` 4.26.0. All nine packages import on 3.14; `lightgbm`, `scikit-learn` and `matplotlib` were only imported, no function was run. A separate Python 3.12 has not been needed so far.

### 14.2 Dependencies (approval status per `resources/DOWNLOAD_LIST.md`; nothing was downloaded or installed for this revision)

| Item | Needed for | Hard? |
|---|---|---|
| `akshare` (pip; installed) | All Z_O data; Eastmoney and Sina dividend / rights tables; Sina cross-check bars | **Hard** |
| `baostock` (pip; installed) | Bar source with `preclose`, status and ST flags, turnover (confirmed by the user, U53); dividend table; index snapshots | **Hard** |
| `pandas`, `pyarrow`, `scipy`, `scikit-learn`, `matplotlib`, `lightgbm` (pip) | Everything; base model and gates | **Hard** |
| `hf-dataset:ellendan/a-share-prices` | External reference for limit prices / turnover (A7, A10) | **Soft**; provenance UNVERIFIED; without it, cross-checks (i)–(v) of §2.1 |
| `github:microsoft/qlib`, `github:myhhub/stock` (read only) | Alpha158 definitions; chip-distribution reference | Soft |
| `github:chenditc/investment_data` release asset; `hf-dataset:joshuaxql/qlib_data` | — | **Not a dependency (Tushare-derived). Optional cross-check only if the user separately confirms (U32)** |
| `mapie`, `stable-baselines3`, selective-prediction repos | Round 2 only | Not needed for round 1 |
| `tushare`, `pyqlib`, `alphalens-reloaded`, `open_spiel`, `rlcard` | — | Not used |

No third-party code is run: round-1 methods are re-implemented; repositories are read-only references. Bulk market-data pulls through `akshare` / `baostock` follow the budget of §2.5, which the user is asked to confirm.

### 14.3 Run-time estimates (**ESTIMATES; only the data-pull rows rest on smoke-test measurements**)
| Step | Estimate | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Base data pull | Fallback universe U-IDX: about 1.5–2.5 days of unattended wall time (the audit report's extrapolation). U-LIQ: bars at about 7.1 hours per 1,000 names for every A-share listed since 2008 (count not yet read); the other tables as for U-IDX | §2.5; AUDIT_REPORT §5 (measured means, extrapolated) |
| Z_O pull (three classes, two passes 30 days apart) | under one hour per pass | §2.5 |
| Features B0, R, Z_H, Z_O | 1–4 hours | Chip recursion and binned history are the slowest |
| `base_strategy` (30 trials × 5 seeds × 2 tuning years, then 14 annual refits × 5 seeds × 3 feature sets) | 10–50 CPU-hours | 1–10 minutes per fit, unmeasured |
| `gates_round1`, `z_factorial` | 5–30 hours each | Many small fits; bootstrap |
| `null_pipeline` | 150–730 CPU-hours for one attempt (the largest item; v0.4: 100–400) | §10.2: about 260,000 fits at an assumed 2–10 s |
| `design_power` | minutes | No fits; 2,000 Monte-Carlo selections per φ |
| Signed date audits (OA10, SD-A9) | LHB and BLK by script plus 30 hand re-checks each; INS 300 rows and ST 150 events by hand; wall time unmeasured | §2.4 |
| `synth_validation` | several CPU-hours | §12.1 |
| `robustness` | 10–40 hours | Refits only for H and Z variants |
| `monitoring_real`, `dependence_diag`, `power_precheck` | minutes each | Stored ledgers |
| `pilot` | 2–6 hours after data are available | Reduced scope |

Anything expected to exceed 10 minutes runs in the background with logs in `experiments/results/<name>/logs/`. If measured costs exceed the local machine, the user is asked; any reduction of budgets applies to all methods equally.
