## 15. Threats to validity, and how outcomes will be described

### 15.1 Threats
- **Data quality.** Free scraped sources; possible survivorship bias, wrong adjustment factors, back-filled fields, upstream revisions; the two feeds may share upstreams (A17). Aggregate agreement rates can hide errors concentrated in distress events; hence A14–A16.
- **Z_O data.** Single free upstream for two classes; values checked only on small hand samples; the share-change table is back-filled and its announcement dates cannot be verified at scale; disclosure-timing rules not yet checked against exchange originals; listing criteria changed over time (UNVERIFIED); events are sparse and concentrated in extreme price–volume days.
- **Execution realism — retrospective synthetic fills.** Daily bars carry no auction timestamps: whether the opening auction matched, the auction volume and intraday suspensions are not observable, so every fill is a synthetic fill under SF and the declared filtration F_t^syn is artificial (it contains facts that on no-match days became known only later that day). All backtest results, and the monitoring results in particular, hold "under the synthetic execution model"; they are not results about an executable strategy. The distance between synthetic and real auction fills cannot be estimated from daily bars (it would need auction data, U35); the SF-strict / SF-loose sensitivity does not measure it. Whole fills, the capacity cap, the constant shortfall, the order-policy defaults of §3.7 and the clock times of U37 are placeholders; limit orders at the limit-up price with pre-determined quantities leave part of each slot uninvested.
- **Operational assumptions behind the theory's assumptions are unaudited.** A1 (with F_t^syn), A1-G, A2(b), A3 (OA-next), A4 / A5 hold for our stream only if the audit items A11, A18, A21–A24 pass; with one downloaded file, point-in-time availability can be checked only as a function of that file. The 13 rule items RV1–RV13 were checked against official texts in v0.6, with the gaps listed in §3.10; that check was made by one agent and has not been reviewed.
- **Labels partly set by convention.** Outcomes U and M and the rights-issue rule record data problems as losses (conservative conventions); their frequency is unknown until the pilot. The withholding rate on distributions is a fixed placeholder, not the holding-period-dependent tax.
- **Costs.** Commission and slippage are unverified placeholders.
- **Single history / low power.** About 1,090 hold-out days; intervals may be too wide for either a difference or equivalence, most of all for the sparse Z_O group.
- **Non-stationarity and rule changes** (stamp duty 2023, ChiNext limits 2020, registration reform 2023, disclosure-rule changes); the block bootstrap does not model them.
- **Researcher degrees of freedom.** Many design choices; one primary specification, frozen placeholders, everything else exploratory. The estimand of the confirmatory tests was changed between v0.2 and v0.3 in response to review, before any data were seen; this is disclosed.
- **Thin tuning data.** Hyper-parameters are chosen once on two validation years; the choice is noisy. It is the same for all methods.
- **Attribution depends on conventions** (interval split, interaction ordering, zero-exposure weights, reference costing, delisting value).
- **"Aggregate composition" is not selection, and θ_sel is not all of selection:** stratification controls only liquidity and volatility terciles; sector and other tilts within strata remain in θ_sel.
- **Selection by tradability.** Entries are fills; executed-trade statistics describe the gate composed with the fill mechanism.
- **Controls for Z are summaries.** A Z contrast is incremental to R as defined, not to every function of the raw history.
- **Label and theory mismatch.** Theorem 1 is applied to O1 (a valuation at the scheduled exit, not a sale); O1 = O2 only for entries whose scheduled sale is recorded as filled at the valuation price; the only bridge to O2 otherwise is Corollary 1.3, a draft on which the two external reviewers disagreed.
- **Monitoring interval is wide** (§9.4) and is not evidence for or against any gate.
- **Theory status.** Three external review rounds (GPT, semi-independent; Gemini Flash) reported no error in Theorem 1, Corollaries 1.1–1.2, Proposition 2 or the identity of Lemma 3, but the revisions made after round 3 were not re-reviewed, so none may be called a theorem in the paper yet; Corollary 1.3 is a draft; the A-share instantiation was never reviewed in its present form; Theorem 1 is, per the internal audit, essentially a corollary of known results; novelty was assessed by no reviewer.
- **Null simulation scope.** Base stage held fixed; every world is a randomisation conditional on one realised price history, not a draw of market histories; W-perm weakens temporal dependence of outcomes, W-swap breaks the persistence of choices at year boundaries, W-Zperm removes the persistence of Z and has a true value that is estimated, not known; the zero values of W-perm and W-swap rest on exchangeability arguments of this plan that nobody has reviewed; the check is run on a one-seed version of the procedure; a pass is absence of detected excess, not proof of nominal error rates; up to three attempts are allowed.
- **Equivalence may be out of reach, and the gates may be weak.** Whether C1 / C2 can be run as equivalence tests at the investor's bound is unknown until `design_power`; if not, RQ-1 is answered by estimates and intervals only. The two gates are in-house implementations tuned on little data; a small θ_sel for them says nothing about better-tuned or published abstention methods.
- **Closing valuation.** The intraday / overnight cut and all close-to-close quantities use a valuation rule defined by this plan (CV, §3.9), outside the theory files; the cards do not depend on it.
- **Date audits are samples.** OA10, SD-A2 and SD-A9 bound the share of early-dated records in a sample of a few hundred; they do not certify the tables.
- **Universe (v0.6).** The proposed universe is a monthly liquidity screen of the whole market, not an index universe; it tilts toward recently active names. Index membership is vendor-reported, irregularly dated and unvalidated, and appears in descriptive tables only.
- **One bar source (v0.6).** Reference prices, suspension status, ST status and turnover come from BaoStock alone. Its upstream is undocumented; its near-identity with the Sina prices is not independent validation; the ST flag was compared with an exchange table for 27 Shenzhen events only; the smoke test covered 67 names chosen to be informative, not representative.
- **Feature prices are a total-return index (v0.6).** Built from the exchange's reference prices: gross of tax, dividends reinvested, rights issues as if subscribed. Holder-state features are in those units, and inherit errors of the `preclose` field.
- **Rule calendar (v0.6).** Transcribed from one unreviewed report; 16 items are unverified against official text and carry conservative defaults. Days without a price limit are not known to the pipeline point-in-time; the fill rule is written so as not to need them, but whether an order at the computed limit would have been valid on such a day is unknown. Names in a delisting-arrangement period are excluded by a proxy (ST within 60 trading days) that can miss cases. Before 2020-12-31 a holding in "suspension of listing" is carried at its last traded price (branch P1), which is not a loss label. A position marked inside a rights-issue payment-period suspension is valued by convention P1-R (I-§3; v0.7, E-1) — a deliberately low valuation, not a price at which it could have been sold.
- **Taxes and small fees (v0.6).** The dividend tax uses the short-holding rate for every position; the actual brackets, first-in-first-out across lots and the tax on bonus shares are not modelled. The transfer fee before 2015-08-01 uses the highest rates found.
- **Disclosure regimes (v0.6).** The listing rule of the Dragon–Tiger list and the pre-disclosure regime for share reductions changed inside the sample, one of them inside the hold-out; regime indicators are controls, not a correction. The Dragon–Tiger rule fixes no publication time; the signed audit of LHB and BLK tests presence in the day's official disclosure, not the hour of publication.
- **Published open (v0.6).** The open can come from the continuous session or from a resumption auction; intraday suspensions are not flagged in the data.
- **Implementation risk.** Own backtester and re-implementations. Mitigation: independent ledger, reconciliation, truncation and planted-effect tests, code release.
- **Generalisation.** One market, one frequency, long-only, one family of base signals.

### 15.2 How each possible outcome will be described (wording fixed in advance; corrected: P10)
- **Q-total:** estimates of ΔAR against G0, E-const-dev, SCORE, VT with intervals; described as measured. No confirmatory verdict.
- **H-A1, H-A2 (C1, C2), if confirmatory:** "economically negligible within ±Δ_ord" only if the equivalence null is rejected after Holm. Otherwise: "inconclusive". An estimate whose unadjusted interval lies outside the bound is described in those words, as evidence against our own hypothesis, without a verdict label (§9.3, M-S12). **If estimation targets:** estimate and intervals only, with the wording of §9.3. In both cases the scale items of §8.3 (ι, θ_PF, θ_WF, θ_sel^{SCORE}) are printed beside the result, and an equivalence verdict for a gate with max(θ_PF, −θ_WF) < Δ_ord is reported as implied by the design.
- **Every verdict sentence carries the scope clause of §9.3** (gate as implemented here; universe; hold-out period; one base-signal family; synthetic execution model).
- **H-ZH, H-ZO (C3, C4):** "a difference was detected" (with its estimated sign) only if the difference null is rejected after Holm. Otherwise: **"no detectable difference; inconclusive"**. The sentence "Z adds nothing" (or equivalent) is not used on the basis of a failure to reject; a statement of negligible incremental value would require an equivalence test, which is not in the confirmatory family and would be exploratory.
- **Monitoring (descriptive; not a hypothesis):** the width limitation is known from the formulas and stated whatever the data show. On simulated streams, a flagged configuration (§12.1 rule) is escalated; a frequency within Monte-Carlo error of nominal is not. **On market data every sentence has the form "for the synthetic fill stream under SF (filtration F_t^syn), the interval for the entry-time conditional loss-probability average of the O1 label was …"**; the words "the strategy's loss probability", "executable", "at the opening-auction match" and "risk control" are not used; the counts of labels set by U / M and the outcome of audit items A18, A21–A24 are printed with the interval. If the fill-determination sensitivity (§3.2) changes a reported conclusion, that is stated in the same paragraph.
- **All market-data results (RQ-1, RQ-2, RQ-3):** described as backtest results under the synthetic execution model, with the SF-strict / SF-loose comparison beside the primary numbers. **RQ-3 results (ΔAR, the exposure-schedule, composition and cost terms, wealth, drawdown) are described as measured, with unadjusted intervals; no sentence of the form "the improvement comes mostly from …" is written on their basis**, and the tables that use the closing valuation carry the label of §3.9.
- **H-M:** on hold.
- Unfavourable or anomalous results, failed runs and every deviation from this plan go into `experiments/RESULTS.md`.

### 15.3 What the paper contributes under each outcome (added v0.5, M-R3; table of `research_question.md` v4, with one row added)

| Outcome | What can be said | What cannot be said |
|---|---|---|
| C1 / C2 (as confirmatory tests): equivalence shown | In this synthetic-fill setting and at the bound Δ_ord, the within-batch selection value of that gate, as implemented here, is economically negligible; its apparent improvement, if any, has to be looked for in the descriptive split into exposure and cost | Not "abstention is useless"; not a statement about other gates, markets, frequencies or executable fills; not "the improvement comes from exposure and cost" as a tested claim |
| C1 / C2: estimate and interval outside the bound | The estimate indicates within-batch selection value of that size; reported with its interval, **descriptively** (no test in the family has "θ_sel within the bound" as its null; §9.3) | Not that its source is a judgement of informational disadvantage; not a confirmatory verdict |
| C3 / C4: difference detected | Adding that group to the gate changed within-batch selection, in the sense of a pipeline feature intervention given the controls R (and subject to the capacity qualifier of §10.2 if it applies) | Not that the group measures anybody's private information; no game-theoretic mechanism |
| C3 / C4: not rejected (no equivalence test exists for them) | "No detectable difference; inconclusive", with the interval and the power report | Not "no incremental value" |
| Inconclusive for lack of power | Estimates and intervals; the design-stage and gate-based power reports; how much evidence one market history can carry (the effective-sample-size reading of Proposition 2 next to the measured dependence, §9.5) | Not a negative and not a positive conclusion |
| **C1 / C2 as pre-declared estimation targets** (row added by this plan: the branch of §10.1 in which the equivalence test is not run; v4's table has no such row — flagged to the lead) | The estimate of θ_sel for each gate with its intervals, its scale (ι, θ_PF, θ_WF, θ_sel^{SCORE}), and the documented reason why no equivalence test was run (the design-stage calculation) | Not "negligible", "equivalent" or "contradicted"; no verdict for H-A1 / H-A2 |

**Contributions that do not depend on any outcome (v4):** (i) a point-in-time-safe, reproducible protocol for evaluating selective trading on A-share daily data: an explicit definition of synthetic fills, benchmark-price accounting, ledger reconciliation, a randomised comparator at matched order opportunities, a frozen annual deployment algorithm, and a full-pipeline null simulation; (ii) a precise estimand for the risk of executed entries and a monitoring interval under delayed labels with a lower bound, positioned as an adaptation of existing tools, in an appendix (statuses: header; not called theorems); (iii) a complete report of the pre-specified tests of the two feature groups in the gate, including negative and inconclusive results. **Contributions that need informative tests:** any statement of the first, third rows above. Whether (i)–(iii) are new relative to prior work is a positioning question that this plan does not answer (§1).

**v0.6.** Gate 2 was passed on 2026-11-15 with the instruction to use the defaults. The list below is the v0.5 list, kept as the record of what was asked. What was answered is in §17.1 A. What is still asked of the user is §17.1 B (two items) and, if they arise, the conditional points of §17.1 C.

2. **Lead's decisions the user may veto:** O1 by the decision tree at the scheduled exit instant (U26); d_label = d = H + 1 fixed in advance (U25); benchmark-price accounting (§3.6).
2a. **Auction data (new, U35):** obtain timestamped opening-auction data (not free) so that fills are executable in the sense of I-§2.3, or — default — keep daily bars with retrospective synthetic fills and word every result accordingly?
2b. **Order-and-fill policy defaults (new, U36–U44):** information cut-off and submission time, order types and limit prices, treatment of unfilled remainders and lots, capacity cap, withholding rate, same-security lots, fallback conventions, and what to do if the `open` field fails A12. All are placeholders chosen so that the backtest is implementable.
3. **Fetch budget and rate limits (§2.5, U24)**, including whether approving `akshare` / `baostock` covers pulls of this size.
4. **Execution design (U27):** H + 1 pre-funded sleeves and pre-determined limit orders leave roughly a quarter of capital uninvested in the ungated strategy (arithmetic estimate). Accept, or supply evidence on same-auction funding so that the H-sleeve design can be primary?
5. **If the base-data audit fails** (delisted stocks, point-in-time constituents): restrict the claim (F2) or obtain licensed base data (F3)? (This is the base-price data question of `research_question.md`, separate from the Z_O decision already taken.)
0. **(added v0.5) The decision points of §17.1** — in particular: `research_question.md` v4 itself; the investor's threshold Δ_cap; the rule that decides whether C1 / C2 are tests or estimation targets (U45); the pass limit and sample size of the signed date audits (U47).
6. **Parameters of §17.2** (defaults), in particular commission, slippage, H, K, the period split including tuning years, c, tuning budget and criterion.
7. **Cost of the mandatory checks:** `null_pipeline` is now estimated at 150–730 CPU-hours for one attempt (three kinds of null world and a five-seed cross-check; up to three attempts). Accept, or reduce B_null to 200 (weaker check)?
8. **Role of the monitoring result** (appendix-level "limits of evidence"); **mechanism side-track** (on hold); **round 2** (wanted at all?).
9. **Separate Python 3.12** if `baostock` or `lightgbm` fails on 3.14.
10. **Acceptance of possible outcomes:** every confirmatory test can end "inconclusive"; C1 / C2 may not be run as tests at all (§10.1); what the paper contributes in each case is in §15.3.

1. The two-interval, wealth-normalised application of Lemma 3 (§3.9, §8.2), the cohort-level form and the level / timing split are plan-level uses of the lemma; if they are to appear in the paper as identities they should be added to theorems.md and reviewed.

Review state: v0.2 was reviewed by GPT only; v0.4 by the ARS methodology-focus panel (two seats of the lead's own model family; role-separated, not independent; proofs not reviewed). **This version (v0.5) has been reviewed by nobody**; the ARS decision letter asks for a re-review of the revised design.

If the lead wants the closing rule to have theory status, it has to be added to `instantiation_ashare.md` and reviewed; (b) the ledger's terminal-value convention for class-U events (U33) is the plan's, since I-§3 defines only the label; (c) the standard order of §8.3 is checked against the capacity cap at a notional slot size, deployed strategies at their actual order size; (d) the VWAP run and the per-submitted-order table are kept as descriptive items outside the theory — delete them if the lead prefers.

Its null and the equivalence null cannot both be true except exactly on the boundary, so the card-level p-value min(p_eq, p_out) could enter Holm without enlarging the family; on the boundary the card-level error rate would be up to 1.5 times the nominal level, and the boundary worlds of §10.2 would need a separate limit. This is the researcher's sketch, unreviewed, and a literature reference for three-way equivalence testing would have to be found and verified by literature-scout first. **State of the two items on 2026-11-15, transcribed from the decision record of `STATUS.md` (v0.7 note; not a decision of this file).** U54: the lead adopted U-LIQ ("the user may veto"); the user's later reply that dividend and rights events are fetched only for names that were ever members of the universe presupposes it; no explicit user confirmation of U54 itself is recorded. U24: the in-budget part of the full pull (unadjusted bars, indices and factors for 5,478 A-shares listed at some time between 2008-01-01 and 2026-06-30, and the three Z_O tables) was started as a resumable background job; the dividend / rights event sources are fetched for ever-members only, after the universe screen. ### 17.2 Defaults in force, and conventions changed in v0.6

**In force as adopted at Gate 2 (decision-record item (10)):** U1, U2 (commission, slippage — a value from the user's own account is still preferable to the placeholder), U4–U16, U18–U21, U25–U28, U30–U34, U37, U38, U40, U42–U44, U46, U48–U50; among them the lead's decisions that the user may veto: U25 (lags), U26 and U43 (label O1 and its fallback conventions), benchmark-price accounting (§3.6), the closing valuation CV (§3.9).

**Changed in v0.6, before any outcome has been computed, because the smoke test or the rule check contradicted the v0.5 text.** Each is logged in the Revision log; the user may veto; none may be changed after any outcome is seen.
- **U41** — tax on distributions: a rate schedule by record date and a cash timing rule replace the flat 20% "withholding" (V-6).
- **(S2) of rule SF** — "open strictly below the order's limit price" replaces "open not at the limit price"; **U36** — SF-loose no longer credits a fill above the order's limit price (V-14).
- **U39** — lot rounding by board; the 5-yuan minimum commission applies in that run (V-10, V-5).
- **U23** — availability lags per class: LHB 2, INS 2, BLK 1 or 2 (V-17).
- **Cost calendar** — stamp duty before 2008-09-19; transfer fee before 2015-08-01 (V-3, V-4).
- **New:** U55 (ST look-back of 60 trading days as the proxy for the delisting-arrangement period), U56 (event-table tolerances), U57 (hand re-check of scripted audit matches).
- **Adjustment:** no vendor factor; own factor from `preclose` for features only (A-2).

**Changed in v0.7, before any outcome has been computed, because the implementation showed the v0.6 text to be contradictory, incomplete, or not what the cited source says.** Each is a ruling of the Revision log v0.6 → v0.7; the user may veto; none may be changed after any outcome is seen.
- **Cost base** — per executed order, not the net traded amount (E-3); **order quantity** with all buy-side rates, the per-share fee and, where it is applied, the minimum commission (E-4).
- **U40** — ADV over the 20 most recent traded days; sells valued at the day's reference price; "cap-zero" as a sensitivity (E-5). **U36** — SF-strict keeps (S2); its threshold is not rounded (E-6).
- **Rights issues** — the plan's text follows convention P1-R of I-§3 (E-1).
- **Delistings** — disposition table, with the write-off fallback (E-18, U62). **Event dates** — the bar source governs the day of the price change (G-1, U61).
- **Z_H primary definitions (U63)** — CGO with the original's one-period lag; abnormal turnover as a ratio; chip distribution without initial mass, renormalised (F-1 – F-3).
- **Z_O encoding** — availability-ordered windows; 250-day look-back for "days since"; single-day rows for LHB intensities; ADV_t as the LHB denominator; BLK lag 2 in force; forward roll of INS dates (F-4 – F-9, U60).
- **Controls** — R_H-hist without the collinear cumulative-turnover bins; top-5 indicator "unknown" over unverified intervals; one rule calendar (F-13, F-15, F-20).
- **New rows:** U58 – U63.

**Changed in v0.8, before any outcome has been computed** (rulings of the Revision log v0.7 → v0.8; the user may veto; none may be changed after any outcome is seen). Event table without hand work: confirmation by the reference price (rule 2a) and the pending rule (H-2, U65). Three-day cumulative LHB threshold in the rule calendar (J-1). Block-trade premium from own bars with a plausibility rule (J-7, U64). Everything else of v0.8 confirms the code as implemented. **Population (U-PILOT, user decision):** confirmatory population pending; exploratory pilot on a convenience list with the screen U-LIQ-P (U66, lead to confirm), development data only (§13A).

### 17.4 Items referred to the user (new in v0.7)

Only items that need data or work outside what has been approved, or that the user alone can supply. None changes the research question or the confirmatory family of §8.4. Each has a default, applied if no answer is given before Freeze-1; the default is the more conservative or the cheaper alternative. **No cost below was measured.**

| # | Item | Why it is the user's | Default if no answer | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Delisting disposition table.** Hand collection, from announcements on the statutory disclosure platform, of what happened to each delisted name that was ever a universe member: termination of listing, cash exit, or share exchange (successor, ratio, dates) | Requests and hand work outside the scripted pull and its approved budget. The number of names is unknown until the universe screen has run (273 delisted names are in the pull; the ever-members among them are fewer) | Not collected: every delisting is written off (class U; ledger value 0), absorptions by share exchange included; stated in every table that uses the ledger or O1; sensitivity "last traded price" | E-18 |
| 2 | **Share-capital history**, so that turnover in CGO and in abnormal turnover can be volume over total shares, as in the two source papers | An additional data pull (of the order of one request per name; source, point-in-time property and cost UNVERIFIED — nothing was probed for this revision) | Not fetched: turnover over tradable shares from the bar source, declared as a deviation; no total-share variant is computed | F-1, F-2 |
| 3 | **Journal versions** of Grinblatt & Han (2005) and Liu, Stambaugh & Yuan (2019), both Journal of Financial Economics, placed in `literature/pdfs/` | Paywalled; only the user can obtain them through a library | The NBER working-paper definitions are frozen; the paper states which version was used | F-1 |

## 18. Freeze checklist (new in v0.6; statuses updated in v0.8)

**What is frozen, and when.** *Freeze-1 — conventions:* §3 (with the rule calendar and the event table), §2.3, §5.6.2 and the parameters of §17 are written into `experiments/src/engine/config.py` and `experiments/src/engine/rule_calendar.py` (v0.7, E-20), hashed, and from then on a change alters the estimand and is logged. Freeze-1 must precede the first gate fitted on the development evaluation years **of the confirmatory population** and the opening of any blinded pilot outcome. *Freeze-2 — hold-out:* the conditions of §4.4; it adds the model, tuning and inference layers. The lead ticks each box and records the evidence file; a box is ticked only on the evidence named, never on a smoke-test result.

**(v0.8, U-PILOT.) Both freezes come after the user has chosen the confirmatory population (§2.3, §17.4 item 4).** The exploratory pilot of §13A is run before Freeze-1 on the pilot list and development data only; it is not a freeze step, and the boxes below are not ticked on pilot results except where a box names a pipeline check that the pilot can supply (FC-A10, FC-L2), and then only for the pilot list. Boxes whose evidence depends on the population (FC-A1 snapshot, FC-A3 event table, FC-A9 OA10 draw, FC-A16 full panel, FC-D2) are re-done on the confirmatory population.

**Status column: as of v0.8 (2026-11-15).** Sources: engine report §8 (v0.7 code: 106 / 106 tests, `run_all.log`, `summary.json`, conventions hash `3e6843c7…5ee6ada`), feature report §7 (v0.7 code: 135 / 135 unit tests, look-ahead 330 / 330 synthetic and 600 / 600 real), the Revision log of `theory/instantiation_ashare.md`, and the decision record of `STATUS.md` as relayed. I re-ran nothing. A box is ticked only where the evidence is complete **and** the v0.8 code changes (C-1 – C-8) do not touch what it certifies. **Ticked: 5 of 41** (FC-U1, FC-T1, FC-T2, FC-T3, FC-T7).

*Exploratory pilot (§13A), development data only:* pilot bars complete (done as relayed) → EV1 / EV2 event pull for the 1,000 pilot names → missing-row repair (rule 12) → event table (mechanical) and URS → pilot feature panel cut at 2021-12-31 → strategy layer (base model, gates, order generation, TODO-E5, E8 – E11) → pilot run with A11 / A18 / A24 and R1 – R4 on the pilot panel → pilot report → **user decides the confirmatory population (FC-U2, FC-U3)**.

*Then, for the confirmatory population:* pull complete and first snapshot hashed (FC-A1) → universe screen and ever-member list → event pull of the population (all sources fetched for it) → missing-row repair → event table and URS (FC-A3) → disposition table or its default (FC-U4 item 1) → population feature panel with reports (FC-A16) → formal audit: panel items, OA2 – OA5, OA7, OA9, SD-A9, adversarial cases (FC-A2, A5, A8, A11) → OA10 redraw on the frozen snapshot, retrieval and judgement (FC-A9; fixes the Z_O classes and the family size) → `AUDIT.md` (FC-A14) → planning scripts and `design_power`, status of C1 / C2 recorded (FC-D1, FC-D2) → **Freeze-1** (FC-F1) → A13 re-pull (FC-A12), OA6 after 30 days (FC-A13) → `power_precheck` (FC-D3), theory review (FC-T5) → `null_pipeline` (FC-N2) → **Freeze-2** (FC-F2) → hold-out opened once.

## 19. Known limitations register (new in v0.8)

**Rule (lead's decision for v0.8).** v0.8 is the last round of plan rulings on implementation findings. **A problem found after v0.8 that does not affect the estimand, the comparator, the null hypothesis or the test statistic of C1–C4 (§8.4; including their inference procedure, hold-out window and family) is only entered in this register; the plan is not changed for it.** The code keeps its tested behaviour, or the smallest point-in-time-safe fix is made and recorded here; counts are reported where the entry says so. A problem that does affect any of these is a plan change: before Freeze-1 it is a new logged version approved by the lead (and by the user where §17 says so); after Freeze-1 it is reported as a deviation and never applied silently. Decisions of the user about the population or scope (e.g. U-PILOT) are outside this rule. Each entry: what, where it was found, what is affected, why C1–C4 are not affected as defined, handling.

| # | Limitation | Found in | Affects | Why not C1–C4 as defined | Handling / report |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KL-1 | Events left to the pending rule are not booked; windows containing them are class U | engine §8.3 #2 (trial: 26 of 241 events unbooked; full share unknown) | ledger cash, O1 / O2 labels (training target), ρ of affected round trips | Same rule for every gate and comparator; estimands unchanged | Shares by year and listed / delisted status (§3.11 "Mechanical rule" item 4); provisional-booking sensitivity |
| KL-2 | No code-change table (OA9): events filed under a former code are not found | engine §8.3 #15 | URS steps and class U for such names | Input-level | Names and counts in OA9 |
| KL-3 | Undated rights rows are excluded; P1-R cannot apply to such an issue's payment-period suspension | engine §8.3 #3 (trial: 0 rows) | O1 value of P1 marks in such suspensions | Input-level | Count of excluded rows |
| KL-4 | Main-board first-5-day no-limit date (2023-04-10) unverified but in the hashed calendar | engine §8.3 #13 | nothing (no computation reads it) | — | List item 21 |
| KL-5 | With the minimum commission a sale worth less than 5 yuan makes sleeve cash negative | engine §8.3 #14 | lot-rounding robustness run only | Primary run has no minimum | Count (C-5) |
| KL-6 | No money-market rate series sourced | engine §8.3 #8 | non-zero cash-return robustness run | Robustness only | Run dropped unless a sourced series is fixed before Freeze-1 |
| KL-7 | INS announcement dates of back-filled rows (41.2% of rows; nearly all before 2019) cannot be checked from free data; 39.5% of entry times precede the announcement date | feature §7.4 | INS features | OA10 decides whether INS enters C4 (unchanged rule) | Back-fill split reported in OA10 |
| KL-8 | INS change ratios have two decimals; 9.4% of events have intensity exactly 0 | feature §7.7 #6 | INS intensities | Feature input-level | Share reported |
| KL-9 | BLK per-stock-day table lacks 10,157 stock-days of the per-trade table (unexplained) | feature §7.4 | nothing (per-trade table governs) | — | Count in OA5 |
| KL-10 | 710 block trades with vendor |premium| > 30%; cause unknown | feature §7.7 #7 | BLK premium | Plausibility rule fixed in advance (U64) | Counts of implausible trades |

## Revision log (v0.7 → v0.8, 2026-11-15, researcher) — last round of rulings on implementation findings; pilot round

**What was done.** Edited: this file, `experiments/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES_v0.8.md` (new), `theory/instantiation_ashare.md` (sync T-1 – T-10). Read: engine report §8 (all) and §1–§7 headings, feature report §7 (all), `IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES_v0.7.md`, the changed sections of this file, `theory/instantiation_ashare.md` (all); in the code, the docstring and rule logic of `experiments/src/engine/event_table.py`, the order-sizing lines of `simulate.py`, the RC-18 rows of `rule_calendar.py`, the three-day threshold lines of `features/controls.py` and `features/config.py`; the header rows of `experiments/data/universe_sample_v1.csv`, `universe_pilot_v1.csv` and the work list, from which I counted listed names per year and the delisted names of the pilot list (listing and delisting dates only) and computed the two SHA-256 sums quoted. **No data source was contacted, nothing was fetched, run, fitted or installed, no process was started or stopped, and no price, volume, feature, label or outcome was read.** The lead's two messages during this revision were applied: the first (study population = the random sample, "U-POP") was withdrawn by the second and every passage written for it was replaced; the second is ruling U-PILOT below. **v0.8 has been reviewed by nobody.**

**Principles.** (1) – (9) as in v0.7; (10) confirm the implementer's reading where it is point-in-time safe, consistent with the theory files' accounting and independent of outcomes, so that the plan text follows the tested code; (11) a problem whose extent was counted gets a deterministic rule that needs no hand work, and the share of records concerned is reported; (12) the Z_O real-data findings become rules.

**Counts.** 24 numbered problems (H-1 – H-15, J-1 – J-9): **confirmed as implemented 21, changed 3** (H-2, J-1, J-7). Further findings ruled: H-16, J-10 – J-15 (all confirmations except J-15, a missing test). Code changes: C-1 (H-2), C-2 (conditional, H-2), C-3 (J-1), C-4 (J-7), C-5 (H-14, a count), C-6 (J-15, a test), C-7 (re-run), C-8 (U-PILOT data cut-off). To synchronise into the theory file: T-1 – T-8 of v0.7, adjusted by H-3, H-4, H-7, H-16, plus T-9 (H-11) and T-10 (H-2). No ruling touches the estimand, comparator, null hypothesis or statistic of C1–C4. New user items from the rulings: none.

**H. Engine report §8.3 (items 1–15) and one further finding.** "Code": `experiments/src/engine` unless stated.

## Revision log (v0.6 → v0.7, 2026-11-15, researcher) — rulings on the problems found during implementation

**What was done.** Only this file was edited (plus the new task list `experiments/IMPLEMENTATION_CHANGES_v0.7.md`). Nothing was run on data, fetched, fitted or installed; no process was started or stopped; no request was sent to any data source; no test was re-run. The edits were applied with a text-patching script kept outside the project, from a v0.6 copy that was first checked to be complete. Inputs read: `experiments/results/engine_tests/ENGINE_TEST_REPORT.md` (all), `experiments/results/feature_tests/FEATURE_TEST_REPORT.md` (all), `theory/instantiation_ashare.md` (all, including its Revision log), `STATUS.md` (all), `experiments/rule_verification.md` (sections B2 and C1 and the items on the Dragon–Tiger list, by search), `theory/theorems.md` (the passage on how C^E is computed, by search), the function lists of `experiments/src/engine` and `experiments/src/features`, and the sections of this file that were changed (§2.2–2.4 in part, §3, §5, §8.2, §17, §18). Sections not listed were not re-read. Every number about tests or data is taken from the two reports. **No relation between any feature and any return, no label statistic and no strategy performance was computed or looked at; the reports contain none. v0.7 has been reviewed by nobody.**

**Principles applied (given by the lead; numbered as cited in the table).** (1) No rule is set by looking at results: only correctness of the definition, point-in-time safety, implementability and conservatism count. (2) Where a variable has a definition in an original paper, that definition is primary and the plan's earlier wording becomes a pre-declared variant; the comparison was made with working-paper versions, the journal versions were not opened. (3) A construction without an academic source takes the point-in-time safe, simplest, reproducible alternative as primary and the other as a pre-declared sensitivity; both are never computed in order to choose. (4) Costs and valuations are conservative and internally consistent with the ledger's definition of traded amounts; every cost once; where two readings collide, the accounting convention of the theory files governs (benchmark-price accounting, trade_t net) and the treatment of gross order values is stated. (5) A rule that distorts the backtest mechanically is repaired with a definition that uses pre-submission information only. (6) Where data sources contradict each other, the governing source and the way of flagging and reporting are fixed in advance; no case-by-case discretion. (7) Termination of listing and absorption by share exchange are distinguished, consistently with the valuation tree of the theory file; where free data cannot support this, a conservative convention is stated. (8) Conventions set by the implementers are examined one by one: confirmed if point-in-time safe and reasonable, corrected otherwise. (9) Where the date of a rule is unknown, the item is marked unverified, the treatment does not depend on the date, and the item goes on the list to be checked.

**Counts (the 41 numbered problems).** Plan text changed: 40 (E-10 is confirmed without a change). Code change required: 26 (engine 15, features 11); no code change: 15. To be synchronised into the theory file: 7 of the 41, plus G-1 — 8 points (T-1 – T-8). Referred to the user: 3 (E-18, F-1, F-2; §17.4). The five further findings G-1 – G-5 are ruled on in the same way.

- **T-1 (E-3), I-§8 item 8.** Add: explicit charges and shortfall are charged per executed order on the order's benchmark-price value; trade_t remains the net share change; same-day buy and sell orders of one name are not netted; a reference path is costed on its net implied trades.

- **T-3 (E-13), I-§3 "Label" and S-d.** State that sell-side charges and shortfall at the mark apply to the security part q·m only and that a cash exit bears none (unverified whether such exits carry charges); the dividend-tax total is unaffected.
- **T-4 (E-14), I-§3 class S-e.** Successor events: entitlement by record date on or after the succession day; an event of the successor with ex-date on the succession day ⇒ U.
- **T-5 (E-15), I-§3 note on S-c.** Say for which entries a rights issue with a missing record date gives M (rule of §3.8 (iii) note (10)).
- **T-6 (E-16), I-§3 "Snapshot" / global override.** A missing row on the mark day, or inside a P1 carry, is a missing input ⇒ M.
- **T-7 (E-18, G-3), I-§3 Step 1.** Say where S-d / S-e records come from (the plan's disposition table), that a cash option is assumed not exercised, that "no longer listed" starts the day after the last row, and confirm that a successor not yet listed on day t+H gives U.
- **T-8 (G-1), I-§3 note on class U (plan-level reading).** Record the plan's reading of date conflicts between an event source and the bar source (outcome U for entries spanning the two dates).
Carried over, unchanged: the three out-of-date descriptive sentences in `theorems.md` and `proofs.md` named in the theory file's own log.

**Limits of this revision, stated.** (i) The rulings rest on the two reports; I did not run the code or open the raw data. (ii) Three rulings replace an implementer's choice by another definition without any test of the new definition existing yet (E-5 ADV, F-5 look-back, F-6 row rule); their tests are in the task list. (iii) The disposition table (E-18) and the date-conflict rule (G-1) are my constructions; they have not been reviewed by the theory task. (iv) Where this revision says "v0.6 code", the behaviour is the one described in the reports, not one I verified in the source. (v) Sections of this file not listed above may still contain v0.6 wording that a v0.7 passage supersedes; the precedence rule in the header applies.

## Revision log (v0.4 → v0.5, 2026-11-15, researcher) — response to the ARS methodology review and alignment with research question v4

Only this file was changed. Nothing was run on data, fitted, downloaded or installed; no web page, paper or rule text was opened. Inputs: `reviews/plan_methodology_ars_1.md` (decision letter and roadmap), the two seat cards in `reviews/ars_work/`, `00_idea/research_question.md` v4, `theory/setup.md`, `theory/instantiation_ashare.md`, `STATUS.md`. The new numbers (binomial limits and passing probabilities of the date audits and of §10.2, the multipliers k_m, the grid of §10.1, the fit counts) are formula evaluations in a scratch script outside the project (pure arithmetic, no data). The review was made by two seats of the lead's own model family; it is role-separated, not an independent cross-model review, and it did not review the proofs. **v0.5 itself has been reviewed by nobody.**

"Accepted" = done as the roadmap asked. Where I did something else, or more, the last column says so.

**A. Required items**

| # | Review item | Where | What was done | Deviation / reservation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M-R1** | R1 (EIC-W1): question promises a four-way attribution, one component is confirmed | §1 (questions, evidence table, hypotheses), §8.4, §9.3, §12, §15.2 | Accepted. §1 now quotes RQ-1–RQ-3 of v4 and its clause-to-estimand table; H-A is replaced by H-A1 / H-A2, each a within-batch-selection statement about one gate as implemented here; the v3 sentence "most of the improvement is reproduced by exposure- and cost-aware comparators" is stated to be withdrawn as a hypothesis; exposure, cost and total value are descriptive (RQ-3), and §15.2 forbids "the improvement comes mostly from …" | The seat's costlier option (a pre-specified interval statement for the exposure-schedule share) is **not taken**: v4 keeps that term descriptive, and the split depends on conventions (§8.2) and on a closing valuation outside the theory |
| **M-R2** | R2 (EIC-W2): Part A not synchronised with Part B | Header, §1, §8.4, §12 (column "Question / hypothesis (v4)"), §15.2, §16.1 | Accepted. v4 now contains both feature groups and C4; this file cites v4 as governing and uses its labels throughout (H-A1, H-A2, H-ZH, H-ZO; monitoring "not a hypothesis"; H-M on hold). Primary statistic stated once (§8.4): θ_sel | v4 is the lead's draft and awaits the user; it is the first row of §17.1 |
| **M-R3** | R3 (EIC-W3): no contribution per outcome | new §15.3; pointer in §1 | Accepted. v4's outcome-by-contribution table and its list of outcome-independent contributions are reproduced, with a sentence on which contributions need informative tests | **One row added** ("C1 / C2 as pre-declared estimation targets"), which v4 lacks and M-R6 makes necessary. **One row narrowed:** v4 lets "θ_sel beyond the bound" support "there is within-batch selection value"; no test in the family controls that statement, so §15.3 words it as descriptive (see M-S12). Both are flagged to the lead (§16.2 item 11); neither contradicts v4's rule that everything outside C1–C4 is descriptive |
| **M-R4** | R4 (R1-W1): W-swap keeps the base score | §10.2 (W-swap, truth table), §15.1 | Accepted, first option: the base score **as a gate input, including the score CONF uses**, is swapped with the other features; the real score is kept for nominations and strata only. The truth table says for every world and card whether the true value holds by construction or is estimated | **Went further than the review:** the bijection is now drawn **independently per calendar year**. With one bijection for the whole history, swapped features still identify the stock, so a gate can learn name-level persistent outcome differences and θ_sel is not 0 by construction even after the score is swapped. With per-year bijections E[Σu_t] = 0 exactly (argument in §10.2). Cost: choice persistence is broken at year boundaries. The argument is mine and unreviewed (§16.2 item 10). Side effect: W-swap now also covers CONF, so both boundary worlds make all four nulls true. The seat's stronger option (estimate the world's true values by a large independent run) is not needed for W-swap |
| **M-R5** | R5 (R1-W2): no world with an informative reference gate | §10.2 (W-Zperm-H, W-Zperm-O; pass criterion (c)), §7.3 (c), §12, U52 | Accepted: a mandatory world with the −Z panel, outcomes and nominations real and the Z columns permuted within day (Z_O: within day and class, block-wise), re-drawn per replication, in the pass criterion of C3 / C4 | **Not as simple as the review assumed, and said so:** in this world ψ is **not 0 by construction** (extra uninformative columns change the fitted gate — the capacity effect the reviewer describes under W1). The true value is therefore **estimated** (replication mean) and the pass rule uses a **centred** test with leave-one-out centring; the uncentred count is reported with a pre-fixed consequence (U52). Further limits stated: replications share one history and one reference gate, so the check is one-directional; within-day permutation removes the persistence of Z. Adds about 52,000 fits |
| **M-R6** | R6 (R1-W3): attainable equivalence power not shown; status chosen after seeing development estimates | new §10.1 (`design_power`), §10.1b, §8.4 (status paragraph and family table), §9.3, §13 item 6, §12, U45, U46, U18 | Accepted. The variance formula under a no-skill stratified selector, SE = σ_w·sqrt(κ(1−c)/(cKT)), and Δ_80 = k_m·SE are written out; an input table says which inputs exist now (c, K, H, α, multipliers), which need development data but no gate (cell sizes, within-cell dispersion, fill rate), and which cannot be had without a gate (κ, bracketed by two gate-free Monte-Carlo references) or at all (hold-out dispersion). The smallest bound with 80% power is the range [k_m·SE_lo, k_m·SE_hi], an output of `design_power`. A mechanical status rule (U45) decides, before any gate is fitted on the development evaluation years, whether C1 / C2 are confirmatory equivalence tests or pre-declared estimation targets; v0.4's after-the-fact user choice is withdrawn; the pilot's gate outcomes are blinded until the status is recorded | **No power value is stated**, because σ_w and κ are unknown; the grid in §10.1 is arithmetic, and the reviewer's 2.2 bps is quoted as the reviewer's assumption. The rule (use SE_hi; 80%) is my proposal and a §17.1 decision. The references are a model of a selector without skill, conditional on one history; a real gate can fall outside them. `design_power` uses development-period returns of nominated names, which I do not count as a "development outcome" because no gate decision enters — if the lead reads v4's "before any development result" more strictly, the calculation has to move to the tuning years. The seat's stronger option (longer window or larger K) is listed as a Gate-2 alternative, not adopted |
| **M-R7** | R7 (R1-W4): margin not anchored; assay sensitivity not shown | §8.3 (choice share ι; θ_PF, θ_WF at the gate's counts; gate-free θ_PF^prop; SCORE; reading rule), §8.4 (cards), §9.3 (bound), U3 | Accepted. Pre-specified reporting beside C1 and C2: the share of orders from cells with a real choice, the perfect-foresight and worst-choice values at the same strata, coverage and per-cell counts, and θ_sel of SCORE. The cost-anchored rule is withdrawn (costs cancel in the excess); the bound is set by the investor-anchored rule alone, Δ_ord = Δ_cap(H+1)/(A·c), with Δ_cap supplied by the user; Δ_ord is printed as a fraction of θ_PF; an equivalence verdict when the attainable range lies inside the bound is reported as "implied by the design" | The bound is **not** defined as a fraction of the attainable range (no economic meaning; data-dependent) — the review allowed either. The seat's stronger option (confirmatory statistic on informative orders only) is **not taken**: it would change the estimand registered in v4, and under the investor-anchored rule the diluted orders are part of what the bound means; θ_sel/ι is reported descriptively |
| **M-R8** | R8 (R1-W5): unsigned match rate for announcement dates; single-snapshot limits on membership and ST | §2.4 (OA10; "Signed date audits": SD-O, SD-A2, SD-A9; A2, A9, OA5, OA6 rows), §8.4 (C4, family table), §11, U47 | Accepted. Signed audit against the original document: early, leak (after the class's lag), late and unverifiable rows; criterion on the exact one-sided 95% upper limit of the leak share, p_max = 2%; stratified random sample fixed before any document is opened; n = 300 per class derived from two stated requirements; a failing class leaves the confirmatory group and the family shrinks if none remains; no re-sampling or lag-lengthening to pass. Signed versions for index membership and ST status with pre-fixed consequences. The seat's stronger option is taken in an exploratory form: ψ_O without the INS class is reported beside C4 with a pre-fixed reading | The criterion is on **leaks after the lag** (use before publication), not on raw early dating, which is reported beside it: a date one day early that the class's two-day lag already covers is not look-ahead. Which official site holds originals for all years is UNVERIFIED. The criterion is strict (a true leak rate of 0.5% fails about 44% of the time) and the hand work is large (up to about 1,200 documents); both are §17.1 items |

**D. Other changes that follow from the above**
- §17 split into §17.1 (must be confirmed), §17.2 (defaults) and §17.3 (full table); U3, U18, U29 reworded; new U45–U52.
- §12: new experiment `design_power`; it must precede every run that produces a gate outcome on the development evaluation years.
- §14.3 and §16.1: `null_pipeline` cost estimate raised from 100–400 to 150–730 CPU-hours per attempt (about 260,000 fits instead of 160,000).
- W-perm's permutation cells made explicit (day × nominated / not nominated × stratum).

**E. Review points not followed as written (in one place)**
1. M-R1: no inferential statement for the exposure-schedule share (the seat's optional stronger remedy).
2. M-R4: per-year bijection instead of the whole-history bijection the review left in place.
3. M-R5: the pass rule is centred on an estimated true value; the review treated the contrast as zero in that world.
4. M-R6: the rule keeps the bound fixed and changes the status of the test; no window or list enlargement.
5. M-R7: bound by the investor-anchored rule only; confirmatory statistic not restricted to informative orders.
6. M-R8: criterion on leaks after the lag rather than on raw early dating.
7. M-S1, M-S4, M-S5, M-S7, part of M-S6 and M-S16: deferred or partly done, for the reasons in table B.
8. M-S12: the "contradicted" verdict is made descriptive rather than turned into a test.

**F. Things I am not sure about**
- Whether a per-year bijection is the best way to close the identity channel in W-swap (a finer period would break more persistence; a coarser one leaves more of the channel).
- Whether within-day permutation or a persistence-preserving swap is the better "realistic null" for C3 / C4, and whether a centred test against a date-bootstrap standard error is a meaningful size check when replications share one history.
- Whether SE_hi (fixed random ranking) is too conservative a reference for the status rule; a real gate's persistence is unknown.
- Whether computing σ_w on the development evaluation years counts as looking at development results in the sense of v4.
- Whether 300 hand-checked originals per class can be found for the early years; if not, the classes' analysis starts move and C4 may disappear.
- CV's case K3 (missing close on a traded day) and the claim that no confirmatory quantity depends on CV were checked by reading the definitions, not by code.
- The day counts (1,090; 1,215), fit times and the 2–10 s per fit behind the cost estimate remain unmeasured.
- Sections not listed in the tables above (§2.1–2.3, §2.5, §3.1–3.4, §3.7–3.8, §4.1–4.2, §5.1–5.5, §6.1–6.2, §7.1–7.2, §9.4–9.5, §12.1, §14.1–14.2) were read for conflicts with v4 and edited only where noted; their content was not re-verified.

*Remaining points where the plan is not literally identical to the theory files, or where I am not sure (none could be resolved inside this file):*
- (a) Two-interval accounting uses close and open valuations; I-§8.8 speaks of the opening price only (§16.2 item 8a).
- (b) The overnight-return construction assumes the record date is the trading day before the ex-date; I-§5 defines entitlement by record date and marks the relation [rule: tbv] (RV9). If RV9 fails, §3.9's interval definition must be restated.
- (c) G_t in the plan is a subset of the G_t allowed by I-§2.1 (announced suspensions are not used because their timestamps are unverified). Not a conflict; it changes C_t relative to v0.3.
- (d) The ledger's value for class-U events is the plan's convention; I-§3 defines the label only.
- (e) LOSS / NET training uses labels and returns of *standard counterfactual orders* for the top-K_tr names (§4.3), most of which are never submitted. These are training targets, not executed entries, and enter no theory quantity; the instantiation does not discuss them.

- (g) I-§8.13 asks that the cost model applied to the reference's never-executed trades be fixed in advance: done in §8.2, not reviewed.
- (h) Statements about what `theorems.md` Lemma 3 defines as Π, ρ_t, W_t are quoted from its revision note and line 254–256 only; I did not re-read the whole lemma for this revision.
- (i) §2.2–2.3, §4–§7, §10 were re-read for conflicts with G_t / F_t^syn / d_label and edited only where listed above; their content was not re-verified.

**F. Things I am not sure about**
- Whether θ_sel with liquidity × volatility strata is the right "selection" estimand; sector tilts remain in it. A reviewer may prefer more strata, at the cost of thinner cells within 30 names.
- Whether tuning on two validation years is adequate; the alternative (yearly re-tuning as a declared part of the algorithm) is also defensible.
- The normal-approximation p-values with a bootstrap standard error for a ratio statistic; this is what §10.2 is meant to test, and the procedure may have to change.
- W-swap's handling of names with non-overlapping histories; W-perm's weakened temporal dependence.
- Trading-day counts (242 per year; 1,090 / 1,215 / 2,305), the fill rate of 1 in §9.4, the invested-fraction arithmetic of §3.7 and all run-time estimates are unmeasured.
- All trading and disclosure rules remain unverified against exchange originals.
- Sections kept verbatim from v0.2 (§2.2–2.3, the A1–A13 table and fallbacks, §3.4–3.6, §4.1–4.2, §5.1–5.3, Appendix) were re-read for consistency but not re-verified.

## Revision log (v0.1 → v0.2, 2026-11-15, researcher, Task B second half)

Only this file was changed. Nothing was run on data, downloaded or installed. The half-width numbers in §9.4 come from evaluating the formulas of `theory/theorems.md` in a throwaway script (assumed sample sizes, no data); they are estimates.

**A. Alignment with `theory/setup.md` / `theory/theorems.md`**
1. **Selected entry = fill, not submission** (§3.1, §3.4, §3.7, §3.8 (i)). v0.1 defined A_{ti} as "order submitted" with label L = 0 for non-fills. setup.md §3.1 defines a selected entry as an executed fill with F_t at the fill point. Changed accordingly: τ_t = opening-auction match; unfilled orders have no label and are excluded from n_t, N_T, K_T, P_T; a separate order ledger records submissions and non-fills. *Why:* setup.md governs; otherwise Theorem 1's target in the experiments would differ from the one in the theory.
2. **Selection by tradability made explicit** (§3.8 (ii), §15.1). Added: what the fill filter does to the executed set (endogenous truncation at limit-up), what μ_t therefore means, and five reports (fill rates, filled-versus-unfilled characteristics, paper outcome of unfilled orders, loose-tradability variant, submission-based alternative in a separate table). RND is matched on submitted counts and passes through the same fill rule; fill-matched version as sensitivity. *Why:* requested; without it, differences between gates' executed trades could be misread as signal differences.
3. **d from H per setup §3.3** (§3.3, §3.8 (iv)). d = H + 1 because τ_t precedes the close and O1 is determined at the close of t+H. v0.1 reached the same number by a different argument (mark at the open of t+H); that argument is removed.
4. **Label O1 redefined as in setup §3.2**: marked at the *close* of t+H (v0.1: at the open). The v0.1 mark is kept as the variant O1'. **Unresolved and flagged** (§16.2 item 1): the exit is at the open of t+H, half a day before the O1 mark. Not decided here because it requires a change to setup.md or to the exit rule.
5. **Label O2**: restated per setup §3.2 (A3 fails; no non-informative-censoring assumption; no resolved-only estimator). Added the deadline labels L^lo, L^hi and the Cor. 1.3 interval, **marked conditional on the result surviving review** wherever used (§3.8 (v), §9.2, §11, §12).
6. **Table "which label enters which quantity"** added (§3.8 (vi)); A4 accounting restated on executed entries (§3.8 (vii)); the matured-only rate labelled as not covered (Cor. 1.2).
7. **Lemma 3 conventions fixed** (§8.3): e_t = 1'h_t, w_t = h_t/e_t; reference h^E = e^G w^B, i.e. interaction booked under composition (eq. (2)), alternative ordering as sensitivity; when e_t^B = 0, w_t^B = equal weight on the base strategy's nominated lists of the last H decision days (v0.1's "set the term to zero" becomes the sensitivity). *Why:* Lemma 3 qualifications 1 and 5 require the conventions to be fixed in advance. *Caveat:* the zero-exposure convention is the plan's choice (setup.md does not fix one) — flagged to the lead.
8. **Metrics** (§9.1–9.2): P2 over executed (not submitted) trades; secondary metrics list the interval in form (b) as primary, with (a), (c), Cor. 1.2 and Cor. 1.3 (conditional).

**B. Experiment-to-claim map** (§12)
9. Table rewritten: every row now names a v3 hypothesis and the specific items of theorems.md (Theorem 1(a)/(b)/(c), Cor. 1.1–1.3, Prop. 2, Lemma 3, Remark 4) or states "empirical only". References to `design_notes.md` numbering removed. Header states that no item has been externally reviewed and that Cor. 1.3 is a draft.
10. Two experiments added: `dependence_diag` (§9.5) and `power_precheck` (§10.1). `synth_validation` extended to Theorem 1(b)/(c), Cor. 1.1–1.3 and to reproducing the §9.4 table.

**C. What the theory is actually good for**
11. **New §9.4**: assumed N, d, M under our conventions (M = K = 30, d = 6, about 1,090 hold-out days, N about 32,700 ungated / 16,350 at c = 0.5), the formula, a worked case, and a table of estimated half-widths. Primary setting: about 0.22 (form (a)) / 0.18 (form (b)) at the end of the hold-out, 0.13 / 0.12 if monitored continuously from 2016; [0,1] for about the first 130 days. Stated consequence: the interval is not used to compare methods or in any confirmatory test; its role is limited to (a) limits of evidence, (b) validity contrast with the matured-only rate, (c) bookkeeping. *Why:* `theory/checks/README.md` shows the interval is very conservative; the plan must not imply otherwise. Observation recorded: with constant n_t the half-width depends on the number of days and d, not on K.
12. **New §9.5 / experiment `dependence_diag`**: same-day intraclass correlation, cross-day autocorrelation, block-bootstrap and long-run-variance effective sample size, placed between the reference points N and N/(Md) of Prop. 2, with explicit limits (cannot certify a restricted class; does not license narrower intervals; confounded by time-varying μ_t).
13. §1 item 3, §15.1, §15.2 updated to state the width limitation as known in advance rather than as a possible finding.

**D. Items v0.1 left open**
14. **PERF** (§6.1, §8.2, §9.3, §10): enters only Q-total and the exposure-schedule attribution; removed from Q-select and from the confirmatory family. **Confirmatory family reduced from 7 to 6 tests; Holm over m = 6.** *Why:* its within-day selection contrast is zero by construction.
15. **Cohort-level decomposition** (§8.2): defined as Lemma 3 applied per entry cohort and summed; stated as our reading (exact by additivity), enforced by a unit test; not a statement of theorems.md (§16.2 item 3).
16. **Exposure level / timing sub-split** (§8.3): kept, now with an explicit definition (λ = Σe^G/Σe^B; level = (λ−1)Σ e^B r; timing = Σ(e^G − λe^B) r) and caveats (ex post, descriptive, exploratory, not in any confirmatory test).
17. **CGO and abnormal turnover** (§5.1, §5.3): relabelled "NOT CHECKED AGAINST THE ORIGINAL"; implementation is blocked until the formulas are checked against Grinblatt & Han (2005) and Liu, Stambaugh & Yuan (2019) and the check is recorded. I did not check them.
18. **Placeholders** collected in the new §17 with how each is to be set and its sensitivity range; values unchanged from v0.1 (commission 2.5 bps, slippage 5 bps, bound ±1%/year, and the others). New placeholders: δ (U15), deadline D (U16), minimum prior power (U18), exploratory alternative bounds (U3, U4).
19. **Prior power** (new §10.1, experiment `power_precheck`): block-bootstrap long-run variance of the daily difference series on development data → predicted hold-out standard error → normal-approximation power for the equivalence and difference tests, with Holm; pre-declared consequences; "inconclusive" stated as a possible, reported outcome. Arithmetic illustration only (no data): for ±1%/year, 80% equivalence power needs an annualised long-run volatility of the difference series of at most about 0.72% (0.58% at the strictest Holm step).

**E. Data sources and approvals**
20. **AKShare is now the primary source; BaoStock the cross-check** (§2.1, §2.4 A1 / F1, §14.2). v0.1 had "BaoStock + AKShare" with BaoStock supplying the bars. *Why:* user decision of 2026-11-15. *Open:* whether AKShare supplies every required field is UNVERIFIED; using BaoStock for a missing field is F1 and needs confirmation (§16.1 item 3).
21. **Tushare**: no experiment depends on it; removed from the dependency table; remaining mentions only state that it is not used. Added a provenance note that two approved cross-check datasets are partly Tushare-derived (§2.1, §16.1 item 2) — a question for the user, not a dependency.
22. **Approval status** updated throughout (header, §2.1, §14.1, §14.2, §16.1): the download list was approved on 2026-11-15; a separate Python 3.12 and the download method for `qlib_bin.tar.gz` are outside that approval.

**F. Mechanism side-track**
23. `mechanism_sidetrack` and H-M marked **ON HOLD — pending lead / user decision** (§1, §12, §13, §15.2, §16.1 item 7). Row kept; scope neither extended nor deleted.

**G. Things I am not sure about (not resolved by this revision)**
- The trading-day counts (about 242 per year; 1,090 / 1,460 / 2,550) are approximations, and the fill rate was set to 1 in §9.4; realised N and widths will differ.
- Whether a mark at the open of t+H would allow d = H is not covered by setup.md; I claim only d = H + 1.
- The cohort-level decomposition and the level / timing split are my own algebra, checked by hand only, not reviewed.
- That PERF's cohort-level composition term is exactly zero under the cash rule assumes equal slots within a cohort and no interaction with blocked exits; to be confirmed by the unit test.
- Same-auction use of sale proceeds (§16.2 item 5) and all trading-rule details remain UNVERIFIED against exchange originals.
- The power formulas are normal approximations; the block-bootstrap long-run variance on about six development years is itself imprecise.
- Sections of v0.1 not mentioned above (§4, §5.2, §5.4–5.5, §6.2–6.3, §7, most of §11, §14.3) were left as written and were not re-verified.
